Do not use for: temporarily switching it off (use set-expression-enabled). Inputs: layer, property path. Returns: the property state showing hasExpression false. Notes: undoable in one step. If you want the motion kept as keys, call bake-expression-to-keyframes instead. Example: property "Transform/Rotation".

Pack: expressions. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `removeExpression`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `property` | string \| string \| integer[] | yes | Property path: "Transform/Position", "Effects/Gaussian Blur/Blurriness", "Text/Source Text", display or match names, or an array of names and 1-based indices. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "remove-expression",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "property": "Transform/Opacity"
  }
}
```

### set-expression

Sets an expression on any property and immediately reads back After Effects' expression error, so a typo is reported in the same call rather than silently disabling the expression. Use when: adding loops, wiggle, inertia, links to sliders, or any procedural motion. Do not use for: the built-in library (use apply-expression-preset, which writes tested code) or removing (remove-expression). Inputs: layer; property path; expression source; enabled (default true). Returns: the property state with expressionState {hasExpression, expression, enabled, error} and a warning field when After Effects reports an error. Notes: the expression language depends on the project engine (JavaScript or legacy ExtendScript); get-ae-version reports it. Undoable in one step. Example: property "Transform/Position", expression "wiggle(2, 15)".

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setExpression`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `property` | string \| string \| integer[] | yes | Property path: "Transform/Position", "Effects/Gaussian Blur/Blurriness", "Text/Source Text", display or match names, or an array of names and 1-based indices. |
| `expression` | string | yes | Expression source code. |
| `enabled` | boolean | no | Enable the expression. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-expression",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "property": "Transform/Opacity",
    "expression": "wiggle(2, 10)"
  }
}
```

### set-expression-enabled

Enables or disables an existing expression without deleting it (the same as the enable switch next to the stopwatch). Use when: comparing a property with and without its expression, or pausing wiggle while adjusting keys. Do not use for: properties with no expression (it errors so you notice). Inputs: layer, property path, enabled true or false. Returns: the property state with expressionState.enabled. Notes: undoable in one step. Example: property "Transform/Position", enabled false.

Pack: expressions. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setExpressionEnabled`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `property` | string \| string \| integer[] | yes | Property path: "Transform/Position", "Effects/Gaussian Blur/Blurriness", "Text/Source Text", display or match names, or an array of names and 1-based indices. |
| `enabled` | boolean | yes |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-expression-enabled",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "property": "Transform/Opacity",
    "enabled": true
  }
}
```

## Text

### add-text-animator

Adds a text animator from a preset and keyframes its range selector so characters, words or lines animate on one after another: fades, slide up, scale in, blur in, rotate in, typewriter, random fade, tracking in, or a continuous wave. Use when: animating a title, caption or list on screen. Do not use for: moving the whole layer (set-keyframes-bulk) or changing the words (set-text). Inputs: layer; preset (see list-text-animator-presets); duration in seconds (default 1) or durationFrames; delay after the layer in point (default 0) or delayFrames; easing (default ease-out); direction forward, backward or center; basedOn; amount to scale the motion; name. Returns: the animator (name, path, properties, selectors), animatorPath, selectorPath, keyframes per property, and scheme. Notes: the animator values apply inside the selector range. forward keys Start 0 to 100 so the first unit finishes first; backward keys End 100 to 0 so the last finishes first; center keys Start 50 to 0 and End 50 to 100 in subtract mode so the middle finishes first. tracking-in keys Tracking Amount directly; wave-y-position adds a wiggly selector with no keys. Untested against a real After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Title"}, preset "fade-in-by-word", duration 0.8, delay 0.25.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addTextAnimator`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `preset` | "fade-in-by-character" \| "fade-in-by-word" \| "fade-in-by-line" \| "slide-up-by-character" \| "slide-up-by-word" \| "scale-in-by-character" \| "blur-in-by-character" \| "tracking-in" \| "typewriter" \| "random-fade-in" \| "rotate-in-by-character" \| "wave-y-position" | yes |  |
| `name` | string | no | Animator name. Default: the preset name in title case. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds the reveal takes. Default 1. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `delay` | number | no | Seconds after the layer in point before the reveal starts. Default 0. |
| `delayFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over delay. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing on the selector keyframes. Default ease-out. typewriter forces linear. |
| `direction` | "forward" \| "backward" \| "center" | no | Which end of the text finishes first. Default forward. |
| `basedOn` | "characters" \| "characters-excluding-spaces" \| "words" \| "lines" | no | Unit the selector counts in. Default characters. |
| `amount` | number | no | Preset-specific magnitude override. See list-text-animator-presets. |
| `randomSeed` | integer | no | Random seed for random-fade-in and wave-y-position. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-text-animator",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "preset": "fade-in-by-character"
  }
}
```

### add-text-box

Adds a background box or pill behind a text layer that always fits the text: a shape layer rectangle whose Size, Position and Roundness are expressions on the text layer's sourceRectAtTime, placed directly below the text and parented to it. Use when: making a button, tag, chip or caption plate whose words change, including per-instance text in a component. Do not use for: a fixed rectangle (create-shape-layer) or a paragraph box that wraps text (set-text-box). Inputs: layer (text); padding (default [24, 12]); cornerRadius in pixels (default 12) or "pill"; fillColor (default #111827 under light text, #FFFFFF under dark text); fillOpacity; stroke {color, width, opacity}; shadow true or drop-shadow settings; heightFrom text (glyph bounds) or font (cap height, equal height for any words; point text); minWidth; link parent or expression; linkOpacity (default true); name. Returns: the box layer, its group path, the expressions and any expression errors, the shadow result, and notes. Notes: the box follows the text's position, rotation and scale through parenting; linkOpacity copies the text's opacity. A shadow may use the Layer Styles menu command (viewer). Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "CTA"}, padding [32, 16], cornerRadius "pill", fillColor "#2F6BFF", heightFrom "font", shadow true.

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addTextBox`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `padding` | number \| [number, number] | no | Pixels around the text: one number, or [horizontal, vertical]. Default [24, 12]. |
| `cornerRadius` | number \| "pill" | no | Corner radius in pixels, or "pill" for half the box height. Default 12. |
| `fillColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Box colour. Default: #111827 under light text, #FFFFFF under dark text. |
| `fillOpacity` | number | no | Percent. Default 100. |
| `stroke` | {color: string \| number[] \| object, width?: number, opacity?: number} | no | Optional outline on the box. |
| `shadow` | boolean \| {color?: string \| number[] \| object, opacity?: number, blendMode?: "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "linear-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity", angle?: number, useGlobalLight?: boolean, distance?: number, spread?: number, size?: number, noise?: number, layerKnocksOut?: boolean, technique?: "softer" \| "precise" \| "smooth" \| "chisel-hard" \| "chisel-soft", range?: number, jitter?: number, source?: "center" \| "edge", bevelStyle?: "outer-bevel" \| "inner-bevel" \| "emboss" \| "pillow-emboss" \| "stroke-emboss", depth?: number, direction?: "up" \| "down", soften?: number, altitude?: number, highlightMode?: "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "linear-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity", highlightColor?: string \| number[] \| object, highlightOpacity?: number, shadowMode?: "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "linear-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity", shadowColor?: string \| number[] \| object, shadowOpacity?: number, invert?: boolean, colors?: string \| number[] \| object \| object[], gradientStyle?: "linear" \| "radial" \| "angle" \| "reflected" \| "diamond", reverse?: boolean, alignWithLayer?: boolean, scale?: number, offset?: [number, number], smoothness?: number, position?: "outside" \| "inside" \| "center"} | no | true adds a soft drop-shadow layer style (black 14 %, angle 90, distance 4, size 16); an object gives drop-shadow settings. |
| `heightFrom` | "text" \| "font" | no | text: the glyph bounds. font: cap height (fontSize x capHeight) plus one leading per extra line, so labels with or without descenders get the same box. Default text. |
| `capHeight` | number | no | Cap height as a fraction of the font size for heightFrom font. Default 0.7. |
| `minWidth` | number | no | Smallest box width in pixels, padding included. Default 0. |
| `link` | "parent" \| "expression" | no | parent (default): the box is parented to the text and follows position, rotation and scale. expression: no parenting; Position follows the text through toComp, rotation and scale do not. |
| `linkOpacity` | boolean | no | Drive the box opacity from the text layer's opacity. Default true. |
| `name` | string | no | Box layer name. Default "<text layer> Box". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-text-box",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### add-text-expression-selector

Adds an expression selector to a text animator and sets the expression on its Amount property. The expression runs once per character (or word or line) with textIndex and textTotal available, and returns the selection amount for that unit, so any formula can drive which parts of the text the animator affects. Use when: a reveal needs custom logic, for example every other character, or a selection driven by a slider. Do not use for: simple ranges (add-text-range-selector) or random motion (add-text-wiggly-selector). Inputs: layer; animator by name or index; expression, the JavaScript expression body; basedOn; name. Returns: the animator summary, the selector with its path, and the expression state with any error After Effects reports. Notes: the Amount value is a percentage per axis; return a number or an array. A typical body is "selectorValue * textIndex / textTotal". Untested against a real After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: animator 1, expression "(textIndex % 2 == 0) ? 100 : 0".

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addTextExpressionSelector`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `animator` | string \| integer | no | Which text animator: its name or 1-based index. Omit it when the layer has exactly one animator. |
| `name` | string | no | Name for the new selector. |
| `expression` | string | yes | Expression body for the selector Amount. |
| `basedOn` | "characters" \| "characters-excluding-spaces" \| "words" \| "lines" | no | Unit the selector counts in. Default characters. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-text-expression-selector",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "expression": "wiggle(2, 10)"
  }
}
```

### add-text-range-selector

Adds a range selector to an existing text animator and sets its start, end, offset, units, based on, mode, amount, shape, smoothness, ease and randomize order in one call. A range selector chooses which characters, words or lines an animator affects. Use when: an animator needs a second selector (for example to restrict a wiggle to one word) or was created without one. Do not use for: changing an existing selector (set-range-selector) or a wiggle or expression driven selection (add-text-wiggly-selector, add-text-expression-selector). Inputs: layer; animator by name or index; start, end, offset (percent, or character counts when units is "index"); basedOn; mode; amount; shape; smoothness; easeHigh; easeLow; randomizeOrder; randomSeed; name. Returns: the animator summary, the selector with its path and current values, and the fields changed. Notes: a new selector defaults to start 0, end 100, offset 0, add mode. Untested against a real After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: animator "Animator 1", start 0, end 50, basedOn "words", shape "ramp-up".

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addTextRangeSelector`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `animator` | string \| integer | no | Which text animator: its name or 1-based index. Omit it when the layer has exactly one animator. |
| `name` | string | no | Name for the new selector. |
| `start` | number | no | Range start in percent (0 to 100) or in characters when units is "index". |
| `end` | number | no | Range end in percent or index. |
| `offset` | number | no | Shifts start and end together. Percent or index. |
| `units` | "percent" \| "index" | no | percent (default) or index (character counts). |
| `basedOn` | "characters" \| "characters-excluding-spaces" \| "words" \| "lines" | no | Unit the selector counts in. Default characters. |
| `mode` | "add" \| "subtract" \| "intersect" \| "min" \| "max" \| "difference" | no | How this selector combines with the ones above it. Default add. |
| `amount` | number | no | How strongly the animator applies inside the range, percent. Default 100. |
| `shape` | "square" \| "ramp-up" \| "ramp-down" \| "triangle" \| "round" \| "smooth" | no | Falloff of the selection across the range. square is hard-edged; triangle, round and smooth fade in and out. |
| `smoothness` | number | no | Edge smoothing for the square shape, percent. |
| `easeHigh` | number | no | Ease High, percent. |
| `easeLow` | number | no | Ease Low, percent. |
| `randomizeOrder` | boolean | no | Apply the range in a random character order. |
| `randomSeed` | integer | no | Seed for randomizeOrder. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-text-range-selector",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### add-text-wiggly-selector

Adds a wiggly selector to a text animator so the animator's values vary randomly over time per character, word or line, with no keyframes needed. Combined with a Position or Rotation animator property this gives jitter, waves and handwriting-like motion. Use when: you want continuous random motion on text. Do not use for: a one-time reveal (add-text-animator with a preset) or expression-driven selection (add-text-expression-selector). Inputs: layer; animator by name or index; maxAmount and minAmount in percent (defaults 100 and -100); basedOn; wigglesPerSecond (default 2); correlation in percent (default 50, how alike neighbours move); temporalPhase and spatialPhase in degrees; lockDimensions; randomSeed; mode. Returns: the animator summary and the new selector with its path and values. Notes: the wiggly selector intersects with any range selector above it by default. Property lookups fall back to display names, so untested version differences degrade gracefully into notes. Undoable in one step. Example: animator "Wave", wigglesPerSecond 1.5, correlation 80.

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addTextWigglySelector`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `animator` | string \| integer | no | Which text animator: its name or 1-based index. Omit it when the layer has exactly one animator. |
| `name` | string | no | Name for the new selector. |
| `maxAmount` | number | no | Percent. Default 100. |
| `minAmount` | number | no | Percent. Default -100. |
| `basedOn` | "characters" \| "characters-excluding-spaces" \| "words" \| "lines" | no | Unit the selector counts in. Default characters. |
| `mode` | "add" \| "subtract" \| "intersect" \| "min" \| "max" \| "difference" | no | How this selector combines with the ones above it. Default add. |
| `wigglesPerSecond` | number | no | Default 2. |
| `correlation` | number | no | Percent. Default 50. |
| `temporalPhase` | number | no | Degrees. |
| `spatialPhase` | number | no | Degrees. |
| `lockDimensions` | boolean | no |  |
| `randomSeed` | integer | no | Seed for the wiggle randomness. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-text-wiggly-selector",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### convert-text-to-shapes

Runs the Create Shapes from Text menu command on a text layer, producing a new shape layer named "<name> Outlines" above it with one group per character, and turns the original text layer off. Use when: you need editable vector outlines of text for trim paths, per-glyph fills, morphing, or masks from glyphs (create-mask-from-shape-layer). Do not use for: animating text as text (add-text-animator), because the outlines no longer respond to font or text changes. Inputs: layer; keepSource true to leave the original text layer enabled. Returns: the source layer reference with its enabled state, the new shape layer summary, and the number of groups it contains. Notes: this uses a menu command, so the composition is opened in the viewer and every other layer is deselected first. If the menu id is not found it fails with unsupported. Fonts with no outline data produce empty groups. Untested against a real After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "LOGO"}.

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `convertTextToShapes`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `keepSource` | boolean | no | Leave the text layer switched on. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "convert-text-to-shapes",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### create-text-layer

Creates a point text layer (or a paragraph box when boxSize is given) with the text, font, size, colour, stroke, tracking, leading and justification set in one call, centred in the composition unless a position is given. Use when: adding any title, label or caption. Do not use for: changing text on an existing layer (set-text) or restyling it (set-text-style). Inputs: text (use "\n" for new lines); the style fields (font as a PostScript name, fontSize, fillColor, strokeColor, strokeWidth, tracking, leading, justification, allCaps and so on); style "@style.h1" to pull a named style from the style file; boxSize [w, h] for paragraph text; the shared placement fields (name, position, timing, parent, above, below). Returns: composition and the layer summary with textDocument showing the font actually applied. Notes: if a font is not installed, After Effects keeps the default and the result shows it; check list-fonts. Defaults: white, 72 px, centred. Undoable in one step. Example: text "HELLO", font "Helvetica-Bold", fontSize 120, fillColor "#ffffff", position [960, 540].

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createTextLayer`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `text` | string | yes | The text. "\n" starts a new line. |
| `boxSize` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] to create paragraph (box) text. |
| `style` | string \| {font?: string, fontSize?: number, fillColor?: string \| number[] \| object, strokeColor?: string \| number[] \| object, strokeWidth?: number, strokeOverFill?: boolean, tracking?: number, leading?: number, autoLeading?: boolean, justification?: "left" \| "center" \| "right" \| "justify-left" \| "justify-right" \| "justify-center" \| "justify-full", baselineShift?: number, allCaps?: boolean, smallCaps?: boolean, fauxBold?: boolean, fauxItalic?: boolean, verticalScale?: number, horizontalScale?: number, tsume?: number} | no | A named text style from the style file ("@style.h1") or an inline style object. Explicit fields override it. |
| `font` | string | no | PostScript font name, for example "Helvetica-Bold". Use list-fonts to find it. |
| `fontSize` | number | no | Pixels. |
| `fillColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour: "#RRGGBB", "#RRGGBBAA", a basic name, [r,g,b(,a)] in 0-1 (or 0-255 if any value is above 1), or {r,g,b,a}. |
| `strokeColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour: "#RRGGBB", "#RRGGBBAA", a basic name, [r,g,b(,a)] in 0-1 (or 0-255 if any value is above 1), or {r,g,b,a}. |
| `strokeWidth` | number | no | Pixels. 0 removes the stroke. |
| `strokeOverFill` | boolean | no |  |
| `tracking` | number | no | Tracking in thousandths of an em. |
| `leading` | number | no | Line spacing in pixels. Turns auto leading off. |
| `autoLeading` | boolean | no |  |
| `justification` | "left" \| "center" \| "right" \| "justify-left" \| "justify-right" \| "justify-center" \| "justify-full" | no |  |
| `baselineShift` | number | no | Baseline shift in pixels. |
| `allCaps` | boolean | no |  |
| `smallCaps` | boolean | no |  |
| `fauxBold` | boolean | no |  |
| `fauxItalic` | boolean | no |  |
| `verticalScale` | number | no | Percent. |
| `horizontalScale` | number | no | Percent. |
| `tsume` | number | no | Tsume (Japanese proportional spacing), 0 to 100. |
| `name` | string | no | Layer name. |
| `position` | [number, number] \| [number, number, number] | no | Position in comp pixels. Default: comp centre. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Layer start time in seconds. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Layer start frame; wins over startTime. |
| `inPoint` | number | no | In point in seconds. |
| `outPoint` | number | no | Out point in seconds. |
| `duration` | number | no | Duration in seconds from the in point. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Duration in frames; wins over duration. |
| `label` | "none" \| "red" \| "yellow" \| "aqua" \| "pink" \| "lavender" \| "peach" \| "sea-foam" \| "blue" \| "green" \| "purple" \| "orange" \| "brown" \| "fuchsia" \| "cyan" \| "sandstone" \| "dark-green" \| integer | no | Label colour name or index 0-16. |
| `parent` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Parent layer. |
| `blendMode` | "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "dancing-dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "classic-color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "add" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "classic-color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "linear-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "classic-difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity" \| "stencil-alpha" \| "stencil-luma" \| "silhouette-alpha" \| "silhouette-luma" \| "alpha-add" \| "luminescent-premul" | no | Blend mode. |
| `threeD` | boolean | no | Make it a 3D layer. |
| `above` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Place the new layer directly above this layer. |
| `below` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Place the new layer directly below this layer. |
| `toBottom` | boolean | no | Place the new layer at the bottom of the stack. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-text-layer",
  "arguments": {
    "text": "Hello"
  }
}
```

### fit-text-to-box

Sets a text layer's font size so the text fits a width and height: for paragraph (box) text the largest size at which every line still composes inside the box, for point text the largest size whose bounds fit. Use when: a caption, label or name must fit a fixed area. Do not use for: resizing the box (set-text-box) or a plate behind text (add-text-box). Inputs: layer (text); width and height in pixels (default the paragraph box; point text needs one); mode once (default) or expression; minFontSize and maxFontSize (1 to 1296); shrinkOnly (default false, true for expression); scaleLeading (default true); time or frame. Returns: font size before and after, measured bounds, box size, method, iterations, and the expression in expression mode. Notes: mode once is the reliable way: After Effects lays the text out at each trial size and bisection finds the size, using boxOverflow on 24.6 or later and a tall-box comparison before. Mode expression puts a Scale expression on point text so changing words keep fitting; it scales the layer, not the font. Keyed Source Text gets a key at the time. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Caption"}, width 900, height 240.

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `fitTextToBox`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `width` | number | no | Pixels. Default: the paragraph box width. |
| `height` | number | no | Pixels. Default: the paragraph box height. |
| `mode` | "once" \| "expression" | no | once (default): compute and set the font size now. expression: a Scale expression on point text that fits it at every frame. |
| `minFontSize` | number | no | Default 1. |
| `maxFontSize` | number | no | Default 1296. |
| `shrinkOnly` | boolean | no | Never make the text bigger than it is. Default false for once, true for expression. |
| `scaleLeading` | boolean | no | Scale a fixed leading with the font size. Default true. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "fit-text-to-box",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### get-text

Reads the text and full style (font, size, colours, tracking, leading, justification, box settings) of a text layer, optionally at a time. Use when: copying a style from a reference, or confirming the font After Effects actually used. Do not use for: non-text layers (it errors). Inputs: layer; time or frame (optional). Returns: text, textDocument, the number of Source Text keys and whether an expression is present. Notes: read-only. Example: layer {name: "Title"}.

Pack: text. Read-only. Bridge command: `getText`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "get-text",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### list-fonts

Lists the fonts installed in After Effects with the PostScript name that set-text-style and create-text-layer expect, plus family and style names, filtered by a search word. Use when: you need the exact PostScript name of a font, or want to know whether a font is installed before styling a layer. Do not use for: reading the font already on a layer (get-text). Inputs: query, a case-insensitive substring matched against the PostScript, family and style names; maxResults (default 200). Returns: supported (true when the font list is available), totalInstalled, count, truncated, and fonts[] of {postScriptName, family, style, fontType, technology, isSubstitute}. Notes: read-only. The font list needs After Effects 24.0 or later (app.fonts). On older versions supported is false with a message; any installed PostScript name can still be passed to set-text-style, and the result of that call shows whether it was applied. Example: query "helvetica".

Pack: text. Read-only. Bridge command: `listFonts`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `query` | string | no | Case-insensitive substring of the PostScript, family or style name. |
| `maxResults` | integer | no | Default 200. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-fonts",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### list-text-animator-presets

Lists the built-in text animator presets for add-text-animator with a description of each motion and every parameter's name, default value and meaning. Use when: choosing how a title should animate on, or checking what amount means for a given preset. Do not use for: effect looks (list-effect-templates) or .ffx files on disk (list-presets). Inputs: none. Returns: count, presets[] of {name, description, params[] of {name, defaultValue, description}}, and scheme, a short explanation of how the direction option drives the range selector. Notes: read-only. Preset names are stable and match the preset enum of add-text-animator. Example: call it once, then add-text-animator with preset "slide-up-by-word" and duration 0.8.

Pack: text. Read-only. Bridge command: `listTextAnimatorPresets`.

Inputs: none.

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-text-animator-presets",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### set-per-character-3d

Turns on per-character 3D for a text layer (each character gets its own Z, X and Y rotation) and optionally adds a 3D reveal: an animator holding the starting pose with a Ramp Up range selector whose Offset is keyed twice, so characters flip, swing, tumble or push in one after another and land softly. Use when: a title should flip up letter by letter, swing in like doors or tumble into place. Do not use for: flat reveals (add-text-animator) or making the whole layer 3D (set-layer-3d). Inputs: layer (text); enabled (default true; false turns per-character 3D off); reveal {preset flip-x, flip-y, tumble or push-z; rotationX, rotationY, rotationZ, positionZ, opacity as the starting pose; pivot center or baseline; basedOn; direction; startTime or startFrame; duration (default 1 s) or durationFrames; easing; name}. Returns: the layer flags (threeD, threeDPerChar), the animator with its properties and selector, the keys written, the pose used, and notes. Notes: the layer becomes 3D. Per-character 3D renders in Classic 3D; Cinema 4D turns motion blur off. pivot center changes Grouping Alignment for the whole layer. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Title"}, reveal {preset: "flip-x", durationFrames: 36}.

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setPerCharacter3d`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `enabled` | boolean | no | Per-character 3D on (default) or off. |
| `reveal` | {preset?: "flip-x" \| "flip-y" \| "tumble" \| "push-z", rotationX?: number, rotationY?: number, rotationZ?: number, positionZ?: number, opacity?: number, pivot?: "center" \| "baseline", basedOn?: "characters" \| "characters-excluding-spaces" \| "words" \| "lines", direction?: "forward" \| "backward", startTime?: number, startFrame?: integer, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object, name?: string} | no | A text animator holding the starting pose, with a Ramp Up range selector whose Offset is keyed -100 to 100 so characters settle one after another. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-per-character-3d",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-range-selector

Changes an existing range selector on a text animator: start, end, offset, units, based on, mode, amount, shape, smoothness, ease high and low, randomize order. With a time or frame the start, end and offset values are written as keyframes with easing instead of static values. Use when: retiming or reshaping a reveal that add-text-animator built, or hand-keying a selector. Do not use for: adding a selector (add-text-range-selector) or wiggly and expression selectors. Inputs: layer; animator by name or index; selector index within the animator (default 1); the range fields; time or frame plus easing to keyframe start, end and offset at that time. Returns: the selector with its path and values, the fields changed, and keyframes written. Notes: keys go on whichever of start, end and offset are given. Units switches which properties exist (percent or index), so set units first when changing it. Untested against a real After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: animator 1, selector 1, offset 100, frame 24, easing "ease-out".

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setRangeSelector`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `animator` | string \| integer | no | Which text animator: its name or 1-based index. Omit it when the layer has exactly one animator. |
| `selector` | integer | no | 1-based selector index inside the animator. Default 1. |
| `start` | number | no | Range start in percent (0 to 100) or in characters when units is "index". |
| `end` | number | no | Range end in percent or index. |
| `offset` | number | no | Shifts start and end together. Percent or index. |
| `units` | "percent" \| "index" | no | percent (default) or index (character counts). |
| `basedOn` | "characters" \| "characters-excluding-spaces" \| "words" \| "lines" | no | Unit the selector counts in. Default characters. |
| `mode` | "add" \| "subtract" \| "intersect" \| "min" \| "max" \| "difference" | no | How this selector combines with the ones above it. Default add. |
| `amount` | number | no | How strongly the animator applies inside the range, percent. Default 100. |
| `shape` | "square" \| "ramp-up" \| "ramp-down" \| "triangle" \| "round" \| "smooth" | no | Falloff of the selection across the range. square is hard-edged; triangle, round and smooth fade in and out. |
| `smoothness` | number | no | Edge smoothing for the square shape, percent. |
| `easeHigh` | number | no | Ease High, percent. |
| `easeLow` | number | no | Ease Low, percent. |
| `randomizeOrder` | boolean | no | Apply the range in a random character order. |
| `randomSeed` | integer | no | Seed for randomizeOrder. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-range-selector",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-text

Replaces the text of a text layer, keeping its styling. Optionally keys the Source Text at a time, which is how text changes over time in After Effects (hold keyframes). Use when: correcting a title, or building a counter or word-change sequence. Do not use for: per-character animation (add-text-animator) or styling (set-text-style). Inputs: layer; text; time or frame to set a Source Text keyframe at that time instead of the static value; renameLayer to rename the layer to the text. Returns: composition, layer, the resulting textDocument and the number of Source Text keys. Notes: Source Text keys are always hold keys. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Counter"}, text "42", frame 30.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setText`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `text` | string | yes | The new text. A backslash-n sequence starts a new line. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `renameLayer` | boolean | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-text",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "text": "Hello"
  }
}
```

### set-text-box

Converts a point text layer to paragraph (box) text with a given box size, resizes an existing box, or converts box text back to point text. Box text wraps lines inside the box; point text grows freely. Use when: a caption should wrap inside a fixed width, or a box needs a new size. Do not use for: creating a new box text layer (create-text-layer with boxSize) or changing the words (set-text). Inputs: layer; boxSize [width, height] in pixels to set box text and its size; pointText true to convert back to point text. Returns: the resulting textDocument (boxText, boxTextSize) and which fields changed. Notes: converting between point and box text through scripting needs a recent After Effects (the boxText flag became writable in 2023 era releases). If the conversion does not take, the call fails with unsupported and suggests create-text-layer with boxSize. Resizing an existing box works in older versions. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Body"}, boxSize [900, 300].

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setTextBox`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `boxSize` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] in pixels. Sets box text. |
| `pointText` | boolean | no | true converts back to point text. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-text-box",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-text-on-path

Puts a text layer on a mask path (Text > Path Options) and sets Perpendicular To Path, Force Alignment, Reverse Path and the First and Last Margins; optionally keys First Margin twice so the text travels along the path. Use when: a title should follow a curve, a circle or a drawn line. Do not use for: moving a whole layer along a path (move-along-path) or drawing a mask from scratch (create-mask). Inputs: layer (text); mask by name or index, or fromShapeLayer {layer, group, hide} to copy a shape layer path onto the text layer as a new mask; maskName; perpendicular; forceAlignment; reversePath; firstMargin and lastMargin in pixels; justification; travel {from, to, startTime or startFrame, duration or durationFrames, easing}; keepMaskMode. Returns: the mask, path length and text width in pixels, the options read back, and the travel keys. Notes: with no mask given, the path already set or the only mask is used. A closed mask is set to mode None so it does not cut the text. Travel sets left justification unless given, because centred text is centred on the path middle. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Title"}, mask "Mask 1", travel {durationFrames: 60}.

Pack: text. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setTextOnPath`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `mask` | string \| integer | no | Which mask: name or 1-based index. |
| `fromShapeLayer` | {layer: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, group?: string \| integer, hide?: boolean} | no | Create the path mask from a shape layer group instead of using an existing mask. |
| `maskName` | string | no | Name for a mask created from fromShapeLayer. |
| `perpendicular` | boolean | no | Perpendicular To Path. After Effects default on. |
| `forceAlignment` | boolean | no | Spread the text over the whole path between the margins. |
| `reversePath` | boolean | no | Run the text the other way (on a circle: outside versus inside). |
| `firstMargin` | number | no | Pixels from the path start. |
| `lastMargin` | number | no | Pixels from the path end. |
| `justification` | "left" \| "center" \| "right" | no | Paragraph justification. Default: unchanged, or left when travel is given. |
| `travel` | {from?: number, to?: number, startTime?: number, startFrame?: integer, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object} | no | Keys First Margin twice so the text travels along the path. Duration default 2 s. |
| `keepMaskMode` | boolean | no | Keep the mask mode instead of setting None. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-text-on-path",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-text-style

Changes any subset of a text layer's style: font (PostScript name), size, fill, stroke colour and width, tracking, leading, justification, baseline shift, all caps, small caps, faux bold and italic, vertical and horizontal scale, tsume. Use when: restyling a layer, or applying a named style from the style file with style "@style.body". Do not use for: changing the words (set-text) or animating style (text animators). Inputs: layer plus any style fields; style "@style.<name>" merges the named style under explicit fields. Returns: the fields changed, the resulting textDocument, and warnings when a requested font could not be applied. Notes: the style applies to the whole layer. If Source Text has keyframes, the change is keyed at the current time. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Subtitle"}, fontSize 36, tracking 50, fillColor "#9aa0b4".

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setTextStyle`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `style` | string \| {font?: string, fontSize?: number, fillColor?: string \| number[] \| object, strokeColor?: string \| number[] \| object, strokeWidth?: number, strokeOverFill?: boolean, tracking?: number, leading?: number, autoLeading?: boolean, justification?: "left" \| "center" \| "right" \| "justify-left" \| "justify-right" \| "justify-center" \| "justify-full", baselineShift?: number, allCaps?: boolean, smallCaps?: boolean, fauxBold?: boolean, fauxItalic?: boolean, verticalScale?: number, horizontalScale?: number, tsume?: number} | no | A named text style from the style file ("@style.h1") or an inline style object. Explicit fields override it. |
| `font` | string | no | PostScript font name, for example "Helvetica-Bold". Use list-fonts to find it. |
| `fontSize` | number | no | Pixels. |
| `fillColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour: "#RRGGBB", "#RRGGBBAA", a basic name, [r,g,b(,a)] in 0-1 (or 0-255 if any value is above 1), or {r,g,b,a}. |
| `strokeColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour: "#RRGGBB", "#RRGGBBAA", a basic name, [r,g,b(,a)] in 0-1 (or 0-255 if any value is above 1), or {r,g,b,a}. |
| `strokeWidth` | number | no | Pixels. 0 removes the stroke. |
| `strokeOverFill` | boolean | no |  |
| `tracking` | number | no | Tracking in thousandths of an em. |
| `leading` | number | no | Line spacing in pixels. Turns auto leading off. |
| `autoLeading` | boolean | no |  |
| `justification` | "left" \| "center" \| "right" \| "justify-left" \| "justify-right" \| "justify-center" \| "justify-full" | no |  |
| `baselineShift` | number | no | Baseline shift in pixels. |
| `allCaps` | boolean | no |  |
| `smallCaps` | boolean | no |  |
| `fauxBold` | boolean | no |  |
| `fauxItalic` | boolean | no |  |
| `verticalScale` | number | no | Percent. |
| `horizontalScale` | number | no | Percent. |
| `tsume` | number | no | Tsume (Japanese proportional spacing), 0 to 100. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-text-style",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

## Shapes

### add-repeater

Adds a Repeater to a shape layer (inside a group or at the root) that draws the shape several times, each copy offset by the repeater transform: position, scale, rotation, anchor point and start/end opacity. Use when: building rows of dots, radial arrays (rotation 360 / copies), grids, or fading trails from one shape. Do not use for: copies as separate layers (duplicate-layer). Inputs: layer; group by name or index (default root); copies (default 3); offset; transform {position [x, y] (default [100, 0]), scale [x, y] percent, rotation degrees, anchorPoint [x, y], startOpacity, endOpacity}; compositeOrder "below" or "above"; name. Returns: the repeater (name, matchName, path, index), its property values and notes. Notes: the transform is applied cumulatively per copy, so rotation 30 with 12 copies makes a full circle around the anchor point. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Dot"}, copies 8, transform {position [0, 0], rotation 45, anchorPoint [0, 150]}.

Pack: shapes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addRepeater`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `group` | string \| integer | no | Which group under the shape layer's Contents: name or 1-based index. Default: the first group. |
| `copies` | number | no | Default 3. |
| `offset` | number | no | Repeater Offset (shifts which copy is the original). |
| `transform` | {position?: [number, number], scale?: [number, number], rotation?: number, anchorPoint?: [number, number], startOpacity?: number, endOpacity?: number} | no |  |
| `compositeOrder` | "below" \| "above" | no |  |
| `name` | string | no | Name for the repeater. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-repeater",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### add-shape-modifier

Adds a shape modifier to a shape layer, either inside one group or at the root of Contents so it applies to every group, and sets its properties by display name. Types: trim-paths, offset-paths, round-corners, pucker-bloat, wiggle-paths, wiggle-transform, twist, zig-zag, repeater, merge-paths. Use when: rounding corners, offsetting an outline, adding jitter, or any modifier not covered by animate-trim-paths and add-repeater. Do not use for: keyframed draw-ons (animate-trim-paths) or copies with a transform (add-repeater). Inputs: layer; type; group by name or index, or omit it (or pass root true) for the layer root; params keyed by display name, for example {"Radius": 20} for round-corners, {"Amount": 12} for offset-paths, {"Size": 10, "Detail": 5} for wiggle-paths, {"Angle": 45} for twist, {"Mode": 2} for merge-paths; name. Returns: the modifier (name, matchName, path, index), its top-level properties with values, params applied and notes for names not found. Notes: a modifier inside a group affects the paths above it in that group. Numeric enum values are used for menu properties. Undoable in one step. Example: type "round-corners", group "Group 1", params {"Radius": 24}.

Pack: shapes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addShapeModifier`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `type` | "trim-paths" \| "offset-paths" \| "round-corners" \| "pucker-bloat" \| "wiggle-paths" \| "wiggle-transform" \| "twist" \| "zig-zag" \| "repeater" \| "merge-paths" | yes |  |
| `group` | string \| integer | no | Which group under the shape layer's Contents: name or 1-based index. Default: the first group. |
| `root` | boolean | no | Add at the Contents root so the modifier applies to every group. Default when group is omitted. |
| `params` | object of any | no | Property values keyed by display name. |
| `name` | string | no | Rename the modifier. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-shape-modifier",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "type": "trim-paths"
  }
}
```

### add-shape-to-layer

Adds another group with one shape (rectangle, ellipse, polygon, star, line, arrow or custom path) plus optional fill and stroke to an existing shape layer, offset from the layer origin by the group's own Position. Use when: building an icon or diagram from several shapes on one layer, or adding a stroke-only outline next to a filled shape. Do not use for: a new layer (create-shape-layer) or changing an existing group's fill or stroke (set-shape-fill, set-shape-stroke). Inputs: layer (must be a shape layer); the same geometry and fill/stroke fields as create-shape-layer; offset [x, y] in pixels applied to the new group's transform Position; groupName. Returns: composition, layer reference, the new group (name, index, path), the path, fill and stroke property paths, the shape info and the total group count. Notes: vertices for shapeType "path" are relative to the group origin before offset. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Icon"}, shapeType "circle", size [40, 40], offset [120, 0], fillColor "#ff5a36".

Pack: shapes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addShapeToLayer`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `shapeType` | "rectangle" \| "rounded-rectangle" \| "ellipse" \| "circle" \| "polygon" \| "star" \| "line" \| "arrow" \| "path" | no | Default rectangle. |
| `size` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] in pixels. Default [200, 200]. For circle only width is used. |
| `roundness` | number | no | Corner radius for rectangles. |
| `cornerRadii` | number[] | no | Per-corner radii; After Effects rectangles are uniform, so the largest is used. |
| `points` | integer \| [number, number][] | no | Point count for polygon/star, or an array of [x,y] vertices for path. |
| `outerRadius` | number | no | Outer radius for polygon and star, pixels. |
| `innerRadius` | number | no | Inner radius for star, pixels. |
| `outerRoundness` | number | no | Outer roundness for polygon and star, percent. |
| `from` | [number, number] | no | Line start, relative to the layer position. |
| `to` | [number, number] | no | Line end. |
| `headSize` | number | no | Arrow head size. |
| `shaftWidth` | number | no | Arrow shaft width. |
| `closed` | boolean | no | Close a custom path. Default true. |
| `inTangents` | [number, number][] | no |  |
| `outTangents` | [number, number][] | no |  |
| `fillColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Default white. |
| `fillOpacity` | number | no | Fill opacity, percent. Default 100. |
| `noFill` | boolean | no | Create no fill. |
| `strokeColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour: "#RRGGBB", "#RRGGBBAA", a basic name, [r,g,b(,a)] in 0-1 (or 0-255 if any value is above 1), or {r,g,b,a}. |
| `strokeWidth` | number | no | 0 (default) means no stroke. |
| `strokeOpacity` | number | no | Stroke opacity, percent. Default 100. |
| `lineCap` | "butt" \| "round" \| "square" | no |  |
| `lineJoin` | "miter" \| "round" \| "bevel" | no |  |
| `dashes` | number[] | no | [dash, gap] in pixels. |
| `groupName` | string | no | Name for the shape group. |
| `offset` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] pixels for the group transform Position. Default [0, 0]. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-shape-to-layer",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### animate-trim-paths

Adds Trim Paths to a shape layer (if missing) and keyframes its Start, End and Offset between from and to values over a time range, producing the classic draw-on or wipe of a stroked path. Use when: drawing a line, underline, circle or outline on screen, or erasing it. Do not use for: masks (mask-reveal-animation) or text (add-text-animator). Inputs: layer; group by name or index, or the layer root by default; start, end and offset each as {from, to}; start and end are percent (0 to 100), offset is degrees (360 is one full lap round the path); startTime or startFrame (default the layer in point); duration in seconds (default 1) or durationFrames; easing (default ease-out); trimMultipleShapes "simultaneously" or "individually". Returns: the modifier path, the keyframes written per property, and the time range in seconds and frames. Notes: the typical draw-on is end {from: 0, to: 100}, which is the default when nothing is given. The path needs a stroke to be visible. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Underline"}, end {from: 0, to: 100}, durationFrames 18.

Pack: shapes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `animateTrimPaths`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `group` | string \| integer | no | Which group under the shape layer's Contents: name or 1-based index. Default: the first group. |
| `start` | {from: number, to: number} | no | {from, to} values for the start and end of the animation. |
| `end` | {from: number, to: number} | no | {from, to} values for the start and end of the animation. |
| `offset` | {from: number, to: number} | no | {from, to} values for the start and end of the animation. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds. Default: the layer in point. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default 1. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Default ease-out. |
| `trimMultipleShapes` | "simultaneously" \| "individually" | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "animate-trim-paths",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### create-shape-layer

Creates a shape layer with one group containing a rectangle, rounded rectangle, ellipse, circle, polygon, star, line, arrow or custom path, with an optional fill and stroke (colour, width, caps, joins, dashes). The path is centred on the layer anchor, and the layer is positioned at the comp centre unless told otherwise. Use when: building cards, buttons, lines, connectors, icons, backgrounds and masks-as-shapes. Do not use for: adding a second shape to an existing shape layer (add-shape-to-layer) or modifiers like trim paths (add-shape-modifier). Inputs: shapeType and its geometry (size, roundness, points, from/to, headSize); fillColor, noFill, strokeColor, strokeWidth, lineCap, lineJoin, dashes; the shared placement fields (name, position, timing, parent, above, below). Returns: the layer summary plus the property paths of the group, path, fill and stroke, ready for set-keyframes-bulk or set-shape-fill. Notes: coordinates for path vertices are relative to the layer position. Undoable in one step. Example: shapeType "rounded-rectangle", size [720, 140], roundness 28, fillColor "#111111", strokeColor "#ffffff", strokeWidth 3.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createShapeLayer`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `shapeType` | "rectangle" \| "rounded-rectangle" \| "ellipse" \| "circle" \| "polygon" \| "star" \| "line" \| "arrow" \| "path" | no | Default rectangle. |
| `size` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] in pixels. Default [200, 200]. For circle only width is used. |
| `roundness` | number | no | Corner radius for rectangles. |
| `cornerRadii` | number[] | no | Per-corner radii; After Effects rectangles are uniform, so the largest is used. |
| `points` | integer \| [number, number][] | no | Point count for polygon/star, or an array of [x,y] vertices for path. |
| `outerRadius` | number | no | Outer radius for polygon and star, pixels. |
| `innerRadius` | number | no | Inner radius for star, pixels. |
| `outerRoundness` | number | no | Outer roundness for polygon and star, percent. |
| `from` | [number, number] | no | Line start, relative to the layer position. |
| `to` | [number, number] | no | Line end. |
| `headSize` | number | no | Arrow head size. |
| `shaftWidth` | number | no | Arrow shaft width. |
| `closed` | boolean | no | Close a custom path. Default true. |
| `inTangents` | [number, number][] | no |  |
| `outTangents` | [number, number][] | no |  |
| `fillColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Default white. |
| `fillOpacity` | number | no | Fill opacity, percent. Default 100. |
| `noFill` | boolean | no | Create no fill. |
| `strokeColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour: "#RRGGBB", "#RRGGBBAA", a basic name, [r,g,b(,a)] in 0-1 (or 0-255 if any value is above 1), or {r,g,b,a}. |
| `strokeWidth` | number | no | 0 (default) means no stroke. |
| `strokeOpacity` | number | no | Stroke opacity, percent. Default 100. |
| `lineCap` | "butt" \| "round" \| "square" | no |  |
| `lineJoin` | "miter" \| "round" \| "bevel" | no |  |
| `dashes` | number[] | no | [dash, gap] in pixels. |
| `groupName` | string | no | Name for the shape group. |
| `name` | string | no | Layer name. |
| `position` | [number, number] \| [number, number, number] | no | Position in comp pixels. Default: comp centre. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Layer start time in seconds. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Layer start frame; wins over startTime. |
| `inPoint` | number | no | In point in seconds. |
| `outPoint` | number | no | Out point in seconds. |
| `duration` | number | no | Duration in seconds from the in point. |

| `label` | "none" \| "red" \| "yellow" \| "aqua" \| "pink" \| "lavender" \| "peach" \| "sea-foam" \| "blue" \| "green" \| "purple" \| "orange" \| "brown" \| "fuchsia" \| "cyan" \| "sandstone" \| "dark-green" \| integer | no | Label colour name or index 0-16. |
| `parent` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Parent layer. |
| `blendMode` | "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "dancing-dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "classic-color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "add" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "classic-color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "linear-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "classic-difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity" \| "stencil-alpha" \| "stencil-luma" \| "silhouette-alpha" \| "silhouette-luma" \| "alpha-add" \| "luminescent-premul" | no | Blend mode. |
| `threeD` | boolean | no | Make it a 3D layer. |

| `below` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Place the new layer directly below this layer. |
| `toBottom` | boolean | no | Place the new layer at the bottom of the stack. |

Example call:


### import-vector-as-shapes

Imports an Illustrator (.ai, .eps, .pdf) or .svg file, adds it to a composition and converts it to an editable shape layer with Create Shapes from Vector Layer, so every path, fill and stroke can be animated. Use when: a logo, icon or illustration from a vector editor must become shape layers. Do not use for: raw SVG path data (shape-from-svg-path) or placing footage unchanged (import-file). Inputs: path, absolute; comp; name for the shape layer; position; removeSource (default false) deletes the vector footage layer; removeFootageItem also deletes the imported item when nothing else uses it; mode auto, footage or composition for SVG. Returns: the route used, the shape layers with their group names, the source item, the source layer or what was removed, and notes. Notes: SVG needs After Effects 26.0 or later; an SVG that arrives as a composition of shape layers has those layers copied into the comp. The menu command opens the comp in the viewer and brings After Effects to the front; the selection is restored. Undoable in one step. Example: path "~/Design/logo.ai", comp {name: "Main"}, removeSource true.

Pack: shapes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `importVectorAsShapes`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | yes | Absolute path to the .ai, .eps, .pdf or .svg file. "~/" is expanded. |
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `name` | string | no | Name for the shape layer. Default: After Effects names it "<file> Outlines". |
| `position` | [number, number] | no | Position of the new layers in comp pixels. Default: where After Effects places them (comp centre). |
| `removeSource` | boolean | no | Delete the vector footage layer after the conversion. Default false (After Effects hides it). |
| `removeFootageItem` | boolean | no | Also delete the imported footage or SVG composition from the project when nothing else uses it. Default false. |
| `mode` | "auto" \| "footage" \| "composition" | no | SVG only: auto (default) tries footage then composition; footage forces Create Shapes from Vector Layer; composition copies the shape layers of an SVG composition. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "import-vector-as-shapes",
  "arguments": {
    "path": "/absolute/path/file"
  }
}
```

### list-shape-groups

Returns the Contents tree of a shape layer: every group with its paths, fills, strokes, gradient fills, transform and modifiers, and the modifiers at the root, each with name, matchName, kind, index and full property path. Use when: before set-shape-fill, set-shape-stroke, set-shape-path or add-shape-modifier, to get the group name or the exact path of a Path property; or after convert-text-to-shapes to see the per-glyph groups. Do not use for: layers that are not shape layers (it errors) or property values (get-layer-details). Inputs: layer; depth (default 3; 1 lists only the root items). Returns: composition, layer, groupCount, and contents[] of nodes {name, matchName, kind, index, path, enabled, children[]}. Notes: read-only. kind is one of group, path, rect, ellipse, star, fill, gradient-fill, stroke, gradient-stroke, transform, modifier, other. Example: layer {name: "LOGO Outlines"}, depth 2.

Pack: shapes. Read-only. Bridge command: `listShapeGroups`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `depth` | integer | no | Default 3. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-shape-groups",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-shape-fill

Sets the fill of a group on a shape layer: solid colour, opacity and fill rule, or switches the group to a gradient fill with type, start and end points. Adds a fill if the group has none. Use when: recolouring a card or icon, fading a shape's fill, or turning a flat fill into a gradient. Do not use for: strokes (set-shape-stroke) or text colour (set-text-style). Inputs: layer; group by name or index (default the first group); color; opacity in percent; fillRule "non-zero" or "even-odd"; gradient {type "linear" or "radial", start [x, y], end [x, y], stops[] of {position 0 to 1, color}}. Returns: the fill property path, its current values, the fields changed and warnings. Notes: this tool cannot write gradient colour stops, so stops are accepted but reported in warnings and left at the default; use set-shape-gradient to set them. Requesting a gradient replaces a solid fill and vice versa. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Card"}, color "#1e90ff", opacity 85.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setShapeFill`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `group` | string \| integer | no | Which group under the shape layer's Contents: name or 1-based index. Default: the first group. |
| `color` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour: "#RRGGBB", "#RRGGBBAA", a basic name, [r,g,b(,a)] in 0-1 (or 0-255 if any value is above 1), or {r,g,b,a}. |
| `opacity` | number | no | Percent. |
| `fillRule` | "non-zero" \| "even-odd" | no |  |
| `gradient` | {type?: "linear" \| "radial", start?: [number, number], end?: [number, number], stops?: object[]} | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-shape-fill",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-shape-gradient

Gives a group on a shape layer a Gradient Fill or Gradient Stroke: linear or radial type, start and end points, highlight length and angle, opacity, width and colour stops. Use when: a card, button or logo shape needs a gradient with chosen colours. Do not use for: solid colours (set-shape-fill, set-shape-stroke). Inputs: layer; group; target fill or stroke; type; start and end in group pixels; stops [{offset 0 to 1, color, opacity}]; highlightLength, highlightAngle; opacity; width; replaceSolid (default true); allowClipboard (default false); stopsOptional; templateFolder. Returns: the gradient item, type, points, opacity, width, fields changed, and stops {requested, applied, route, reason}. Notes: scripts cannot write colour stops, so stops travel in a preset made from a template saved once by hand (gradient-fill.ffx or gradient-stroke.ffx in templateFolder or <bridge folder>/templates) and are pasted into the group. That replaces the clipboard and brings After Effects to the front, so it runs only with allowClipboard true. Otherwise type and points are set and the call fails with unsupported unless stopsOptional. Example: layer {name: "Card"}, type "linear", start [-200, 0], end [200, 0], stops [{offset: 0, color: "#6A5CFF"}, {offset: 1, color: "#00C2FF"}].

Pack: shapes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setShapeGradient`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `group` | string \| integer | no | Which group under the shape layer's Contents: name or 1-based index. Default: the first group. |
| `target` | "fill" \| "stroke" | no | Gradient Fill (default) or Gradient Stroke. |
| `type` | "linear" \| "radial" | no | Gradient type. Default linear for a new gradient; unchanged on an existing one. |
| `start` | [number, number] | no | Start point in group pixels. |
| `end` | [number, number] | no | End point in group pixels. |
| `stops` | {offset: number, color: string \| number[] \| object, opacity?: number}[] | no | Colour stops. At least two. |
| `highlightLength` | number | no | Radial only: Highlight Length in percent. |
| `highlightAngle` | number | no | Radial only: Highlight Angle in degrees. |
| `opacity` | number | no | Opacity of the gradient fill or stroke in percent. |
| `width` | number | no | Stroke width in pixels (target stroke). Default: the width of the solid stroke it replaces, else 2. |
| `replaceSolid` | boolean | no | Remove the solid Fill (or Stroke) of the group so only the gradient draws. Default true. |
| `allowClipboard` | boolean | no | Allow the copy and paste route that writes colour stops: it replaces the clipboard and brings After Effects to the front. Default false, so unattended runs never take over the screen. |
| `stopsOptional` | boolean | no | Report stops that could not be set as a warning instead of failing. Default false. |
| `templateFolder` | string | no | Folder holding gradient-fill.ffx and gradient-stroke.ffx. Default: AE_MCP_GRADIENT_TEMPLATES, else <bridge folder>/templates. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-shape-gradient",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-shape-path

Replaces the vertices of a bezier Path on a shape layer, as a static value or as a keyframe at a time for path morphing. Use when: drawing a custom outline, or morphing one shape into another by keying two vertex sets with the same point count. Do not use for: parametric Rectangle or Ellipse paths (their size lives on Size; this tool reports them) or masks (set-mask-path-keyframe). Inputs: layer; group by name or index, or pathProperty as a full property path such as "Contents/Group 1/Path 1/Path"; points[] of [x, y] in group pixels; inTangents[] and outTangents[] (same length, relative to each vertex, default straight); closed (default true); time or frame plus easing to write a keyframe instead. Returns: the path property state, vertexCount, closed, and the keyframe when one was written. Notes: for a morph, both keys must have the same vertex count and order. If the property already has keys and no time is given, a key is set at the current time. Undoable in one step. Example: group "Group 1", points [[-100, -100], [100, -100], [0, 100]], frame 24, easing "ease-in-out".

Pack: shapes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setShapePath`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `group` | string \| integer | no | Which group under the shape layer's Contents: name or 1-based index. Default: the first group. |
| `pathProperty` | string \| string \| integer[] | no | Explicit path to a Path property. Wins over group. |
| `points` | [number, number][] | yes |  |
| `inTangents` | [number, number][] | no |  |
| `outTangents` | [number, number][] | no |  |
| `closed` | boolean | no | Default true. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-shape-path",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "points": [
      [
        0.5,
        0.5
      ]
    ]
  }
}
```

### set-shape-stroke
