Sets the stroke of a group on a shape layer: colour, width, opacity, line cap, line join, miter limit and dash pattern. Adds a stroke if the group has none. Use when: outlining a shape, preparing a line for a trim-paths draw-on, or making a dashed border. Do not use for: fills (set-shape-fill) or text strokes (set-text-style). Inputs: layer; group by name or index (default first); color; width in pixels; opacity in percent; lineCap butt, round or square; lineJoin miter, round or bevel; miterLimit; dashes as [dash, gap, dash, gap, ...] up to three pairs, in pixels; dashOffset. Returns: the stroke property path, its values, the fields changed and notes. Notes: a new stroke defaults to white, 2 px. Dash entries are added to the Dashes group only when given; existing entries are updated in place. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Line"}, color "#ffffff", width 6, lineCap "round", dashes [20, 12].

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setShapeStroke`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `group` | string \| integer | no | Which group under the shape layer's Contents: name or 1-based index. Default: the first group. |
| `color` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour: "#RRGGBB", "#RRGGBBAA", a basic name, [r,g,b(,a)] in 0-1 (or 0-255 if any value is above 1), or {r,g,b,a}. |
| `width` | number | no | Pixels. |
| `opacity` | number | no | Percent. |
| `lineCap` | "butt" \| "round" \| "square" | no |  |
| `lineJoin` | "miter" \| "round" \| "bevel" | no |  |
| `miterLimit` | number | no | Miter limit for miter joins. Default 4. |
| `dashes` | number[] | no | [dash, gap, ...] in pixels. |
| `dashOffset` | number | no | Pixels. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-shape-stroke",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### shape-from-svg-path

Draws SVG path data as After Effects bezier paths. The "d" string is parsed on the server (M, L, H, V, C, S, Q, T, A, Z, relative forms too; arcs become cubics; each subpath becomes a Path in one group) and written as a new shape layer, a new group, or the paths of an existing group. Use when: an icon or logo is available as SVG path data. Do not use for: whole .svg or .ai files (import-vector-as-shapes) or plain rectangles and ellipses (create-shape-layer). Inputs: d; viewBox; size to fit into, or scale (wins) and offset; center (default true) puts the viewBox or path centre on the group origin; layer and group to add to or replace; name, groupName, position; fillColor, noFill, strokeColor, strokeWidth, fillRule. Returns: the layer, the group with its path count and property paths, vertexCount, the placement and the bounds in group pixels. Notes: holes need the right fill rule (most icon sets use non-zero). Replacing a group's paths keeps its fill, stroke and transform. Undoable in one step. Example: d "M12 2a10 10 0 1 0 0 20a10 10 0 1 0 0-20z", viewBox [0, 0, 24, 24], size [240, 240], fillColor "#FFFFFF".

Pack: shapes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `shapeFromSvgPath`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `d` | string | yes | SVG path data, the "d" attribute. |
| `viewBox` | [number, number, number, number] | no | [minX, minY, width, height] of the SVG viewBox. |
| `size` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] in pixels to fit the viewBox (or the path bounds) into, keeping the aspect ratio. Default: 1 path unit = 1 pixel. |
| `scale` | number \| [number, number] | no | Pixels per path unit, one number or [x, y]. Wins over size. |
| `offset` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] pixels added after scaling, relative to the group origin. Default [0, 0]. |
| `center` | boolean | no | Centre the viewBox (or the path bounds when there is no viewBox) on the group origin. Default true. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Existing shape layer to draw on. Default: a new shape layer. |
| `group` | string \| integer | no | With layer: an existing group whose paths are replaced. Default: a new group. |
| `name` | string | no | Name of a new shape layer. Default "SVG Path". |
| `groupName` | string | no | Name of a new group. Default "SVG Path". |
| `position` | [number, number] | no | Position of a new shape layer in comp pixels. Default: comp centre. |
| `fillColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Fill colour. Default white when no stroke is given. |
| `noFill` | boolean | no | No fill. |
| `strokeColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour: "#RRGGBB", "#RRGGBBAA", a basic name, [r,g,b(,a)] in 0-1 (or 0-255 if any value is above 1), or {r,g,b,a}. |
| `strokeWidth` | number | no | Stroke width in pixels. 0 or omitted means no stroke unless strokeColor is given (then 2). |
| `fillRule` | "non-zero" \| "even-odd" | no | Fill rule for overlapping subpaths. Default non-zero, like SVG. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "shape-from-svg-path",
  "arguments": {
    "d": "value",
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

## Masks

### create-mask

Adds a mask to a layer as a rectangle, ellipse, custom point path, or a rectangle around the visible text or source bounds, with mode, inversion, feather, opacity and expansion set in the same call. A mask hides everything on the layer outside its path. Use when: cropping a layer, making a soft vignette on a solid, or preparing a reveal. Do not use for: shape layer geometry (create-shape-layer) or copying a shape path onto a layer (create-mask-from-shape-layer). Inputs: layer; shape rectangle, ellipse, points or text-bounds; rect {x, y, width, height} or size plus center in layer pixels (origin top-left of the layer); points[] with optional tangents and closed; padding around the bounds; mode add, subtract, intersect, lighten, darken, difference or none; inverted; feather as a number or [x, y]; opacity; expansion; name; time or frame for measuring text bounds. Returns: the mask (index, name, path "Masks/<name>", mode, feather, opacity, expansion, vertexCount) and the composition and layer references. Notes: with no geometry the mask covers the layer bounds. Ellipses are four bezier vertices. Cameras and lights cannot hold masks. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Photo"}, shape "ellipse", size [600, 600], feather 80.

Pack: masks. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createMask`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `shape` | "rectangle" \| "ellipse" \| "points" \| "text-bounds" | no | Geometry kind. Inferred from points, rect or size when omitted; default rectangle over the layer bounds. |
| `rect` | {x: number, y: number, width: number, height: number} | no | Top-left corner and size in layer pixels (origin at the layer's top-left). |
| `size` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] in layer pixels, used with center. |
| `center` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] centre in layer pixels. Default: the layer centre. |
| `points` | [number, number][] | no | Vertices in layer pixels for shape "points". |
| `inTangents` | [number, number][] | no | Per-vertex incoming tangents, relative to the vertex. |
| `outTangents` | [number, number][] | no | Per-vertex outgoing tangents. |
| `closed` | boolean | no | Close the path. Default true. |
| `padding` | number | no | Pixels added around the layer or text bounds. Default 0. |
| `mode` | "none" \| "add" \| "subtract" \| "intersect" \| "lighten" \| "darken" \| "difference" | no | How the mask combines with the layer and other masks. Default add. |
| `inverted` | boolean | no |  |
| `feather` | number \| [number, number] | no | Feather in pixels, one number for both axes or [x, y]. |
| `opacity` | number | no | Mask opacity in percent. |
| `expansion` | number | no | Mask expansion in pixels; negative shrinks. |
| `name` | string | no | Mask name. Default "Mask N". |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-mask",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### create-mask-from-shape-layer

Copies the path of a group on a shape layer onto another layer as a mask, translating the vertices from the shape layer's space into the target layer's space using both layers' position and anchor point. Parametric rectangles, ellipses, polygons and stars are converted to bezier vertices. Use when: a shape layer was drawn (or text was outlined with convert-text-to-shapes) and the same outline should crop footage, a solid or a precomp. Do not use for: masks from scratch (create-mask). Inputs: layer (the target); shapeLayer; group by name or index on the shape layer (default first); mode, inverted, feather, opacity, expansion, name; time or frame at which the path is read. Returns: the new mask summary, the source group path, and the offset applied. Notes: best effort: rotation and scale on either layer, the group transform's rotation and scale, and rectangle roundness are ignored. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Footage"}, shapeLayer {name: "LOGO Outlines"}, group 1, feather 4.

Pack: masks. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createMaskFromShapeLayer`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `shapeLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The shape layer to read the path from. |
| `group` | string \| integer | no | Which group under the shape layer's Contents: name or 1-based index. Default: the first group. |
| `mode` | "none" \| "add" \| "subtract" \| "intersect" \| "lighten" \| "darken" \| "difference" | no | How the mask combines with the layer and other masks. Default add. |
| `inverted` | boolean | no |  |
| `feather` | number \| [number, number] | no | Feather in pixels, one number for both axes or [x, y]. |
| `opacity` | number | no | Mask opacity in percent. |
| `expansion` | number | no | Mask expansion in pixels; negative shrinks. |
| `name` | string | no | Mask name. Default "Mask N". |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-mask-from-shape-layer",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "shapeLayer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### delete-mask

Removes one mask from a layer by name or index, with its path, feather, opacity and expansion keyframes. Use when: cleaning up an experiment or replacing a mask with a different one. Do not use for: hiding a mask temporarily (set-mask-properties with mode "none" keeps it) or shape groups on a shape layer. Inputs: layer; mask by name or index. Returns: the removed mask (index, name) and the remaining masks with their indices and names. Notes: indices of the masks below it shift up by one. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Photo"}, mask "Mask 2".

Pack: masks. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `deleteMask`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `mask` | string \| integer | yes | Which mask: name or 1-based index. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "delete-mask",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "mask": "value"
  }
}
```

### list-masks

Lists the masks on a layer with index, name, path, mode, inverted, locked, colour, feather, opacity, expansion, vertex count, whether the path is closed, and keyframe counts for path, feather, opacity and expansion. Use when: before set-mask-properties, delete-mask or set-mask-path-keyframe, to get the mask name or index; or to check what a reveal animation wrote. Do not use for: shape layer contents (list-shape-groups). Inputs: layer. Returns: composition, layer, count and masks[]. Notes: read-only. Index 1 is the top mask in the Masks group. Vertex counts are read at the current time. Example: layer {name: "Photo"}.

Pack: masks. Read-only. Bridge command: `listMasks`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-masks",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### mask-reveal-animation

Adds a rectangular mask covering the layer's bounds and keyframes it so the layer wipes on: from an edge (the mask grows in the chosen direction), from the centre outward, by feather (a soft resolve from blurred edges to sharp) or by expansion (the mask grows from nothing). Use when: revealing footage, a card, a photo or a precomp with a wipe. Do not use for: text (add-text-animator) or stroked paths (animate-trim-paths). Inputs: layer; direction right (grows from the left edge toward the right), left, down (grows from the top edge), up, center-out, feather or expansion; startTime or startFrame (default the layer in point); duration in seconds (default 1) or durationFrames; easing (default ease-out); padding in pixels around the bounds; featherAmount, the starting feather for direction "feather" (default 200); name; mode. Returns: the mask summary, the direction, the keyframes written per property, and the time range in seconds and frames. Notes: bounds come from sourceRectAtTime at the start time, so a layer whose content moves may need padding. The mask is new each call; delete-mask removes it. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Photo"}, direction "right", durationFrames 20.

Pack: masks. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `maskRevealAnimation`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `direction` | "left" \| "right" \| "up" \| "down" \| "center-out" \| "feather" \| "expansion" | yes |  |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds. Default: the layer in point. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default 1. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Default ease-out. |
| `padding` | number | no | Pixels added around the bounds. Default 0. |
| `featherAmount` | number | no | Starting feather for direction "feather". Default 200. |
| `name` | string | no | Name for the mask that is created. |
| `mode` | "none" \| "add" \| "subtract" \| "intersect" \| "lighten" \| "darken" \| "difference" | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "mask-reveal-animation",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "direction": "left"
  }
}
```

### set-mask-path-keyframe

Writes the Mask Path of an existing mask as a keyframe at a time (or as the static value when no time is given), from a rectangle, ellipse or vertex list, with easing. Two calls with different geometry make the mask animate between them. Use when: hand-building a wipe or a morphing cut-out. Do not use for: the standard reveals (mask-reveal-animation) or shape layers (set-shape-path). Inputs: layer; mask by name or index; shape rectangle, ellipse or points (inferred from rect, size or points); rect or size plus center in layer pixels; points[] with optional tangents and closed; padding; time or frame; easing describing the motion arriving at this key. Returns: the mask summary, the keyframe written (index, time, frame) and vertexCount. Notes: mask path keys interpolate vertex by vertex, so keep the same vertex count and order across keys (rectangle and ellipse both use four vertices). Undoable in one step. Example: mask 1, rect {x: 0, y: 0, width: 0, height: 1080}, frame 0; then rect {x: 0, y: 0, width: 1920, height: 1080}, frame 20, easing "ease-out".

Pack: masks. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setMaskPathKeyframe`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `mask` | string \| integer | yes | Which mask: name or 1-based index. |
| `shape` | "rectangle" \| "ellipse" \| "points" \| "text-bounds" | no | Geometry kind. Inferred from points, rect or size when omitted; default rectangle over the layer bounds. |
| `rect` | {x: number, y: number, width: number, height: number} | no | Top-left corner and size in layer pixels (origin at the layer's top-left). |
| `size` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] in layer pixels, used with center. |
| `center` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] centre in layer pixels. Default: the layer centre. |
| `points` | [number, number][] | no | Vertices in layer pixels for shape "points". |
| `inTangents` | [number, number][] | no | Per-vertex incoming tangents, relative to the vertex. |
| `outTangents` | [number, number][] | no | Per-vertex outgoing tangents. |
| `closed` | boolean | no | Close the path. Default true. |
| `padding` | number | no | Pixels added around the layer or text bounds. Default 0. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-mask-path-keyframe",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "mask": "value"
  }
}
```

### set-mask-properties

Changes a mask's mode, inverted flag, feather, opacity, expansion, name, lock and colour. With a time or frame the feather, opacity and expansion values are written as keyframes with easing. Use when: softening a mask edge, fading a mask, switching add to subtract, or keying a feather change. Do not use for: the path itself (set-mask-path-keyframe) or full reveals (mask-reveal-animation). Inputs: layer; mask by name or index; mode; inverted; feather (number or [x, y]); opacity in percent; expansion in pixels; name; locked; color in any colour form; time or frame plus easing for keyframes. Returns: the mask summary, the fields changed, and keyframes written. Notes: if a property already has keys and no time is given, a key is set at the current time. Undoable in one step. Example: mask "Mask 1", feather [40, 40], frame 12, easing "ease-out".

Pack: masks. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setMaskProperties`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `mask` | string \| integer | yes | Which mask: name or 1-based index. |
| `mode` | "none" \| "add" \| "subtract" \| "intersect" \| "lighten" \| "darken" \| "difference" | no | How the mask combines with the layer and other masks. Default add. |
| `inverted` | boolean | no |  |
| `feather` | number \| [number, number] | no | Feather in pixels, one number for both axes or [x, y]. |
| `opacity` | number | no | Mask opacity in percent. |
| `expansion` | number | no | Mask expansion in pixels; negative shrinks. |
| `name` | string | no | Mask name. Default "Mask N". |
| `locked` | boolean | no |  |
| `color` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Mask outline colour in the timeline. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-mask-properties",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "mask": "value"
  }
}
```

## Effects

### apply-effect

Adds an effect to a layer by display name or matchName and sets any of its properties in the same call. Idempotent by default: if the layer already carries that effect, the existing one is returned and updated instead of a duplicate being stacked; pass allowDuplicate true to stack. Use when: adding any single effect. Do not use for: the built-in looks (apply-effect-template), several layers (apply-effects-bulk), or .ffx files (apply-preset). Inputs: layer; effect, a display name ("Gaussian Blur") or matchName ("ADBE Gaussian Blur 2", more reliable); settings, an object of property name to value ("Blurriness": 25, colours in any form, layer references as {name} for layer-type properties); effectName to rename it; enabled; moveToIndex; includeProperties (default true) to return the property tree. Returns: created (true) or alreadyPresent (true), the effect with its index, matchName and top-level properties, which settings were applied, and notes for any that were not. Notes: unknown effect names fail with a hint to use list-available-effects. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Title"}, effect "ADBE Drop Shadow", settings {"Distance": 8, "Softness": 20}.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `applyEffect`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `effect` | string | yes | Display name or matchName of the effect to add. |
| `settings` | object of any | no | Property values to set right after adding, keyed by display name. |
| `effectName` | string | no | Rename the effect instance. |
| `enabled` | boolean | no |  |
| `allowDuplicate` | boolean | no | Stack a second copy even if the effect is already present. Default false. |
| `moveToIndex` | integer | no | Position in the effect stack after adding. |
| `includeProperties` | boolean | no | Return the effect property tree. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "apply-effect",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "effect": "value"
  }
}
```

### apply-effect-template

Applies one of the built-in looks, each a tested stack of stock effects with sensible values: blurs, shadows (drop, soft, long), outline, glows (glow, neon-glow, deep-glow-with-fallback), grain, vignette, letterbox, posterize-time, pixelate, motion-tile, wipe transitions (linear, radial, venetian-blinds, keyframed over start and duration), desaturate, tint, lumetri-basic, gradient-ramp, 4-color-gradient, fractal-noise-texture, turbulent-displace, wave-warp, cc-light-sweep, cinematic-look, text-pop. Use when: you want the look, not the parameter hunt. Do not use for: an effect not covered (apply-effect) or your own .ffx (apply-preset). Inputs: layer; template name; params, an object of the template's parameters (list-effect-templates shows names, defaults and meanings). Unknown params fail before anything changes. Returns: the effects added and notes, including which glow was used by deep-glow-with-fallback. Notes: a zero value is respected. Templates are not idempotent; applying twice stacks. Undoable in one step. Example: template "soft-shadow", params {opacity: 40, softness: 80}.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `applyEffectTemplate`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `template` | "gaussian-blur" \| "directional-blur" \| "color-balance" \| "brightness-contrast" \| "curves" \| "glow" \| "neon-glow" \| "deep-glow-with-fallback" \| "drop-shadow" \| "soft-shadow" \| "long-shadow" \| "outline" \| "chromatic-aberration" \| "film-grain" \| "vignette" \| "letterbox" \| "posterize-time" \| "pixelate" \| "motion-tile" \| "linear-wipe-transition" \| "radial-wipe-transition" \| "venetian-blinds" \| "desaturate" \| "tint" \| "lumetri-basic" \| "gradient-ramp" \| "4-color-gradient" \| "fractal-noise-texture" \| "turbulent-displace" \| "wave-warp" \| "cc-light-sweep" \| "cinematic-look" \| "text-pop" | yes |  |
| `params` | object of any | no | Template parameters. See list-effect-templates. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "apply-effect-template",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "template": "gaussian-blur"
  }
}
```

### apply-effects-bulk

Applies many effects to many layers in one round-trip. Each item has the same shape as apply-effect (layer, effect, settings, allowDuplicate). Use when: giving every layer in a group the same blur or shadow, or building a stack of effects on one layer. Do not use for: a single effect (apply-effect). Inputs: items[]; comp as the default for every item; stopOnError (default false). Returns: per-item status with the effect created or found, and a failed count. Notes: idempotent per item like apply-effect. One undo step. Example: items = five layers each with effect "ADBE Gaussian Blur 2" and settings {"Blurriness": 12}.

Pack: effects. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `applyEffectsBulk`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `items` | {comp?: object, layer: object, effect: string, settings?: object of any, effectName?: string, allowDuplicate?: boolean, enabled?: boolean}[] | yes |  |
| `stopOnError` | boolean | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "apply-effects-bulk",
  "arguments": {
    "items": [
      {
        "layer": {
          "name": "My Layer"
        },
        "effect": "value"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### copy-effects

Copies all or selected effects, with their values, keyframes and expressions, from one layer to one or more other layers, in the same or another composition. Use when: applying a finished look to sibling layers. Do not use for: presets on disk (apply-preset). Inputs: fromLayer; toLayer or toLayers[] (or {selected: true}); toComp (optional); effects[] to copy only some, by name, matchName or index. Returns: per target and effect, ok or unavailable (when a plugin is missing). Notes: layer-reference properties are copied by index, which may point at a different layer in another comp. Undoable in one step. Example: fromLayer {name: "Title"}, toLayers [{name: "Subtitle"}, {name: "Tag"}].

Pack: effects. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `copyEffects`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `fromLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `toLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `toLayers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean} | no |  |
| `toComp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `effects` | string \| integer \| object[] | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "copy-effects",
  "arguments": {
    "fromLayer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### get-effect-properties

Returns the full property tree of one effect with names, matchNames, paths, value types, current values, keyframe counts, expressions, and min and max where After Effects reports them. Use when: you need exact property names for set-effect-property or want to see a plugin's controls. Do not use for: the whole layer (get-layer-details). Inputs: layer; effect by name, matchName or index; depth (default 3). Returns: composition, layer, effect with properties[]. Notes: read-only. Example: effect "CC Light Sweep".

Pack: effects. Read-only. Bridge command: `getEffectProperties`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `effect` | string \| integer \| {index?: integer, name?: string, matchName?: string} | yes | Which effect on the layer: name, matchName, index, or an object with one of those. |
| `depth` | integer | no | Property tree depth. Default 3. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "get-effect-properties",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "effect": "value"
  }
}
```

### list-available-effects

Lists the effects installed in this After Effects, including third-party plugins, with display name, matchName and category, filtered by a search word or a category. Use when: you are not sure of an effect's exact name or matchName, or want to know whether a plugin such as Deep Glow is installed. Do not use for: effects on a layer (list-layer-effects). Inputs: query, a case-insensitive substring of the name, matchName or category; category, for example "Blur & Sharpen"; maxResults (default 500). Returns: totalInstalled, returned, a categories count map, and effects[] with displayName, matchName, category. Notes: read-only. The result is cached inside the panel for the session. Prefer matchNames in later calls. Example: query "glow".

Pack: effects. Read-only. Bridge command: `listAvailableEffects`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `query` | string | no | Case-insensitive substring of the name, matchName or category. |
| `category` | string | no | Category name such as "Blur & Sharpen". |
| `maxResults` | integer | no | Maximum effects to return. Default 500. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-available-effects",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### list-effect-templates

Lists the built-in effect templates with a description of each look and every parameter's name, default value and meaning, so you can call apply-effect-template without guessing. Use when: choosing a look for apply-effect-template, checking a parameter name, or telling the human what looks are available. Do not use for: effects installed in After Effects (list-available-effects) or presets on disk (list-presets). Inputs: none. Returns: count and templates[] with name, description and params[] of {name, defaultValue, description}. Notes: read-only. Template names are stable and also appear as the template enum of apply-effect-template. Example: call it once, then apply-effect-template "vignette" with params {amount: 45}.

Pack: effects. Read-only. Bridge command: `listEffectTemplates`.

Inputs: none.

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-effect-templates",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### list-layer-effects

Lists the effects on a layer in stack order with index, name, matchName and enabled state, optionally with each effect's property tree and current values. Use when: before set-effect-property or remove-effect, to get the exact effect name and property names. Do not use for: effects available to install (list-available-effects). Inputs: layer; includeProperties (default false); depth (default 2). Returns: count and effects[]. Notes: read-only. Example: layer {index: 2}, includeProperties true.

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Bridge command: `listLayerEffects`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `includeProperties` | boolean | no |  |
| `depth` | integer | no | Property tree depth when includeProperties is true. Default 2. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-layer-effects",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### remove-all-effects

Removes every effect from a layer in one call, leaving transform, masks, text and layer styles untouched. Use when: resetting a layer before rebuilding its look, or stripping effects a preset added that you do not want. Do not use for: one effect (remove-effect) or switching effects off while keeping them (toggle-effect). Inputs: layer. Returns: how many effects were removed and their names and matchNames, in stack order. Notes: layer styles are not effects and stay. Keyframes and expressions on the removed effects are lost; undo brings everything back in one step. Example: layer {name: "Card"}.

Pack: effects. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `removeAllEffects`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "remove-all-effects",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### remove-effect

Removes one effect from a layer, identified by its display name, matchName or position in the effect stack. Use when: undoing an experiment, replacing an effect with a different one, or cleaning up a layer before copying its look elsewhere. Do not use for: clearing everything (remove-all-effects) or disabling temporarily so it can be compared (toggle-effect). Inputs: layer; effect by name, matchName or 1-based index. Returns: the removed effect (index, name, matchName) and the names of the effects that remain, in order. Notes: keyframes and expressions on the effect go with it. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Title"}, effect "Gaussian Blur".

Pack: effects. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `removeEffect`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `effect` | string \| integer \| {index?: integer, name?: string, matchName?: string} | yes | Which effect on the layer: name, matchName, index, or an object with one of those. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "remove-effect",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "effect": "value"
  }
}
```

### reorder-effect

Moves an effect to a new position in the layer's effect stack. Order matters: a blur before a glow looks different from a glow before a blur. Use when: fixing render order after adding effects. Do not use for: ordering layers (move-layer). Inputs: layer; effect; toIndex, or toTop, or toBottom. Returns: the effect's new index and the whole stack order. Notes: undoable in one step. Example: effect "Drop Shadow", toBottom true.

Pack: effects. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `reorderEffect`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `effect` | string \| integer \| {index?: integer, name?: string, matchName?: string} | yes | Which effect on the layer: name, matchName, index, or an object with one of those. |
| `toIndex` | integer | no | 1-based position in the effect stack. |
| `toTop` | boolean | no |  |
| `toBottom` | boolean | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "reorder-effect",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "effect": "value"
  }
}
```

### set-effect-keyframe

Sets a keyframe on an effect property at a time or frame with easing, using the same easing model as layer keyframes. Use when: animating one effect control with a single key. Do not use for: two or more keys (set-keyframes-bulk with property "Effects/<effect>/<property>" writes them in one call). Inputs: layer; effect; property; value; time or frame; easing. Returns: the property state and the keyframe written. Notes: undoable in one step. Example: effect "Glow", property "Glow Intensity", value 2.5, frame 0, then again with value 0 at frame 24 and easing "ease-out".

Pack: effects. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setEffectKeyframe`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `effect` | string \| integer \| {index?: integer, name?: string, matchName?: string} | yes | Which effect on the layer: name, matchName, index, or an object with one of those. |
| `property` | string \| string \| integer[] | yes | Property under the effect: a display name such as "Blurriness", a nested path "Compositing Options/Effect Opacity", or an index. |
| `value` | any | yes | Value in the property's units. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-effect-keyframe",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "effect": "value",
    "property": "Transform/Opacity",
    "value": 100
  }
}
```

### set-effect-properties

Sets many properties on one effect from a name to value object, and optionally toggles the effect. Use when: configuring an effect with several controls (Drop Shadow distance, softness, direction and colour). Do not use for: keyframes (set-effect-property with time, or set-keyframes-bulk). Inputs: layer; effect; settings object keyed by property display name; enabled. Returns: the effect with its top-level properties, the list applied, and notes for names that were not found. Notes: names are matched exactly first, then case-insensitively, then by depth-first search, so nested controls can be named directly. Undoable in one step. Example: effect "Drop Shadow", settings {"Distance": 12, "Softness": 30, "Shadow Color": "#000000"}.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setEffectProperties`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `effect` | string \| integer \| {index?: integer, name?: string, matchName?: string} | yes | Which effect on the layer: name, matchName, index, or an object with one of those. |
| `settings` | object of any | yes |  |
| `enabled` | boolean | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-effect-properties",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "effect": "value",
    "settings": {}
  }
}
```

### set-effect-property

Sets one property on an effect: a static value, a keyframe at a time (with easing), or an expression. Layer-type properties (blur layer, displacement map layer) accept a layer reference. Use when: tuning an effect after apply-effect, or keying one control. Do not use for: several properties at once (set-effect-properties) or several keys (set-keyframes-bulk with the path "Effects/<name>/<property>"). Inputs: layer; effect; property name or nested path; value; time or frame to key instead of set; easing for that key; expression. Returns: the property state, the previous value, and the keyframe if one was written. Notes: if the property already has keys and no time is given, a key is set at the current time. Colours accept any colour form. Undoable in one step. Example: effect "Gaussian Blur", property "Blurriness", value 0, frame 20, easing "ease-out".

Pack: effects. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setEffectProperty`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `effect` | string \| integer \| {index?: integer, name?: string, matchName?: string} | yes | Which effect on the layer: name, matchName, index, or an object with one of those. |
| `property` | string \| string \| integer[] | yes | Property under the effect: a display name such as "Blurriness", a nested path "Compositing Options/Effect Opacity", or an index. |
| `value` | any | no | Value in the property's units. Colours in any form; layer properties accept a layer reference. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |
| `expression` | string | no | Expression source to set on the property. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-effect-property",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "effect": "value",
    "property": "Transform/Opacity"
  }
}
```

### toggle-effect

Enables or disables an effect without removing it, the same as the fx switch next to the effect name. Use when: comparing a frame with and without an effect (see-frame twice), or disabling a heavy effect such as a large glow while previewing other work. Do not use for: removal (remove-effect) or effect opacity (set-effect-property on "Compositing Options/Effect Opacity"). Inputs: layer; effect by name, matchName or index; enabled true or false, or omit it to flip the current state. Returns: the effect summary with its index, matchName and enabled state. Notes: undoable in one step. Example: effect "Deep Glow", enabled false.

Pack: effects. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `toggleEffect`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `effect` | string \| integer \| {index?: integer, name?: string, matchName?: string} | yes | Which effect on the layer: name, matchName, index, or an object with one of those. |
| `enabled` | boolean | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "toggle-effect",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "effect": "value"
  }
}
```

## Animation presets

### apply-preset

Applies an animation preset file (.ffx) to a layer, the same as Animation > Apply Animation Preset. Presets can add effects, keyframes, expressions and text animators. Use when: using After Effects' shipped presets or the studio's own. Do not use for: built-in looks (apply-effect-template) or single effects (apply-effect). Inputs: layer; presetPath, an absolute path from list-presets or search-presets. Returns: the layer summary and the effects that were added by the preset. Notes: text presets need a text layer. Keyframes inside the preset land at the current time indicator; call set-current-time first if it matters. Undoable in one step. Example: presetPath "/Applications/Adobe After Effects 2025/Presets/Text/Animate In/Typewriter.ffx".

Pack: presets. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `applyPreset`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `presetPath` | string | yes | Absolute path to the .ffx file. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "apply-preset",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "presetPath": "/absolute/path/file"
  }
}
```

### list-presets

Lists animation preset files (.ffx) found in the After Effects Presets folder, the user presets folder, or folders you name, with full paths for apply-preset. Runs on the server, so After Effects need not respond. Use when: browsing what ships with After Effects (Text > Animate In, Backgrounds, Transitions) or the user's own presets. Do not use for: searching by name (search-presets) or built-in effect looks (list-effect-templates). Inputs: presetRoots[] (optional), recursive (default true), maxResults (default 500), maxDepth (default 10). Returns: searchedRoots, resultCount, presets[] with path, name, directory, size, modifiedAt. Notes: read-only. Mac and Windows install folders are both searched. Example: list-presets with presetRoots ["/Applications/Adobe After Effects 2025/Presets/Text"].

Pack: presets. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `presetRoots` | string[] | no | Folders to search. Default: the After Effects Presets folder and the user presets folder. |
| `recursive` | boolean | no | Search subfolders. Default true. |
| `maxResults` | integer | no | Maximum preset files to return. |
| `maxDepth` | integer | no | Maximum folder depth. Default 10. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-presets",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### save-preset

Saves the effects on a layer (all, or the ones named) as an animation preset file (.ffx) so they can be reused with apply-preset. Use when: a look is finished and the human wants it in their preset library. Do not use for: copying to other layers in the same project (copy-effects). Inputs: layer; outputPath (an .ffx path, folders are created); effects[] to save a subset. Returns: the preset path and how many effects it holds. Notes: saving presets by script is only available in some After Effects versions; when unsupported the error says to use Animation > Save Animation Preset. Does not change the project. Example: layer {name: "Title"}, outputPath "~/Documents/Adobe/After Effects User Presets/Title Glow.ffx".

Pack: presets. Read-only. Bridge command: `savePreset`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `outputPath` | string | yes | Destination .ffx path. A leading ~ is expanded; folders are created. |
| `effects` | string \| integer \| object[] | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "save-preset",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "outputPath": "/absolute/path/file"
  }
}
```

### search-presets

Searches animation preset files (.ffx) by a word in the file name or folder path and returns full paths for apply-preset. Runs on the server. Use when: the human asks for a named preset ("Typewriter", "Fade Up Characters", "Rain"). Do not use for: listing everything (list-presets). Inputs: query; presetRoots[] (optional); recursive (default true); maxResults (default 200); maxDepth (default 10). Returns: query, searchedRoots, resultCount, presets[]. Notes: read-only, case-insensitive. Example: query "typewriter" then apply-preset with the returned path.

Pack: presets. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `query` | string | yes | Text to match in the file name or path, case-insensitive. |
| `presetRoots` | string[] | no | Folders to search. Default: the After Effects Presets folder and the user presets folder. |
| `recursive` | boolean | no | Search subfolders. Default true. |
| `maxResults` | integer | no | Maximum preset files to return. |
| `maxDepth` | integer | no | Maximum folder depth. Default 10. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "search-presets",
  "arguments": {
    "query": "value"
  }
}
```

## Camera, lights and 3D

### add-environment-light

Adds an Environment light that lights the 3D scene from an image: an HDR or EXR, or (After Effects 25.2 and later) any 2D still, video or comp layer. Sets intensity and rotation, shadows, and an optional background layer showing the environment. Use when: lighting extruded logos, meshes, metal or glass with real reflections. Do not use for: spot, point, parallel or ambient lights (create-light-layer). Inputs: comp; one of source (a 2D layer), sourceItem (a project item placed as a new layer) or sourceFile (a path to import); intensity in percent (default 100); rotation, Y degrees or [x, y, z]; castsShadows; shadowDarkness; useAsBackground; hideSource (default true); switchRenderer; name. Returns: the light (type, options), the source layer, the background layer and how it was made, the renderer, and notes. Notes: needs After Effects 24.3 or later and the Advanced 3D renderer; refuses otherwise unless switchRenderer is true. Rotation and shadows are set while the light is still a spot light, because environment lights hide them. The background uses Create Environment Light Background Layer, else a solid with CC Environment. Undoable in one step. Example: sourceFile "~/HDRI/studio.hdr", intensity 120, rotation 45, useAsBackground true.

Pack: camera-3d. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addEnvironmentLight`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `source` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | An existing 2D layer in the comp to use as the light source. |
| `sourceItem` | {id?: integer, name?: string} | no | A footage item or composition in the project, placed as a new 2D layer. |
| `sourceFile` | string | no | Absolute path of an image, HDR, EXR or video to import and use. "~/" is expanded. |
| `name` | string | no | Light name. Default "Environment Light". |
| `intensity` | number | no | Intensity in percent. Default 100. |
| `rotation` | number \| [number, number, number] | no | Y rotation in degrees, or [x, y, z] rotation in degrees. |
| `castsShadows` | boolean | no | Light casts shadows (Advanced 3D). |
| `shadowDarkness` | number | no | Shadow Darkness in percent. |
| `useAsBackground` | boolean | no | Add a background layer that shows the environment. Default false. |
| `hideSource` | boolean | no | Turn the source layer's visibility off so it does not render flat. Default true. |
| `switchRenderer` | boolean | no | Switch the comp to Advanced 3D first when it uses another renderer. Default false (refuse). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-environment-light",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### animate-camera

Writes a ready-made camera move as keyframes: dolly (along the view axis by pixels), crane (vertically), orbit (around the point of interest by degrees, sampled as linear keys because a circle is not one bezier), push-in and pull-out (zoom by a factor, default 1.3, or move by a distance), and rack-focus (focus distance from one layer to another, depth of field on). Use when: you want a cinematic camera move without computing positions. Do not use for: arbitrary paths (set-keyframes-bulk on Transform/Position) or camera options alone (set-camera-settings). Inputs: layer (a camera); move; amount (pixels, degrees or zoom factor by move); distance in pixels for push-in and pull-out; fromLayer and toLayer for rack-focus; startTime or startFrame (default: current time); duration or durationFrames (default 2 s); easing (default ease-in-out); sampleFrames for orbit (default 2). Returns: the move, the time range, property paths, every keyframe written and notes. Notes: on a one-node camera dolly assumes a +Z view axis and orbit becomes a Y Rotation. Positive crane moves down. Rack-focus uses straight-line distances to each layer's Position. Not verified against a live After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Camera 1"}, move "orbit", amount 45, startFrame 0, durationFrames 90.

Pack: camera-3d. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `animateCamera`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `move` | "dolly" \| "orbit" \| "crane" \| "push-in" \| "pull-out" \| "rack-focus" | yes | Which move to write. |
| `amount` | number | no | dolly and crane: pixels; orbit: degrees; push-in and pull-out: zoom factor (default 1.3). |
| `distance` | number | no | push-in and pull-out only: move the camera this many pixels along its view axis instead of changing zoom. |
| `fromLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | rack-focus: the layer in focus at the start. |
| `toLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | rack-focus: the layer in focus at the end. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Start of the move in seconds. Default: current time. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Start frame; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Length of the move in seconds. Default 2. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Length in frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing of the move. Default "ease-in-out". |
| `sampleFrames` | integer | no | orbit only: frames between sampled keys. Default 2. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "animate-camera",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "move": "dolly"
  }
}
```

### look-at-layer

Aims a camera, a light or a 3D layer at another layer. Two-node cameras and lights get their Point of Interest set to the target's position (a static value, or a keyframe when time or frame is given). One-node cameras and ordinary 3D layers get an Orientation expression, lookAt(position, target position), that keeps following the target. Use when: a camera should frame a specific layer, or a light should point at it. Do not use for: parenting (set-layer-parent) or auto-orient toward the camera (set-layer-3d with autoOrient). Inputs: layer; target (a layer reference); useExpression true to force the expression even on two-node cameras; time or frame and easing for a Point of Interest keyframe. Returns: mode "point-of-interest" or "expression", the property state, the target and the value or expression written. Notes: the target's own Position value is used, parenting ignored; a note says so when the target has a parent. The expression follows the target as it moves; the point of interest value does not unless keyframed. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Camera 1"}, target {name: "Hero"}.

Pack: camera-3d. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `lookAtLayer`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `target` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The layer to look at. |
| `useExpression` | boolean | no | Force the Orientation expression. Default false. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing for a Point of Interest keyframe. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "look-at-layer",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "target": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-camera-settings

Sets any subset of a camera layer's Camera Options: zoom (the distance in pixels from the camera to the plane it sees at 100 percent), focal length in millimetres (converted to zoom using a 36 mm film width, an approximation of the Camera Settings dialog), depth of field on or off, focus distance, aperture, blur level, iris shape, rotation, roundness, aspect ratio and diffraction fringe, and highlight gain, threshold and saturation. Use when: framing a 3D scene, enabling depth of field before a rack focus, or matching a lens. Do not use for: moving the camera (use set-transform or animate-camera) or lights (set-light-settings). Inputs: layer (must be a camera); the option fields; time or frame plus easing to write keyframes instead of static values. Returns: the camera summary (position, point of interest, every camera option, focal length estimate), what was applied with property paths, and notes for options the renderer does not expose. Notes: giving both zoom and focalLength uses zoom. Iris options only affect renders with depth of field on. Not verified against a live After Effects; the mock covers the shape. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Camera 1"}, focalLength 35, depthOfField true, focusDistance 1800, aperture 60.

Pack: camera-3d. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setCameraSettings`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `zoom` | number | no | Zoom in pixels. Larger values narrow the view. |
| `focalLength` | number | no | Focal length in mm, converted to zoom = compWidth * focalLength / 36. Ignored when zoom is given. |
| `depthOfField` | boolean | no | Enable depth of field blur. |
| `focusDistance` | number | no | Distance in pixels from the camera that is in sharp focus. |
| `aperture` | number | no | Aperture in pixels; larger blurs more away from the focus distance. |
| `blurLevel` | number | no | Blur level in percent. 100 is normal. |
| `irisShape` | integer \| "fast-rectangle" \| "triangle" \| "square" \| "pentagon" \| "hexagon" \| "heptagon" \| "octagon" \| "nonagon" \| "decagon" | no | Iris shape as an index 1 to 10 or a name. |
| `irisRotation` | number | no | Iris rotation in degrees. |
| `irisRoundness` | number | no | Iris roundness in percent. |
| `irisAspectRatio` | number | no | Iris aspect ratio. 1 is round. |
| `irisDiffractionFringe` | number | no | Diffraction fringe in percent. |
| `highlightGain` | number | no | Highlight gain in percent. |
| `highlightThreshold` | number | no | Highlight threshold, 0 to 255. |
| `highlightSaturation` | number | no | Highlight saturation in percent. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing for the keyframes written when time or frame is given. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-camera-settings",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-composition-renderer

Sets the 3D renderer of a composition, the same as the 3D Renderer menu in the Composition panel: classic-3d, advanced-3d (match name ADBE Calder) or cinema-4d (ADBE Ernst). Without renderer it only lists what this After Effects offers. Use when: before extruding text or shapes, adding an environment light or meshes (advanced-3d), or going back to Classic 3D for layer styles and older effects. Do not use for: camera or light settings (set-camera-settings, set-light-settings). Inputs: comp; renderer as a name, or matchName for any entry of the available list. Returns: previous and current renderer (matchName and name), changed, the available renderers, and warnings such as layers with layer styles, which do not render in Advanced 3D or Cinema 4D. Notes: set Advanced 3D before adding a mesh or nesting a mesh comp, or After Effects shows a modal dialog. Cinema 4D may be missing from newer versions; the error lists what exists. Undoable in one step. Example: comp {name: "Logo 3D"}, renderer "advanced-3d".

Pack: camera-3d. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setCompositionRenderer`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `renderer` | "classic-3d" \| "advanced-3d" \| "cinema-4d" | no | Renderer to use. Omit to only list the available renderers. |
| `matchName` | string | no | A renderer match name from the available list. Wins over renderer. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-composition-renderer",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### set-extrusion

Sets Geometry Options on a text or shape layer so it becomes a solid 3D object: extrusion depth, bevel style (none, angular, concave, convex), bevel depth and hole bevel depth, as static values or keyframes. Use when: extruding a logo, title or icon for a 3D shot. Do not use for: footage layer curvature, material and shadows (set-material-options) or the renderer (set-composition-renderer). Inputs: layer (text or shape); extrusionDepth and bevelDepth in pixels; bevelStyle; holeBevelDepth in percent; make3d (default true) turns the 3D switch on; keyframes [{time or frame, extrusionDepth, bevelDepth, holeBevelDepth, easing}]. Returns: the renderer, the Geometry Options values read back with property paths, what was applied, keyframes written and notes for options the renderer hides. Notes: refuses unless the comp renderer is Advanced 3D or Cinema 4D, because Classic 3D cannot extrude; run set-composition-renderer first. Bevel Depth is in layer pixels. Keys land softly unless easing is given. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "LOGO"}, extrusionDepth 60, bevelStyle "convex", bevelDepth 4.

Pack: camera-3d. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setExtrusion`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `extrusionDepth` | number | no | Extrusion Depth in pixels. |
| `bevelStyle` | "none" \| "angular" \| "concave" \| "convex" | no | Bevel Style. |
| `bevelDepth` | number | no | Bevel Depth in pixels. |
| `holeBevelDepth` | number | no | Hole Bevel Depth in percent of the bevel depth. |
| `make3d` | boolean | no | Turn the layer's 3D switch on when it is off. Default true. |
| `keyframes` | {time?: number, frame?: integer, extrusionDepth?: number, bevelDepth?: number, holeBevelDepth?: number, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object}[] | no | Keys for extrusion and bevel depths. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-extrusion",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-layer-3d

Turns the 3D switch on or off for one layer or several, and optionally sets auto-orientation: none, along-path (the layer turns to follow its motion path) or camera-or-poi (a 3D layer faces the camera; a camera or light aims at its point of interest). Use when: preparing layers for a camera move, or making cards face the camera. Do not use for: material and shadow settings (set-material-options) or the 3D switch at creation time (create-* tools accept threeD). Inputs: layer, or layers as an array, {selected: true} or {all: true}; threeD true or false; autoOrient. Returns: count and each layer summary with its auto-orient mode, plus notes for layers that were skipped. Notes: turning 3D off on a keyframed layer keeps the Z values but stops using them. Cameras and lights are always 3D, so threeD is ignored for them. Undoable in one step. Example: layers [{name: "Card 1"}, {name: "Card 2"}], threeD true, autoOrient "camera-or-poi".

Pack: camera-3d. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setLayer3d`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean} | no | Several layers: an array of layer references, {selected: true} for the timeline selection, or {all: true}. |
| `threeD` | boolean | no | true turns the 3D switch on, false off. |
| `autoOrient` | "none" \| "along-path" \| "camera-or-poi" | no | Auto-orientation mode. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-layer-3d",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-light-settings

Sets any subset of a light layer's options: light type (parallel, spot, point, ambient), intensity in percent, colour, cone angle and feather for spots, falloff (none, smooth, inverse-square) with radius and falloff distance, casts shadows, shadow darkness and shadow diffusion. Use when: lighting a 3D scene, dimming or recolouring a light, or turning on shadows. Do not use for: aiming the light (use look-at-layer or set-transform) or cameras (set-camera-settings). Inputs: layer (must be a light); lightType; intensity; color in any colour form; coneAngle and coneFeather in degrees and percent; falloff as a name or 1 to 3; radius and falloffDistance in pixels; castsShadows; shadowDarkness and shadowDiffusion; time or frame plus easing to write keyframes. Returns: the light summary (type, position, point of interest, every light option), what was applied with property paths, and notes for options the light type does not expose. Notes: ambient lights only have intensity and colour; cone options exist on spot lights only. Shadows need 3D layers with Casts Shadows on (set-material-options). Not verified against a live After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Key"}, lightType "spot", intensity 120, coneAngle 60, castsShadows true.

Pack: camera-3d. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setLightSettings`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `lightType` | "parallel" \| "spot" \| "point" \| "ambient" | no | Light type. |
| `intensity` | number | no | Intensity in percent. 100 is normal; negative values subtract light. |
| `color` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Light colour. |
| `coneAngle` | number | no | Spot cone angle in degrees. |
| `coneFeather` | number | no | Spot cone feather in percent. |
| `falloff` | "none" \| "smooth" \| "inverse-square" \| integer | no | Falloff type: none (1), smooth (2) or inverse-square (3). |
| `radius` | number | no | Falloff start radius in pixels. |
| `falloffDistance` | number | no | Falloff distance in pixels. |
| `castsShadows` | boolean | no | Whether the light casts shadows. |
| `shadowDarkness` | number | no | Shadow darkness in percent. |
| `shadowDiffusion` | number | no | Shadow diffusion in pixels. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing for the keyframes written when time or frame is given. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-light-settings",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-material-options

Sets the Material Options of a 3D image layer: how it responds to lights and shadows. castsShadows off, on or only; lightTransmission percent; acceptsShadows off, on or only; acceptsLights; appearsInReflections; and the surface coefficients ambient, diffuse, specularIntensity, specularShininess and metal in percent. Use when: a layer should cast or receive shadows, or should ignore scene lights. Do not use for: the light itself (set-light-settings) or the 3D switch alone (set-layer-3d). Inputs: layer (a 3D image layer; the 3D switch is turned on when off unless enable3d false); the option fields. Returns: the layer summary, the current material options, what was applied with property paths, and notes for options the renderer does not expose. Notes: shadows only render when a light has castsShadows on and the renderer is Classic 3D or Cinema 4D; appearsInReflections belongs to the ray-traced renderer. Cameras and lights have no material options. Not verified against a live After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Card"}, castsShadows "on", acceptsShadows "on", diffuse 60.

Pack: camera-3d. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setMaterialOptions`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `enable3d` | boolean | no | Turn the 3D switch on when the layer is not 3D. Default true. |
| `castsShadows` | boolean \| "off" \| "on" \| "only" | no | true/false, or "off", "on", "only" (only means the layer is invisible and only its shadow or reflection shows). |
| `lightTransmission` | number | no | Percent of light that passes through the layer. |
| `acceptsShadows` | boolean \| "off" \| "on" \| "only" | no | true/false, or "off", "on", "only" (only means the layer is invisible and only its shadow or reflection shows). |
| `acceptsLights` | boolean | no |  |
| `appearsInReflections` | boolean \| "off" \| "on" \| "only" | no | true/false, or "off", "on", "only" (only means the layer is invisible and only its shadow or reflection shows). |
| `ambient` | number | no | Percent. |
| `diffuse` | number | no | Percent. |
| `specularIntensity` | number | no | Percent. |
| `specularShininess` | number | no | Percent. |
| `metal` | number | no | Percent. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-material-options",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

## Time

### enable-time-remap

Turns time remapping on or off for a layer. Time remapping adds a Time Remap property whose value is the source time shown at each composition time; After Effects seeds it with two keys (0 at the layer start, source duration at the layer end). Use when: before speed ramps, freeze frames or loops that need Time Remap keys, or to remove a remap. Do not use for: writing the keys (set-time-remap-keyframes), a simple constant speed change (set-speed) or a hold (freeze-frame-at). Inputs: layer; enabled (default true). Returns: enabled, whether anything changed, the layer summary, timing, the source duration and the Time Remap property with its keyframes. Notes: only layers with a time-based source (footage, precomps, audio) can be remapped; solids, shapes, text, nulls, cameras and lights fail with code unsupported. Disabling deletes the keys. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Clip"}, enabled true.

Pack: time. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `enableTimeRemap`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `enabled` | boolean | no | Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
```
