```json
  "tool": "enable-time-remap",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### freeze-frame-at

Holds one source frame for the whole layer: enables time remapping, removes every Time Remap key and writes a single hold key whose value is the source time visible at the given composition time. Use when: a still from a clip, a frozen last frame, or a reference frame to build on. Do not use for: a hold in the middle of a moving clip (use set-time-remap-keyframes with two keys of the same value) or splitting (split-layer-at-time). Inputs: layer; time or frame (default: current time), the moment whose frame is frozen; keyAtTime true places the key at that time instead of the layer in point; outPoint or outFrame, or extendToCompEnd, to lengthen the layer. Returns: frozenAt, sourceTime and sourceFrame, the hold keyframe, the layer timing and the Time Remap property state. Notes: the source time takes the layer start, trims and stretch into account. Layers without a time-based source fail with code unsupported. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Clip"}, frame 48, extendToCompEnd true.

Pack: time. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `freezeFrameAt`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `keyAtTime` | boolean | no | Place the hold key at the given time rather than the layer in point. Default false. |
| `outPoint` | number | no | New out point in seconds. |
| `outFrame` | integer | no | New out point in frames; wins over outPoint. |
| `extendToCompEnd` | boolean | no | Set the out point to the composition end. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "freeze-frame-at",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### loop-layer

Makes a layer repeat its source: enables time remapping, pulls the last Time Remap key one frame earlier so the final frame is not shown twice at the seam, sets a loopOut expression (cycle, pingpong, offset or continue), and extends the layer out point to the composition end or a given length. Use when: a short clip or precomp must fill a longer composition. Do not use for: repeating keyframed animation on a property (set-expression with loopOut on that property) or looping footage in the project item settings. Inputs: layer; mode (default cycle); fixLastFrame (default true); loopBefore true also loops before the first key; outPoint or outFrame, or duration or durationFrames, instead of the composition end. Returns: mode, the expression and any expression error, lastKeyFixed, the layer timing and the Time Remap property with its keyframes. Notes: pingpong plays forward then backward. The layer must have a time-based source. Not verified against a live After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Loop clip"}, mode "cycle".

Pack: time. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `loopLayer`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `mode` | "cycle" \| "pingpong" \| "offset" \| "continue" | no | Default "cycle". |
| `fixLastFrame` | boolean | no | Move the last key one frame earlier to avoid a duplicated frame. Default true. |
| `loopBefore` | boolean | no | Also loop before the first key with loopIn. Default false. |
| `outPoint` | number | no | Out point in seconds. Default: composition end. |
| `outFrame` | integer | no | Out point in frames; wins over outPoint. |
| `duration` | number | no | Length from the in point in seconds. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Length in frames; wins over duration. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "loop-layer",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### reverse-layer

Plays a layer backwards by negating its time stretch, then puts the layer back on the same time range (After Effects shifts a layer when stretch turns negative). Calling it twice restores forward playback. Use when: a reveal should play as a hide, or footage should run backwards. Do not use for: reversing only some keyframes (reverse-keyframes) or partial rewinds (set-time-remap-keyframes). Inputs: layer. Returns: the new stretch, reversed true or false, before and after timing, and notes when the out point could not be kept exactly. Notes: keyframes on the layer are reversed too. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Wipe"}.

Pack: time. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `reverseLayer`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "reverse-layer",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-frame-blending

Sets frame blending on a footage or precomp layer: none, frame-mix (cross-dissolves between frames, fast) or pixel-motion (interpolates motion, slow but smooth), and turns the composition's frame blending switch on so it renders. Use when: slowed footage stutters, or footage at a different frame rate looks steppy. Do not use for: motion blur on animation (set-layer-flags with motionBlur). Inputs: layer; mode (default frame-mix); compSwitch to force the composition switch on or off (default: on when mode is not none, untouched otherwise). Returns: the layer summary, the layer frame blending mode, the layer and composition switches. Notes: pixel-motion slows previews and renders considerably. Layers without a footage or precomp source fail with code unsupported. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Clip"}, mode "pixel-motion".

Pack: time. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setFrameBlending`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `mode` | "none" \| "frame-mix" \| "pixel-motion" | no | Default "frame-mix". |
| `compSwitch` | boolean | no | Composition frame blending switch. Default: true when mode is not none. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-frame-blending",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-speed

Changes a layer's playback speed through time stretch: give a stretch percent (100 normal, 200 half speed, 50 double), a speedFactor (2 doubles the speed), or fitToDuration or fitToDurationFrames to make the visible part last exactly that long. Use when: slowing or speeding a clip uniformly, or fitting a clip to a slot. Do not use for: speed ramps (set-time-remap-keyframes) or reversing (reverse-layer, which keeps the time range). Inputs: layer; one of percent, speedFactor, fitToDuration or fitToDurationFrames; keepInPoint (default true) keeps the in point where it is, so only the out point moves. Returns: mode, the new stretch and speedFactor, before and after timing (start, in, out, duration, stretch) and notes when After Effects clamped a value. Notes: a negative current stretch stays negative. Keyframes on the layer are stretched with it. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Clip"}, speedFactor 0.5, keepInPoint true.

Pack: time. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setSpeed`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `percent` | number | no | Time stretch percent. 100 normal, 200 half speed, 50 double speed. |
| `speedFactor` | number | no | Speed multiplier. 2 is double speed (stretch 50). |
| `fitToDuration` | number | no | Make the visible part last this many seconds. |
| `fitToDurationFrames` | integer | no | Make the visible part last this many frames; wins over fitToDuration. |
| `keepInPoint` | boolean | no | Keep the in point fixed. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-speed",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-time-remap-keyframes

Writes Time Remap keyframes on a layer: each key pairs a composition time with the source time to show there, so a speed ramp, slow motion, a rewind or a jump cut is a short list of keys. Enables time remapping first when needed. Use when: variable speed, playing a clip backwards over a range, or holding then resuming. Do not use for: a constant speed (set-speed), a single hold frame (freeze-frame-at) or seamless loops (loop-layer). Inputs: layer; keys[] of {time or frame, value in source seconds or valueFrame, easing}; easing default for keys without their own; replace (default: true when remap was just enabled, otherwise false) removes existing keys first. Returns: keyframesWritten, the keys written with time, frame and value, the layer timing and the Time Remap property state. Notes: the layer out point is not extended; use set-layer-timing to lengthen a slowed clip. valueFrame uses the composition frame rate. Undoable in one step. Example: keys [{frame: 0, value: 0}, {frame: 30, value: 1}, {frame: 90, value: 1.5, easing: "ease-in-out"}] for a slow-down.

Pack: time. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setTimeRemapKeyframes`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `keys` | {time?: number, frame?: integer, value?: number, valueFrame?: integer, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object}[] | yes |  |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Default easing for keys without their own. |
| `replace` | boolean | no | Remove existing Time Remap keys first. Default true right after enabling, otherwise false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-time-remap-keyframes",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "keys": [
      {}
    ]
  }
}
```

## Markers and audio

### add-marker

Adds one marker to a composition or a layer at a time or frame, with comment, duration, label colour, chapter, URL, frame target, cue point name and parameters, and protected region. Use when: marking a beat, a section, or a note for the human. Do not use for: many markers (add-markers-bulk) or beat grids (markers-from-beats). Inputs: target "comp" (default when no layer is given) or "layer" with layer; time or frame (default: current time); the marker fields. Returns: the marker as written and the new marker count. Notes: a second marker at exactly the same time replaces the first. Undoable in one step. Example: target "comp", frame 48, comment "Logo in", label "green".

Pack: markers-audio. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addMarker`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `target` | "comp" \| "layer" | no |  |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `comment` | string | no | Marker text. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. 0 is a point marker. |
| `label` | "none" \| "red" \| "yellow" \| "aqua" \| "pink" \| "lavender" \| "peach" \| "sea-foam" \| "blue" \| "green" \| "purple" \| "orange" \| "brown" \| "fuchsia" \| "cyan" \| "sandstone" \| "dark-green" \| integer | no |  |
| `chapter` | string | no | Chapter name for the marker. |
| `url` | string | no | Web link for the marker. |
| `frameTarget` | string | no | Frame target for the URL. |
| `cuePointName` | string | no | Cue point name for the marker. |
| `protectedRegion` | boolean | no | Protected region marker (After Effects 2020 and later). |
| `params` | object of string | no | Cue point key/value pairs. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-marker",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### add-markers-bulk

Adds many markers to a composition or a layer in one round-trip. Combine with analyze-audio-waveform peaks to mark transients. Use when: placing more than one marker. Do not use for: a single marker (add-marker) or evenly spaced beats (markers-from-beats). Inputs: target and layer as in add-marker; markers[] each with time or frame plus the marker fields; clearExisting to remove current markers first. Returns: addedCount, errorCount, the markers added and any per-marker errors. Notes: markers at the same time replace each other. Undoable in one step. Example: markers = peakTimes mapped to {time, comment: "hit"}.

Pack: markers-audio. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addMarkersBulk`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `target` | "comp" \| "layer" | no |  |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `markers` | {time?: number, frame?: integer, comment?: string, duration?: number, label?: "none" \| "red" \| "yellow" \| "aqua" \| "pink" \| "lavender" \| "peach" \| "sea-foam" \| "blue" \| "green" \| "purple" \| "orange" \| "brown" \| "fuchsia" \| "cyan" \| "sandstone" \| "dark-green" \| integer, chapter?: string, url?: string, frameTarget?: string, cuePointName?: string, protectedRegion?: boolean, params?: object of string}[] | yes |  |
| `clearExisting` | boolean | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-markers-bulk",
  "arguments": {
    "markers": [
      {}
    ],
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### analyze-audio-waveform

Analyses an audio file on disk and returns an amplitude envelope and detected peak (transient) times. PCM WAV files are read directly; MP3, AAC, M4A, MOV and other formats are decoded through ffmpeg when it is installed on PATH, otherwise the error says so. Runs on the server. Use when: syncing animation to music or a voice track: get the file path from get-audio-info, analyse, then add-markers-bulk or sequence-layers at the peaks. Do not use for: reading levels inside After Effects (get-audio-info). Inputs: filePath; numPoints (default 200, higher for finer timing); peakThreshold 0..1 of the loudest point (default 0.6); minGapSeconds between peaks (default 0.08). Returns: duration, sampleRate, channels, peakCount, peakTimes[], waveformPoints[] of {time, amplitude}. Notes: read-only. Times are relative to the file start; add the layer start time when it does not start at 0. Example: filePath from get-audio-info, numPoints 400, then markers at peakTimes.

Pack: markers-audio. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `filePath` | string | yes | Absolute path to the audio file. |
| `numPoints` | integer | no | Number of envelope samples. Default 200. |
| `peakThreshold` | number | no | Fraction of the loudest point a peak must reach. Default 0.6. |
| `minGapSeconds` | number | no | Minimum time between peaks. Default 0.08. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "analyze-audio-waveform",
  "arguments": {
    "filePath": "/absolute/path/file"
  }
}
```

### audio-to-keyframes

Runs After Effects' own "Convert Audio to Keyframes" on a layer with audio. It creates a null layer named "Audio Amplitude" with three Slider Control effects, Left Channel, Right Channel and Both Channels, keyframed on every frame with that channel's loudness, so other properties can follow the sound through an expression. Use when: scaling a logo to the beat, driving glow intensity or a bar graph from music or a voice track. Do not use for: finding beat times (analyze-audio-waveform) or placing markers (markers-from-beats). Inputs: layer (default: the selected layer); name to rename the new null; useLayerRange true to set the work area to the layer's in and out points during the conversion and restore it after. Returns: the new layer summary, sliders[] with property paths, key counts and peak values, and ready-made expression snippets such as thisComp.layer("Audio Amplitude").effect("Both Channels")("Slider"). Notes: only the composition work area is sampled, so set-work-area first or pass useLayerRange. Slider values are unscaled amplitude, typically 0 to 30; map them with linear(). Fails with code unsupported when the menu command is missing. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Music"}, then set-expression on "Transform/Scale" with linear(thisComp.layer("Audio Amplitude").effect("Both Channels")("Slider"), 0, 20, 100, 120).

Pack: markers-audio. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `audioToKeyframes`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer with audio. Default: the layer selected in the timeline. |
| `name` | string | no | Rename the created "Audio Amplitude" null. |
| `useLayerRange` | boolean | no | Temporarily set the work area to the layer's in and out points. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "audio-to-keyframes",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### clear-markers

Removes every marker from a composition or from one layer in one call. Use when: regenerating a beat grid with markers-from-beats or add-markers-bulk, or clearing markers a script left behind. Do not use for: one marker (delete-marker) or keyframes (clear-keyframes). Inputs: target "comp" (default when no layer is given) or "layer" with a layer reference. Returns: how many markers were removed, plus the composition and layer touched. Notes: comment text on the markers is lost; undo brings them all back in one step. Markers on other layers are not affected. Example: target "layer", layer {name: "Music"}.

Pack: markers-audio. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `clearMarkers`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `target` | "comp" \| "layer" | no |  |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "clear-markers",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### delete-marker

Deletes one marker from a composition or a layer, by index or by time. Use when: removing a wrong marker. Do not use for: all markers (clear-markers). Inputs: target and layer as in add-marker; index (1-based) or time or frame (matched within half a frame). Returns: removed count and the markers remaining. Notes: undoable in one step. Example: target "comp", frame 48.

Pack: markers-audio. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `deleteMarker`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `target` | "comp" \| "layer" | no |  |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `index` | integer | no | 1-based marker index from list-markers. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "delete-marker",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### get-audio-info

Reads a layer's audio facts: whether it has audio and it is enabled, in and out points, the source file path (needed by analyze-audio-waveform), channels, sample rate, duration, current Audio Levels with keyframes, and the layer markers. Use when: before analyzing a soundtrack or setting levels. Do not use for: the waveform itself (analyze-audio-waveform). Inputs: layer. Returns: hasAudio, audioEnabled, source, sourceFilePath, audioLevels, markers. Notes: read-only. Example: layer {name: "Music"} then analyze-audio-waveform with sourceFilePath.

Pack: markers-audio. Read-only. Bridge command: `getAudioInfo`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "get-audio-info",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### list-markers

Lists composition markers and layer markers with index, time, frame, comment, duration, label, chapter, URL and cue point data. Use when: reading beat markers a human placed, or verifying add-markers-bulk. Do not use for: keyframes (get-keyframes). Inputs: comp (optional); layer to list one layer only; includeLayers (default true) to include every layer that has markers. Returns: compMarkers[] and layers[] with their markers[]. Notes: read-only. Example: list-markers, then use the times to sequence layers.

Pack: markers-audio. Read-only. Bridge command: `listMarkers`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `includeLayers` | boolean | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-markers",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### markers-from-beats

Places a marker on every beat of a composition or layer. Either give a tempo in bpm (with offset, beatsPerBar, subdivisions and a start and end range) to build an even grid whose comments count the beat in the bar and whose downbeats get a label colour, or pass peakTimes[] from analyze-audio-waveform to mark detected hits. Use when: cutting or animating to music, or turning detected transients into visible markers. Do not use for: arbitrary marker lists (add-markers-bulk) or reading markers (list-markers). Inputs: target "comp" (default) or "layer" with layer; bpm; offset in seconds added to every time (default 0); beatsPerBar (default 4); subdivisions per beat (default 1); start and end in seconds or startFrame and endFrame (default: the whole composition, or the work area with useWorkArea true); labelDownbeats (default true) with downbeatLabel (default "red"); peakTimes[]; comment for peak markers; snapToFrames (default true); clearExisting. Returns: mode, bpm and beatInterval, count, first and last time and frame, and the marker count after writing. Notes: capped at 5000 markers. Markers at the same time replace each other. Undoable in one step. Example: bpm 123.5, offset 0.12, beatsPerBar 4, clearExisting true.

Pack: markers-audio. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `markersFromBeats`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `target` | "comp" \| "layer" | no |  |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `bpm` | number | no | Tempo in beats per minute. |
| `offset` | number | no | Seconds added to every marker time. Default 0. |
| `beatsPerBar` | integer | no | Beats per bar for numbering and downbeats. Default 4. |
| `subdivisions` | integer | no | Markers per beat. Default 1. |
| `start` | number | no | Range start in seconds. Default 0. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Range start in frames; wins over start. |
| `end` | number | no | Range end in seconds. Default: composition end. |
| `endFrame` | integer | no | Range end in frames; wins over end. |
| `useWorkArea` | boolean | no | Use the work area as the range when start and end are not given. Default false. |
| `labelDownbeats` | boolean | no | Colour the first beat of each bar. Default true. |
| `downbeatLabel` | "none" \| "red" \| "yellow" \| "aqua" \| "pink" \| "lavender" \| "peach" \| "sea-foam" \| "blue" \| "green" \| "purple" \| "orange" \| "brown" \| "fuchsia" \| "cyan" \| "sandstone" \| "dark-green" \| integer | no | Label colour for downbeats. Default "red". |
| `peakTimes` | number[] | no | Times in seconds from analyze-audio-waveform. Used instead of bpm. |
| `comment` | string | no | Comment for peak markers. Default: the peak number. |
| `snapToFrames` | boolean | no | Round times to whole frames. Default true. |
| `clearExisting` | boolean | no | Remove existing markers on the target first. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "markers-from-beats",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-audio-levels

Sets a layer's Audio Levels in dB, for both channels or each separately, as a static value or as a keyframe at a time with easing (for fades). Use when: ducking music under a voice, muting, or fading out. Do not use for: audio-driven animation (audio-to-keyframes). Inputs: layer; level (both channels) or leftLevel and rightLevel in dB (0 unity, -6 half, -48 near silent); time or frame to keyframe; easing for that key. Returns: the Audio Levels property state and the values set. Notes: two calls with times make a fade. Undoable in one step. Example: level -12 at frame 0, then level -48 at frame 24 with easing "ease-in".

Pack: markers-audio. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setAudioLevels`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `level` | number | no | Level in dB for both channels. 0 unity, -6 half, -48 near silence. |
| `leftLevel` | number | no | Left channel level in dB; overrides level. |
| `rightLevel` | number | no | Right channel level in dB; overrides level. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-audio-levels",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

## Render and preview

### add-to-render-queue

Adds a composition to the render queue with an output path and optional templates, the same as Composition > Add to Render Queue followed by setting the output file. Nothing renders until you call render. Use when: preparing a movie or image sequence export. Do not use for: single stills (export-frame-png) or looking at a frame (see-frame). Inputs: comp; outputPath, an absolute file path (~ expanded; for image sequences include a frame pattern such as name_[#####].png); outputModuleTemplate, a name from list-output-module-templates (default the current default template); renderSettingsTemplate from list-render-settings-templates; useWorkArea (default true) renders the work area, or start and end in seconds; skipExisting to skip frames already on disk. Returns: itemIndex (1-based position in the queue), comp, outputPath, status (QUEUED, NEEDS_OUTPUT and so on), timeSpanStart, timeSpanDuration and the output modules. Notes: the file extension comes from the output module template, so pass a path that matches it (for example .mov for a QuickTime template). The item stays in the queue until clear-render-queue. Undoable in one step. Unverified against a real After Effects in this build. Example: comp {name: "Main"}, outputPath "~/Movies/main.mov", outputModuleTemplate "H.264 - Match Render Settings - 15 Mbps".

Pack: render. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addToRenderQueue`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `outputPath` | string | yes | Absolute output file path. A leading ~ is expanded. Sequences may use a [#####] frame pattern. |
| `outputModuleTemplate` | string | no | Output module template name. See list-output-module-templates. |
| `renderSettingsTemplate` | string | no | Render settings template name. See list-render-settings-templates. |
| `useWorkArea` | boolean | no | Render the work area. Default true. Ignored when start or end is given. |
| `start` | number | no | Range start in seconds. |
| `end` | number | no | Range end in seconds. |
| `skipExisting` | boolean | no | Skip frames whose files already exist. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-to-render-queue",
  "arguments": {
    "outputPath": "/absolute/path/file"
  }
}
```

### clear-render-queue

Removes items from the render queue: all of them, or only the finished and unqueued ones when onlyFinished is true. Use when: starting a clean export, or tidying after a render. Do not use for: stopping a render in progress (not possible from the bridge; the human must stop it in After Effects). Inputs: onlyFinished (default false) keeps items whose status is QUEUED, RENDERING, NEEDS_OUTPUT or WILL_CONTINUE and removes DONE, UNQUEUED, ERR_STOPPED and USER_STOPPED ones. Returns: removed count and remaining count. Notes: fails while a render is running. Undoable in one step. Example: onlyFinished true after render, then list-render-queue to confirm the queue is empty.

Pack: render. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `clearRenderQueue`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `onlyFinished` | boolean | no | Remove only finished or unqueued items. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "clear-render-queue",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### export-frame-png

Saves one frame of a composition as a PNG file at a path the human chooses. Nothing is returned as an image; call see-frame when you want to look at it yourself. Use when: the human asks for a still, a thumbnail or a poster frame on disk. Do not use for: checking your own work (see-frame) or many frames (export-still-sequence). Inputs: comp; time in seconds or frame (default the current time); outputPath, an absolute path ending in .png (a leading ~ is expanded, folders are created); scale 0.1 to 1 (optional) downsizes the file on the server after the full-size capture. Returns: file, width, height, time, frame, the composition reference and the capture method. Notes: the file is written at full composition size by After Effects first and only then downsized, so a scale below 1 costs a little extra time. An existing file at the path is replaced. Example: outputPath "~/Desktop/title-frame.png", frame 45.

Pack: render. Read-only. Bridge command: `exportFramePng`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `outputPath` | string | yes | Absolute path for the PNG. A leading ~ is expanded. |
| `scale` | number | no | Optional downscale factor applied on the server, 0.1 to 1. Default 1 (full size). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "export-frame-png",
  "arguments": {
    "outputPath": "/absolute/path/file"
  }
}
```

### export-still-sequence

Saves many frames of a composition as numbered PNG files in a folder, one file per frame, without touching the render queue. Use when: the human wants an image sequence, storyboard stills or every Nth frame on disk. Do not use for: a movie file (add-to-render-queue then render) or checking your own work (see-frames). Inputs: comp; outputDir (absolute, ~ expanded, created if missing); filePrefix (default the composition name); which frames: start and end in seconds (default the whole composition, or the work area with useWorkArea true) for every frame in that range, count for evenly spaced frames in that range, or explicit frames[] or times[]; format only "png"; scale 0.1 to 1 (optional, applied on the server); maxFrames cap (default 300) and allowLarge true to go past it. Returns: outputDir, count, method and files[] with index, frame, time and file, named <prefix>_<frame padded to 5 digits>.png. Notes: each frame is a separate capture, so a 300 frame sequence can take minutes; the server waits with the render timeout. Existing files with the same names are replaced. Example: outputDir "~/Desktop/stills", count 12, useWorkArea true.

Pack: render. Read-only. Bridge command: `exportStillSequence`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `outputDir` | string | yes | Folder for the PNG files. A leading ~ is expanded. Created if missing. |
| `filePrefix` | string | no | File name prefix. Default the composition name with unsafe characters replaced. |
| `start` | number | no | Range start in seconds. Default 0 or the work area start. |
| `end` | number | no | Range end in seconds. Default the composition end or the work area end. |
| `useWorkArea` | boolean | no | Use the work area as the default range. Default false. |
| `count` | integer | no | Evenly spaced frames within the range instead of every frame. |
| `frames` | integer[] | no | Explicit frame numbers. Wins over times and the range. |
| `times` | number[] | no | Explicit times in seconds. |
| `format` | "png" | no | Only "png" is supported. |
| `scale` | number | no | Optional downscale factor applied on the server, 0.1 to 1. |
| `maxFrames` | integer | no | Refuse to export more frames than this. Default 300. |
| `allowLarge` | boolean | no | Lift the maxFrames cap. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "export-still-sequence",
  "arguments": {
    "outputDir": "value"
  }
}
```

### list-output-module-templates

Lists the output module template names (file format and codec presets such as "Lossless", "H.264 - Match Render Settings - 15 Mbps" or "PNG Sequence") and the render settings template names available in this After Effects, for use with add-to-render-queue. Use when: choosing a format before add-to-render-queue, or when applying a template failed and you need the exact spelling. Do not use for: effect templates (list-effect-templates). Inputs: comp (optional). Returns: outputModuleTemplates[], renderSettingsTemplates[], the composition used and currentOutputModule, the template a new item gets by default. Notes: read-only in effect, but After Effects only exposes templates on a render queue item, so the command adds a temporary item for the given composition (or the active one, or the first in the project), reads the lists and removes the item again. Names starting with _HIDDEN are left out. Example: call it once, then add-to-render-queue with outputModuleTemplate "PNG Sequence".

Pack: render. Read-only. Bridge command: `listOutputModuleTemplates`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-output-module-templates",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### list-render-queue

Lists every item in the render queue with its composition, status, output paths, templates and time span, so you can see what is waiting, done or failed. Use when: checking what add-to-render-queue produced, finding items to remove, or reading the result of a render that timed out. Do not use for: template names (list-output-module-templates, list-render-settings-templates). Inputs: none. Returns: count, rendering (true while After Effects renders), items[] with index, comp, status (one of QUEUED, UNQUEUED, RENDERING, DONE, ERR_STOPPED, USER_STOPPED, NEEDS_OUTPUT, WILL_CONTINUE), render flag, timeSpanStart, timeSpanDuration, elapsedSeconds, outputPaths[] and outputModules[] with file and template. Notes: read-only. NEEDS_OUTPUT means the item has no output file set. Indices shift when items are removed. Example: call it after render to collect outputPaths of the DONE items.

Pack: render. Read-only. Bridge command: `listRenderQueue`.

Inputs: none.

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-render-queue",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### list-render-settings-templates

Lists the render settings template names (quality and resolution presets such as "Best Settings", "Draft Settings" or "Current Settings") for use as renderSettingsTemplate in add-to-render-queue. Use when: you want a draft or a full quality render and need the exact template name. Do not use for: output formats (list-output-module-templates). Inputs: comp (optional). Returns: renderSettingsTemplates[] and the composition used. Notes: After Effects only exposes templates on a render queue item, so a temporary item is added for the composition and removed right after. Names starting with _HIDDEN are left out. Example: renderSettingsTemplate "Draft Settings" for a quick check render.

Pack: render. Read-only. Bridge command: `listRenderSettingsTemplates`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-render-settings-templates",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### live-view-settings

Reads or changes the AE MCP Live View plug-in settings and reports whether it is running. Use when: see-live says live view is off, a picture is too small or too slow, or the human wants live view paused while they work. Inputs (all optional; none = read only): enabled; scale 1, 2 or 4 (full, half, quarter size of the full-size picture); maxFps (0.2 to 30, new pictures per second); budgetMs (50 to 1500: a full-size frame expected to take longer stays at the quick size, which keeps After Effects responsive); settleMs (0 to 5000: wait this long after the last change, so nothing is drawn during playback); quality (JPEG, 30 to 95). Returns: the settings now in effect, the plug-in state (on, off, stale = After Effects busy or closed, missing = not installed) and its last status. Notes: the plug-in re-reads the file every 2 seconds. Example: { scale: 1 } for full-size pictures of a small comp.

Pack: render. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `enabled` | boolean | no | Turn live view on or off. |
| `scale` | 1 \| 2 \| 4 | no | Size of the full picture: 1 full, 2 half, 4 quarter. Default 2. |
| `maxFps` | number | no | At most this many new pictures per second. Default 4. |
| `budgetMs` | number | no | A full-size frame expected to take longer stays at the quick size. Default 400. |
| `settleMs` | number | no | Wait this long after the last change before drawing. Default 250. |
| `quality` | integer | no | JPEG quality. Default 80. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "live-view-settings",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### render

Starts rendering the queued render queue items. By default After Effects renders inside the application and this call blocks until every queued item is done, then returns per-item status, elapsed time and output files. With useAerender true the server instead launches the aerender command line tool on the saved project file and returns at once with a process id and a log file path. Use when: the queue is prepared with add-to-render-queue. Do not use for: stills (export-frame-png) or previews (see-frame). Inputs: itemIndices[] to render only some items (others are unqueued for this run); useAerender (default false); with aerender: comp name (required), outputPath (required, ~ expanded), outputModuleTemplate, renderSettingsTemplate, aerenderPath to point at the binary when it is not found automatically. Returns: in-app: count, done, failed, elapsedSeconds, outputFiles[] and items[]. aerender: pid, logPath, binary and args. Notes: the in-app render blocks the panel, so nothing else can run until it finishes; if this call times out the render continues, and get-results with the returned id, or list-render-queue, shows the outcome later. aerender needs the project saved with no unsaved changes (save-project first) and there is no polling tool; tell the human where the log is. Unverified against a real After Effects in this build. Example: add-to-render-queue, then render with no arguments and wait.

Pack: render. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `render`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `itemIndices` | integer[] | no | 1-based render queue indices to render. Default: every queued item. |
| `useAerender` | boolean | no | Spawn the aerender command line tool instead of rendering inside After Effects. Default false. |
| `comp` | string | no | aerender only: composition name to render. |
| `outputPath` | string | no | aerender only: output file path. A leading ~ is expanded. |
| `outputModuleTemplate` | string | no | aerender only: output module template name (-OMtemplate). |
| `renderSettingsTemplate` | string | no | aerender only: render settings template name (-RStemplate). |
| `aerenderPath` | string | no | aerender only: path to the aerender binary when it is not in the default install folder. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "render",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### see-frame

Renders one frame of a composition and returns it as an image you can look at. This is how you check your own work: after building or changing anything visual, call see-frame at the relevant time and confirm the layers, colours, text and positions are what you intended before you report success to the human. Use when: verifying a change, comparing before and after (call it twice), or describing what a composition looks like. Do not use for: files the human keeps (export-frame-png) or several moments at once (see-frames). Inputs: comp (default the active composition); time in seconds or frame (default the current time); scale 0.1 to 1 (default 0.5, keeps the payload small); maxWidth in pixels (default 1280) as a second cap; setCurrentTime (default true) also moves the playhead there; includeInfo (default true) adds a JSON block with the file path and sizes. Returns: an image content block (PNG) plus text with file, time, frame, width, height and the capture method. Notes: transparent areas are shown over the composition background colour, as in the viewer (flatten false keeps the alpha). The full-size PNG stays in the bridge frames folder until the server cleans it up. Capture uses saveFrameToPng (After Effects 22.3 and later) and falls back to a temporary render queue item on older versions, which is slower. Example: after animating a title, see-frame with time 1.0 and check the title is fully on screen.

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Bridge command: `seeFrame`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `scale` | number | no | Downscale factor applied on the server, 0.1 to 1. Default 0.5. |
| `maxWidth` | integer | no | Cap on the returned image width in pixels. Default 1280. |
| `setCurrentTime` | boolean | no | Move the playhead to the captured time. Default true. |
| `includeInfo` | boolean | no | Add a JSON text block with file and size details. Default true. |
| `flatten` | boolean | no | Show the frame over the composition background colour, as the viewer does. Default true; false keeps transparency. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "see-frame",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### see-frames

Renders several frames of a composition and returns them tiled into one contact-sheet image, so you can check motion over time in a single look. Use when: verifying an animation (does the logo arrive by frame 30, is the fade smooth), or reviewing a whole composition quickly. Do not use for: one moment in detail (see-frame) or files for the human (export-still-sequence). Inputs: comp; count (default 4, evenly spaced over the composition, or the work area when useWorkArea is true) or explicit times[] in seconds or frames[]; columns (default min(count, 3)); scale 0.1 to 1 per tile (default 0.33); maxWidth for the whole sheet (default 1600); label (default true) draws a one pixel white border around each tile. At most 25 frames per call. Returns: one PNG image content block plus text with tiles[] mapping each tile index to its time, frame and pixel rectangle {x, y, w, h} in the sheet, reading left to right, top to bottom. Notes: frame numbers are not drawn on the image; use the tiles[] map. Transparent areas are shown over the composition background colour (flatten false keeps the alpha). Each frame is captured full size first, so 25 frames of a 4K composition takes a while. Example: count 6, columns 3, useWorkArea true, then compare tile 0 with tile 5.

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Bridge command: `seeFrames`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `count` | integer | no | How many frames, evenly spaced across the composition (or the work area when useWorkArea is true). Default 4. Ignored when times or frames is given. |
| `times` | number[] | no | Explicit times in seconds. |
| `frames` | integer[] | no | Explicit frame numbers. Wins over times. |
| `useWorkArea` | boolean | no | Space count frames across the work area instead of the whole composition. Default false. |
| `columns` | integer | no | Tiles per row. Default min(count, 3). |
| `scale` | number | no | Downscale factor per tile, 0.1 to 1. Default 0.33. |
| `maxWidth` | integer | no | Cap on the whole sheet width in pixels. Default 1600. |
| `label` | boolean | no | Draw a one pixel border around each tile. Default true. |
| `flatten` | boolean | no | Show frames over the composition background colour, as the viewer does. Default true; false keeps transparency. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "see-frames",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### see-live

Returns the current picture of the active composition as kept by the AE MCP Live View plug-in, the same frame the human sees at the playhead. It costs After Effects nothing: the plug-in draws the picture whenever the comp settles after a change, and this tool only reads the file. Use when: glancing at what the human is working on, checking the result of a change without moving the playhead, or watching the comp while another script runs. Do not use for: a specific time other than the playhead, or exact full-resolution checks (see-frame). Inputs: waitMs (default 1500) waits up to this long for a picture drawn after the last change made through this server, and for the full-size picture when only the quick small one is ready; includeInfo (default true). Returns: a JPEG image content block plus text with comp, time, frame, size, scale, final (false = only the quick picture), renderMs, ageSeconds and afterLastChange (false, with a note, when no newer picture came in time). Notes: needs the plug-in installed and enabled (live-view-settings). Pictures are half size by default. A frame whose full-size render would take longer than budgetMs stays at the quick size. Example: after a batch that restyles a title, see-live to confirm the look at the current time.

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `waitMs` | integer | no | Wait up to this long for the full-size picture. Default 1500. |
| `includeInfo` | boolean | no | Add a JSON text block describing the picture. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "see-live",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

## Rigs

### list-rigs

Describes every rig builder: what it looks like, which layers and effects it creates and in what order, what must already exist in the composition, optional plugins, and every parameter with its default. Use when: deciding whether a rig fits a brief, or checking parameter names and defaults before calling rig-edge-glow, rig-power-warp-transition, rig-depth-map-blur-reveal or rig-blur-color-reveal. Do not use for: the general tool catalogue (use list-tools) or effect presets (use list-presets). Inputs: rig (optional) to return one entry by tool name or rig name. Returns: count and rigs[] with tool, name, panel path, summary, builds[], layerOrder[], requires[], optional[], parameters[] {name, default, description}, fixedValues and returns. Notes: read-only. The data lives in the ExtendScript rig files, which the panels mirror, so it matches what the tools build. Example: call with no arguments to see all four rigs, then call rig-blur-color-reveal with the parameters you need.

Pack: rigs. Read-only. Bridge command: `listRigs`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rig` | string | no | Tool name (rig-edge-glow) or rig name (Edge Glow) to return one entry. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-rigs",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### rig-blur-color-reveal

Builds the blur colour reveal: a soft-edged disc with a pale core and a coloured ring grows from a point (scale 0 to 120 percent, eased) over the content and wipes it into view. Two blurred ellipse layers are added, the colour one clipped by the other as an alpha matte. Use when: revealing a card, dashboard or footage with a colour bloom. Do not use for: a mask wipe (use mask-reveal-animation) or a plain fade. Inputs: comp (active by default); layers (one LayerRef or an array, default the top layer); precompose true (wraps the content into "BR Content"); core [251,248,149]; ring [173,215,255]; ringWidth 200 px (0 for a single colour); blur 80; sizeMultiplier 1.5; endScale 120; start 0 s; duration 1.3 s; ease 33; origin [x,y] (default the comp centre); addControlNull false; nullPush false; nullFrom 80; nullTo 90; openInViewer true. Returns: the content layer, the precomposed comp ref, colorLayer, matteLayer, controlNull, the window and notes. Notes: layers BR Reveal Matte and BR Reveal Color are added above the content. The composition needs at least one layer. Same logic as the Blur Color Reveal panel. One undo step. Not verified against a real After Effects in this build. Example: layers [{name:"Dashboard"}], core "#fbf895", ring "#add7ff", start 0.5.

Pack: rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `rigBlurColorReveal`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} \| object[] | no | The content to reveal: one LayerRef or an array. Default: the top layer. |
| `precompose` | boolean | no | Precompose the content into "BR Content" first. Default true. |
| `core` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Fill colour at the centre of the disc. Default [251,248,149]. |
| `ring` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Stroke colour of the rim. Default [173,215,255]. |
| `ringWidth` | number | no | Stroke thickness in pixels; 0 gives a single-colour disc. Default 200. |
| `blur` | number | no | Fast Box Blur radius, the edge softness. Default 80. |
| `sizeMultiplier` | number | no | Ellipse size as a multiple of the comp size. Default 1.5. |
| `endScale` | number | no | Scale percent the bloom grows to. Default 120. |
| `start` | number | no | Reveal start in seconds. Default 0. |
| `duration` | number | no | Reveal length in seconds. Default 1.3. |
| `ease` | number | no | Keyframe influence 0 to 100. Default 33 (Easy Ease). |
| `origin` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] in comp pixels the disc grows from. Default: the comp centre. |
| `addControlNull` | boolean | no | Parent both ellipses to a null named BR Control. Default false. |
| `nullPush` | boolean | no | Animate the null scale for a subtle push-in (needs addControlNull). Default false. |
| `nullFrom` | number | no | Null scale percent at the start of the push. Default 80. |
| `nullTo` | number | no | Null scale percent at the end of the push. Default 90. |
| `openInViewer` | boolean | no | Open the composition in the viewer after building. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "rig-blur-color-reveal",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### rig-depth-map-blur-reveal

Builds the depth map blur reveal on one layer: the picture starts as a heavy blur weighted by depth and resolves into focus, the far distance sharpening first and the near foreground last, while exposure lifts from dark to bright and a glow bloom fades away. Use when: revealing a landscape, product shot or still with a focus pull feel. Do not use for: a uniform blur-in (use set-keyframes-bulk on "Effects/Gaussian Blur/Blurriness"). Inputs: comp (active by default); layer (required); depthLayer optional with blurSource "auto" (a vertical gradient fakes depth) or "layer"; maxBlur 120; autoDir bottom; invert false; useExposure true with expStart -2 stops; useGlow true with glowStart 2.5, glowEnd 0, glowRadius 90, glowThreshold 50; useScale false with scaleStart 104 percent; start 0 s or startAtPlayhead; duration 0.8 s; ease 33; openInViewer true. Returns: the layer summary with its new effects, depthSource {mode, layer}, blurEffect used, the window and notes. Notes: auto mode adds a hidden guide solid "DBR Depth (auto)" at the top. If Compound Blur is missing it falls back to Gaussian Blur. Same logic as the Depth Map Blur Reveal panel. One undo step. Not verified against a real After Effects in this build. Example: layer {name:"meadow.jpg"}, start 1.0, duration 0.8.

Pack: rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `rigDepthMapBlurReveal`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The image or footage layer to reveal. |
| `depthLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | A grayscale depth map layer (white = near). Used when blurSource is "layer". |
| `blurSource` | "auto" \| "layer" | no | "auto" fakes depth with a gradient; "layer" uses depthLayer. Default "auto", or "layer" when depthLayer is given. |
| `maxBlur` | number | no | Compound Blur Maximum Blur at the start, animates to 0. Default 120. |
| `autoDir` | "bottom" \| "top" \| "left" \| "right" | no | Edge that stays soft longest in auto mode. Default bottom. |
| `invert` | boolean | no | Invert the depth so the other end blurs longest. Default false. |
| `useExposure` | boolean | no | Add the dark-to-bright exposure lift. Default true. |
| `expStart` | number | no | Exposure start in stops, ramps to 0. Default -2. |
| `useGlow` | boolean | no | Add the fading glow bloom. Default true. |
| `glowStart` | number | no | Glow Intensity at the start. Default 2.5. |
| `glowEnd` | number | no | Glow Intensity at the end. Default 0. |
| `glowRadius` | number | no | Glow Radius. Default 90. |
| `glowThreshold` | number | no | Glow Threshold in percent. Default 50. |
| `useScale` | boolean | no | Add a gentle scale settle. Default false. |
| `scaleStart` | number | no | Start scale as a percent of the current scale, settles to 100. Default 104. |
| `start` | number | no | Reveal start in seconds. Default 0. |
| `startAtPlayhead` | boolean | no | Ignore start and begin at the composition current time. Default false. |
| `duration` | number | no | Reveal length in seconds. Default 0.8. |
| `ease` | number | no | Keyframe influence 0 to 100. Default 33 (Easy Ease). |
| `openInViewer` | boolean | no | Open the composition in the viewer after building. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "rig-depth-map-blur-reveal",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### rig-edge-glow

Builds the Edge Glow badge: a rounded rectangle whose thin outline glows with a two-colour gradient, a bright highlight sweeps around that outline over time, the inside is a dark panel, and a soft red, blue and white halo blooms behind it. Use when: you want a glowing pill or badge to put text or an icon on, in one call. Do not use for: a plain glow on an existing layer (use apply-effect-template "glow" or "deep-glow-with-fallback"). Inputs: comp (active by default); width 718, height 142, roundness 28 and strokeWidth 3 in pixels; gradientA and gradientB rim colours; fillColor; halo1, halo2, halo3; haloDistance 100, haloSoftness 525, haloDirection -36 degrees; sweepWidth 88; edgeIntensity 100; animate true with speed 50 degrees per second; addBackground false; openInViewer true. Returns: the stroke, fill, glow and background layer summaries, glowUsed ("Deep Glow (matchName)" or "built-in Glow"), and notes. Notes: three shape layers named EG Stroke, EG Fill and EG Glow are added at the top of the composition. Deep Glow by Plugin Everything is used when installed, otherwise the built-in Glow. Same logic as the Edge Glow panel. One undo step. Not verified against a real After Effects in this build. Example: build the default badge in the active comp, then add a text layer above EG Stroke.

Pack: rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `rigEdgeGlow`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `width` | number | no | Rectangle width in pixels. Default 718. |
| `height` | number | no | Rectangle height in pixels. Default 142. |
| `roundness` | number | no | Corner radius in pixels. Default 28. |
| `strokeWidth` | number | no | Outline thickness in pixels. Default 3. |
| `gradientA` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Rim colour on gradient corners 1 and 3. Default [236,0,200] (pink). |
| `gradientB` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Rim colour on gradient corners 2 and 4. Default [120,40,255] (violet). |
| `fillColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Inner panel colour. Default black. |
| `halo1` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | First drop-shadow halo colour. Default red. |
| `halo2` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Second drop-shadow halo colour. Default blue. |
| `halo3` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Third drop-shadow halo colour. Default white. |
| `haloDistance` | number | no | Drop Shadow distance in pixels. Default 100. |
| `haloSoftness` | number | no | Drop Shadow softness. Default 525. |
| `haloDirection` | number | no | Drop Shadow direction in degrees. Default -36. |
| `sweepWidth` | number | no | CC Light Sweep width. Default 88. |
| `edgeIntensity` | number | no | CC Light Sweep edge intensity. Default 100. |
| `animate` | boolean | no | Animate the sweep with the expression time * speed on Direction. Default true. |
| `speed` | number | no | Sweep speed in degrees per second. Default 50. |
| `addBackground` | boolean | no | Add a black solid named EG Background at the bottom. Default false. |
| `openInViewer` | boolean | no | Open the composition in the viewer after building. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "rig-edge-glow",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### rig-power-warp-transition

Builds the Power Warp transition between two shots using a depth map: the outgoing shot is wiped away from near to far, a glowing scan line sweeps through it, the picture warps and smears, red and green split apart, the frame shakes with depth parallax and fine ripples run across it, all ramping in and out inside the transition window. Use when: you have an outgoing layer and a grayscale depth map of it (white near, black far) in the same composition. Do not use for: a simple cross-dissolve or wipe (use set-keyframes-bulk on Opacity or apply-effect-template "linear-wipe-transition"). Inputs: comp (active by default); outgoing and depth layer references (required, distinct); incoming (optional, distinct); start 0.5 s; duration 1.5 s; intensity 1.0 (0.5 subtle, 2.0 extreme); scanColor [0,90,255]; modules {scanLine, warp, aberration, shake, fine} all true. Returns: the outgoing, depth and incoming layer summaries, every created layer keyed by role, the stack order, the window {start, end, mid} and notes; lines starting with "?" mean a popup fell back to its default. Notes: the depth layer is hidden and the playhead is parked at mid. The rig cannot create a depth map. Same logic as the Power Warp Transition panel. One undo step. Not verified against a real After Effects in this build. Example: outgoing {name:"Shot A"}, depth {name:"Shot A depth"}, incoming {name:"Shot B"}, start 2, duration 1.2.

Pack: rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `rigPowerWarpTransition`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `outgoing` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The shot that warps away. Gets the Gradient Wipe. |
| `depth` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Grayscale depth map of the outgoing shot (white = near, black = far). Must differ from outgoing. |
| `incoming` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | The shot revealed underneath. Optional; must differ from outgoing and depth. |
| `start` | number | no | Transition start in seconds, snapped to a frame. Default 0.5. |
| `duration` | number | no | Transition length in seconds. Default 1.5. |
| `intensity` | number | no | Master distortion strength. Default 1.0. |
| `scanColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour of the glowing scan line. Default [0,90,255]. |
| `modules` | {scanLine?: boolean, warp?: boolean, aberration?: boolean, shake?: boolean, fine?: boolean} | no | Turn individual modules off. Gradient Wipe is always on. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "rig-power-warp-transition",
  "arguments": {
    "outgoing": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "depth": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

## Batch, workflow and utilities

### apply-snapshot

Restores or copies the state captured by snapshot-composition onto a composition: for each snapshot layer it finds the target layer by name (default) or index, then writes transform values, keyframes (with interpolation and ease), expressions, effects (adding missing ones by matchName and setting their properties) and, on request, text. Use when: undoing changes across many layers, moving an animation to a duplicate composition, or copying a look between projects. Do not use for: one property (set-keyframes-bulk) or one effect (copy-effects). Inputs: comp; snapshot (the object from snapshot-composition); matchBy "name" or "index"; what {transform, effects, keyframes, expressions (all default true), text (default false)}. Returns: per-layer applied and unchanged counts, effects added, skipped properties with reasons, layers with no match, and the list of things a snapshot cannot restore. Notes: masks, shape contents, layer order, parenting, layer styles, timing and markers are not restored. Existing keys on a restored property are replaced. Not verified against a live After Effects. Undoable in one step. Example: snapshot from snapshot-composition, matchBy "name", what {effects: false}.

Pack: batch. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `applySnapshot`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `snapshot` | object of any | yes | The object returned by snapshot-composition. |
| `matchBy` | "name" \| "index" | no | How snapshot layers map to target layers. Default "name". |
| `what` | {transform?: boolean, effects?: boolean, keyframes?: boolean, expressions?: boolean, text?: boolean} | no |  |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "apply-snapshot",
  "arguments": {
    "snapshot": {}
  }
}
```

### batch

Runs several tool calls in one round-trip to After Effects, inside one undo group. This is the fastest way to build anything with more than two or three steps: one queue file, one result file, one undo entry. Use when: building a title card, a lower third, a rig, or applying the same change to many layers. Do not use for: tools that return images (see-frame) or Node-only tools (list-presets, analyze-audio-waveform, get-help); those must be called on their own. Inputs: steps, an array of {tool, args, label?} using the same tool names and argument shapes as the individual tools; stopOnError (default true) stops at the first failing step and marks the rest skipped. Returns: per-step status (ok, error, skipped), each step's normal result, timing, and counts. Notes: arguments are validated on the server before anything is sent, so a typo in step 7 fails fast with nothing changed. Layer indices shift as layers are created; prefer names or ids for steps that follow a create step. Example: steps = [create-composition, create-text-layer, set-keyframes-bulk on Opacity, set-keyframes-bulk on Position].

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Runs on the server; not available inside batch. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `steps` | {tool: string, args?: object of any, label?: string}[] | yes |  |
| `stopOnError` | boolean | no | Stop at the first failing step. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "batch",
  "arguments": {
    "steps": [
      {
        "tool": "value"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### find-and-replace-text

Finds text inside every text layer of a composition or the whole project and replaces it, in static Source Text values and in every Source Text keyframe, keeping the styling. Use when: renaming a product across a template, fixing a typo in many titles, or previewing what would change with dryRun. Do not use for: changing one layer (set-text) or layer names (rename-layer). Inputs: scope "comp" or "project"; find; replace (default empty string); caseSensitive (default false); wholeWord (default false); useRegex (default false, then replace may use $1 groups); dryRun (default false) reports matches without writing. Returns: matchedLayers, changedLayers, totalMatches and layers[] with per-value before and after text and the key index when keyframed. Notes: text animators and expressions on Source Text are untouched. Undoable in one step. Example: scope "project", find "Acme", replace "Acme Corp", wholeWord true.

Pack: batch. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `findAndReplaceText`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `scope` | "comp" \| "project" | no | "comp" (default) searches one composition, the given or the active one; "project" searches every composition. |
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `find` | string | yes | Text or pattern to find. |
| `replace` | string | no | Replacement text. Default empty (deletes the match). |
| `caseSensitive` | boolean | no | Default false. |
| `wholeWord` | boolean | no | Match whole words only. Default false. |
| `useRegex` | boolean | no | Treat find as a regular expression. Default false. |
| `dryRun` | boolean | no | Report matches without changing anything. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "find-and-replace-text",
  "arguments": {
    "find": "value"
  }
}
```

### find-layers

Searches layers in one composition or the whole project by name pattern (case-insensitive substring, or "/regex/i"), layer type, an effect they carry, expression text, whether they have expressions, enabled state, label colour and the 3D switch; all filters combine. Use when: locating every layer that uses a font or effect, finding expressions that reference a deleted layer, or gathering targets for a bulk change. Do not use for: listing one composition without filters (list-layers). Inputs: scope "comp" or "project"; namePattern; type; hasEffect (display name or matchName); expressionContains; hasExpressions; enabled; label; threeD; includeExpressions to list expressions on each match; limit (default and maximum 500). Returns: count, truncated, layers[] each with the composition and the layer summary, plus matchedExpressions when expressionContains is used. Notes: read-only. Expressions are scanned to depth 6 under Transform, Effects, Text, Contents, Masks and the option groups. Example: scope "project", hasEffect "ADBE Gaussian Blur 2", enabled true.

Pack: batch. Read-only. Bridge command: `findLayers`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `scope` | "comp" \| "project" | no | "comp" (default) searches one composition, the given or the active one; "project" searches every composition. |
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `namePattern` | string | no | Substring (case-insensitive) or a regular expression written as "/pattern/flags". |
| `type` | "text" \| "shape" \| "solid" \| "footage" \| "precomp" \| "null" \| "adjustment" \| "camera" \| "light" \| "audio" \| "placeholder" \| "av" | no | Layer type. |
| `hasEffect` | string | no | Effect display name or matchName the layer must carry. |
| `expressionContains` | string | no | Substring or "/regex/" that an expression on the layer must contain. |
| `hasExpressions` | boolean | no | true: only layers with at least one expression; false: only layers without. |
| `enabled` | boolean | no | Filter by the video switch. |
| `label` | "none" \| "red" \| "yellow" \| "aqua" \| "pink" \| "lavender" \| "peach" \| "sea-foam" \| "blue" \| "green" \| "purple" \| "orange" \| "brown" \| "fuchsia" \| "cyan" \| "sandstone" \| "dark-green" \| integer | no | Label colour name or index. |
| `threeD` | boolean | no | Filter by the 3D switch. |
| `includeExpressions` | boolean | no | List every expression on each matched layer. Default false. |
| `limit` | integer | no | Maximum results. Default 500. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "find-layers",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### get-ae-version

Returns the After Effects version, build, language, operating system, expression engine and the bridge panel version. Use when: a tool reports "not supported in After Effects <version>" and you want to confirm the version, or before using a feature that only exists in newer releases (per-layer track mattes, the font API, saveFrameToPng). Do not use for: checking whether the bridge is alive (use ping). Inputs: none. Returns: version string such as "24.3", a numeric version, a guessed release year, buildName, buildNumber, language, os, bridgeVersion, protocol, expressionEngine. Notes: read-only. Example: call it once, then avoid list-fonts on versions below 24.

Pack: batch. Read-only. Bridge command: `getAeVersion`.

Inputs: none.

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "get-ae-version",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### get-bridge-status

Reports the health of the file bridge without waiting on After Effects: whether the panel heartbeat is recent, queue length, bridge folder, server and panel versions and whether they match, poll interval, counters and the last command. Use when: diagnosing timeouts, or after reinstalling the panel to confirm the versions agree. Do not use for: a round-trip liveness check (use ping, which actually executes inside After Effects). Inputs: none. Returns: panelAlive (heartbeat younger than 10 s), heartbeatAgeMs, queueLength, bridgeDir, serverVersion, bridgeVersion, versionsMatch, options, and the raw heartbeat. Notes: reads files only; never blocks. If versionsMatch is false, run npm run build and npm run install-bridge, then reopen the panel. Example: a tool timed out; call get-bridge-status and, if panelAlive is false, ask the human to open the panel.

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

Inputs: none.

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "get-bridge-status",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### get-results

Fetches the stored result of an earlier command by its id, or lists the most recent results. Every command the panel executes writes results/res-<id>.json in the bridge folder; a timeout error tells you the id to look up. Use when: a render or long batch timed out and you want its outcome once it finishes, or to review what ran recently. Do not use for: normal calls, which already wait for and return their result. Inputs: id (optional). Without it, limit (default 10) most recent results are listed newest first. Returns: with id, the full result envelope (status, result or error, timing, versions). Without id, a list of {id, command, status, finishedAt, durationMs}. Notes: results older than an hour are removed when the server starts (AE_MCP_RESULT_MAX_AGE_MS). Example: after "render is still running" with id "lq3k9x1a2b", call get-results with that id a minute later.

Pack: batch. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | string | no | Command id from a previous tool result or timeout message. |
| `limit` | integer | no | How many recent results to list when id is omitted. Default 10. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "get-results",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### ping

Checks that After Effects and the MCP Bridge Auto panel are running and answering. Use when: at the start of a session, or when another tool timed out and you want to know whether the bridge is alive before retrying. Do not use for: reading project state (use get-project-info). Inputs: an optional message that is echoed back. Returns: pong, the After Effects version, the bridge panel version and protocol, and the time inside After Effects. Notes: read-only, opens no undo entry of consequence. A timeout here means the panel is closed or scripting file access is off; the error text says which. Example: call ping with no arguments before building anything, and tell the human to open Window > mcp-bridge-auto.jsx if it fails.

Pack: batch. Read-only. Bridge command: `ping`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `message` | string | no | Text to echo back. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "ping",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### redo

Redoes the last undone change in After Effects, the same as Edit > Redo. Use when: you undid one step too many. Do not use for: replaying a tool call; just call the tool again. Inputs: count, how many redo steps (default 1). Returns: how many steps were redone. Notes: redo history is cleared by any new change, so redo right after undo or not at all.
