Example: undo twice, look at the frame, redo once to keep the first change.

Pack: batch. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `redo`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `count` | integer | no | Number of redo steps. Default 1. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "redo",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### relink-fonts

Changes the font of text layers from one PostScript font name to another across a composition or the project, including every Source Text keyframe, and reports where After Effects kept the old font. fromFont may be "*" for every text layer or "missing" for fonts After Effects has substituted. Use when: a template arrives with missing fonts, or the brand font changed. Do not use for: size, colour or tracking (set-text-style) or listing fonts (list-fonts). Inputs: scope "comp" or "project"; fromFont (PostScript name, "*" or "missing"); toFont (PostScript name from list-fonts); dryRun. Returns: matchedLayers, changedCount, changes[] with before and after font per layer and key, and warnings[] for values After Effects did not accept. Notes: "missing" needs After Effects 2023 (23.0) or later and fails with code unsupported before that. Mixed fonts inside one text layer are read as the first character's font. Undoable in one step. Example: scope "project", fromFont "missing", toFont "Inter-Regular".

Pack: batch. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `relinkFonts`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `scope` | "comp" \| "project" | no | "comp" (default) searches one composition, the given or the active one; "project" searches every composition. |
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `fromFont` | string | yes | PostScript name to replace, or "*" for all, or "missing" for substituted fonts. |
| `toFont` | string | yes | PostScript name of the new font. |
| `dryRun` | boolean | no | Report without changing. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "relink-fonts",
  "arguments": {
    "fromFont": "value",
    "toFont": "value"
  }
}
```

### replace-color

Swaps one colour for another across a composition: shape fills and strokes (including keyframed colours), text fill and stroke colours, solid layer colours and, on request, every colour property on effects. Colours match when their RGB distance is within a tolerance. Use when: rebranding a template, fixing an off-by-one colour, or previewing the affected properties with dryRun. Do not use for: a single fill (set-shape-fill) or colour grading (apply-effect-template "tint"). Inputs: comp; from and to in any colour form; tolerance as RGB distance in 0..1 space (default 0.02, 0 exact); includeShapes, includeText, includeSolids (default true); includeEffects (default false); dryRun. Returns: from and to as hex, changedCount and changes[] with the layer, the target kind, property path, key index, before and after. Notes: changing a solid changes every layer that uses that solid item. Alpha is kept. Gradient fills are not touched. Undoable in one step. Example: from "#ff0000", to "#1e90ff", tolerance 0.05, dryRun true.

Pack: batch. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `replaceColor`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `from` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | yes | Colour to find. |
| `to` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | yes | Replacement colour. |
| `tolerance` | number | no | RGB distance in 0..1 space. Default 0.02. |
| `includeShapes` | boolean | no | Shape fills and strokes. Default true. |
| `includeText` | boolean | no | Text fill and stroke. Default true. |
| `includeSolids` | boolean | no | Solid layer colours. Default true. |
| `includeEffects` | boolean | no | Colour properties on effects. Default false. |
| `dryRun` | boolean | no | Report without changing. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "replace-color",
  "arguments": {
    "from": "value",
    "to": "value"
  }
}
```

### run-extendscript

Evaluates an arbitrary ExtendScript string inside After Effects, wrapped in one undo group, and returns whatever the script evaluates to. This can do anything the scripting API can, including deleting the project. It is disabled unless the server was started with AE_MCP_ALLOW_RAW_SCRIPT=1. Use when: no dedicated tool covers what you need and the human has enabled it. Do not use for: anything a listed tool already does; prefer the tool because it validates input, returns structured state and is tested. Inputs: script, ECMAScript 3 only (no let, const, arrow functions, template strings). The last expression is the return value; return plain objects, arrays, numbers or strings. Returns: the value, or a string form of it, plus its type. Notes: errors include the line number inside your script. Undo reverts the whole script. Example: script = "app.project.activeItem.numLayers".

Pack: batch. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `runExtendscript`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `script` | string | yes | ExtendScript (ES3) source. The last evaluated expression is returned. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "run-extendscript",
  "arguments": {
    "script": "wiggle(2, 10)"
  }
}
```

### set-bridge-options

Changes how often the panel inside After Effects checks the queue and how much it logs. Use when: a long batch of small commands feels slow (lower pollMs to 200) or the panel log is too noisy (verbosity 0). Do not use for: server-side timeouts, which come from environment variables (AE_MCP_TIMEOUT_MS). Inputs: pollMs between 100 and 10000 (default 500); verbosity 0 (errors only), 1 (normal) or 2 (debug). Returns: the options now in effect, as confirmed by the panel. Notes: the change is written to bridge-options.json in the bridge folder and survives panel restarts. Very low poll intervals cost After Effects UI responsiveness. Example: before a 200-step build, set pollMs to 200, then restore 500 afterwards.

Pack: batch. Read-only. Bridge command: `setBridgeOptions`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pollMs` | integer | no | Panel poll interval in milliseconds. |
| `verbosity` | integer | no | 0 quiet, 1 normal, 2 debug. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-bridge-options",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### snapshot-composition

Captures a composition as one JSON object: the composition settings, markers, and for every layer its summary, parent, the Transform property tree with values, keyframes (time, value, interpolation, ease) and expressions, each effect with its property tree, the text document for text layers, a mask summary and Time Remap. Use when: saving a state before a risky change, diffing two versions, or copying animation to another composition through apply-snapshot. Do not use for: a single layer (get-layer-details) or rendering (see-frame). Inputs: comp; depth of the property trees (default 3); includeKeyframes (default true); includeEffects (default true); includeText (default true); includeShapes (default false, shape trees are large); maxLayers (default 100). Returns: snapshotVersion, composition, options, layerCount, truncated, markers[], layers[]. Pass the whole object to apply-snapshot as snapshot. Notes: read-only. Masks, shape contents, layer order and styles are described but apply-snapshot cannot restore them. Example: comp {name: "Intro"}, depth 3, includeShapes false.

Pack: batch. Read-only. Bridge command: `snapshotComposition`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `depth` | integer | no | Property tree depth. Default 3. |
| `includeKeyframes` | boolean | no | Default true. |
| `includeEffects` | boolean | no | Default true. |
| `includeText` | boolean | no | Include the text document of text layers. Default true. |
| `includeShapes` | boolean | no | Include the Contents tree of shape layers. Default false. |
| `maxLayers` | integer | no | Stop after this many layers. Default 100. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "snapshot-composition",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### undo

Undoes the last change in After Effects, the same as Edit > Undo. Every tool in this server runs inside one undo group named "MCP: <tool>", so one undo reverts one tool call in full, and one undo reverts a whole batch. Use when: a build step produced the wrong result and you want to revert it cleanly before trying again, instead of deleting layers by hand. Do not use for: reverting the human's own edits (ask first). Inputs: count, how many undo steps (default 1). Returns: how many steps were undone. Notes: undo history is shared with the human; check list-layers after undoing so your picture of the comp stays right. Example: rig-edge-glow built with the wrong colours, call undo once, then rebuild.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `undo`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `count` | integer | no | Number of undo steps. Default 1. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "undo",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

## AE guard: status, dialogs, panel

### dismiss-ae-dialog

Closes a modal dialog that blocks After Effects, without activating it or moving the mouse: presses a named button through Accessibility, or posts Escape to the After Effects process only. Refuses when After Effects is the front app (the dialog may be the human's), and never presses Reset, Safe Mode, Disable, Don't Save, Delete, Quit, Remove, Replace, Revert or Discard. Use when: get-ae-status says dialog and the dialog is one a script caused (script error, warning, Crash Repair "Continue"). Do not use for: anything when unsure what the dialog is; read it with get-ae-status first. Inputs: button (optional) exact button title to press, otherwise Escape (cancel); evenIfFront (default false). Returns: what was done, the front app before and after (they must match), and the state afterwards. Notes: off unless AE_MCP_ALLOW_DIALOG_DISMISS=1 or guard-options.json in the bridge folder has {"allowDialogDismiss": true}. When on, the guard also closes plain message boxes by itself before and during commands. Needs Accessibility permission for the app that runs the server. Example: a script error alert blocks the queue; call dismiss-ae-dialog with no button.

Pack: guard. Changes the project (one undo step). Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `button` | string | no | Exact title of the button to press, e.g. "OK" or "Continue". |
| `evenIfFront` | boolean | no | Act even when After Effects is the front app. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "dismiss-ae-dialog",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### get-ae-status

Reports what After Effects is doing, read from outside it, so it works even when After Effects is stuck: not-running, dialog (with its text and buttons), running-command, running-script, busy, idle, paused, panel-stalled or panel-closed; plus its memory footprint, swap, free disk, the driver lock holder, recent DoScript runs and advice. Use when: a tool timed out or failed with dialog-open, panel-not-running or ae-busy; before a long unattended build; or to see whether another client is driving After Effects. Do not use for: project contents (use get-project-info). Inputs: deep (default false) also samples After Effects' main thread for about 3 seconds, which tells a modal dialog from a running script even when the dialog is on another desktop. Returns: state, summary, ae {pid, app, uptimeMin}, memory {aeFootprintGB, swapUsedGB, swapTotalGB, diskFreeGB}, panel {heartbeatAgeMs, fresh, version, state, running, autoRun, pollMs, project}, lock, external {running, recent}, dialogs, windowScan, mainThread, advice. Notes: never sends anything to After Effects and never takes focus. macOS only. Example: a build call failed with dialog-open; call get-ae-status with deep true to read the dialog text.

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deep` | boolean | no | Also sample the main thread (about 3 s). Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "get-ae-status",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### rearm-bridge-panel

Restarts the bridge panel's polling timer inside After Effects when it has stopped (state panel-stalled: After Effects is idle, the panel is open, but its heartbeat is old; this happens after a project is opened). Use when: get-ae-status says panel-stalled, or a tool failed with panel-not-running and the panel is open. Do not use for: a closed panel (open Window > mcp-bridge-auto.jsx) or a dialog (close it first). Inputs: force (default false) re-arms even when the state is not panel-stalled. Returns: before and after states and the new heartbeat age. Notes: sends one tiny script to After Effects through AppleScript, without activating it, under the driver lock, and only after checking that no dialog or script is running. Example: after opening a project by script, call rearm-bridge-panel, then ping.

Pack: guard. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `force` | boolean | no | Re-arm even if the panel does not look stalled. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "rearm-bridge-panel",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### reload-bridge-panel

Loads the newly installed bridge panel code into the open panel without closing it, restarting After Effects or touching the mouse: it stops the old timer, clears the old controls and evaluates the installed mcp-bridge-auto.jsx into the same docked panel. Use when: after npm run build and installing the panel (install-bridge), so After Effects runs the new version; get-bridge-status shows versionsMatch false. Do not use for: opening a panel that is not open (use Window > mcp-bridge-auto.jsx). Inputs: file (optional) panel file to load; default the installed one of the running After Effects. force (default false) reloads even when After Effects is not idle. Returns: what the script returned (OK <version>), and the new heartbeat version. Notes: under the driver lock, refused while a command, script or dialog is active. Example: after installing 2.1.0, call reload-bridge-panel and check that bridgeVersion is 2.1.0.

Pack: guard. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | no | Absolute path of the panel .jsx to load. |
| `force` | boolean | no | Reload even if After Effects is not idle. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "reload-bridge-panel",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

## Components and templates

### add-component-instances

Places one or many instances of a component composition into a target composition in one call, each with its own name, position, start time, duration and Essential Property values. Use when: putting three buttons with different labels on screen, filling a list of cards, or repeating a badge. Do not use for: a single precomp layer without values (add-composition-as-layer) or library components from disk (insert-library-component). Inputs: comp, the target; component, the component comp by id or name; instances [{name, position, startTime or startFrame, duration or durationFrames, values, keyframes}]; or count for identical copies. Returns: count, failed, and results per instance with the layer summary and the values written. Notes: labels are checked on the first instance; an unknown label removes it and fails with the label list. Instances keep list order (the first is on top), and repeated names are numbered. An instance cannot run past the component's length. Undoable in one step. Example: component {name: "Button"}, instances [{position: [560, 540], values: {"Label": "Back"}}, {position: [960, 540], values: {"Label": "Skip"}}, {position: [1360, 540], values: {"Label": "Next", "Fill": "#2F6BFF"}}].

Pack: components. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addComponentInstances`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Target composition. Default: the active composition. |
| `component` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | yes | The component composition to place, by id or name. |
| `instances` | {name?: string, position?: [number, number] \| [number, number, number], startTime?: number, startFrame?: integer, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, values?: object of string \| number \| boolean \| number[] \| object, keyframes?: object[]}[] | no | One entry per instance. |
| `count` | integer | no | Number of identical instances when instances is not given. Default 1. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-component-instances",
  "arguments": {
    "component": {
      "name": "Main Comp"
    }
  }
}
```

### add-essential-property

Adds one property of a layer to its composition's Essential Properties under a label, so every instance of that composition can override it. Checks first that After Effects accepts the property and fails with the list of supported kinds when it does not. Use when: a component needs one more control after create-component, or a comp built by hand should become a component. Do not use for: building the component from layers (create-component) or setting an instance's value (set-component-values). Inputs: comp, the component composition; layer inside it; path to the property, for example "Text/Source Text", "Effects/Width/Slider" or "Contents/Button/Contents/Fill 1/Color"; label (default the property name). Returns: the property summary, the label, its 1-based position and the full list of Essential Property names. Notes: supported kinds are checkboxes, colours, Source Text, single numbers (opacity, rotation, sliders, angles), 2D and 3D points (position, scale, Point Control) and Dropdown Menu Controls. Shape paths, masks and groups cannot be added. Labels must be unique. There is no scripting call to remove an Essential Property. Undoable in one step. Example: comp {name: "Button"}, layer {name: "BG"}, path "Effects/Width/Slider", label "Width".

Pack: components. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addEssentialProperty`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | The component composition (the one whose Essential Graphics panel gets the control). |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `path` | string \| string \| integer[] | yes | Property path: "Transform/Position", "Effects/Gaussian Blur/Blurriness", "Text/Source Text", display or match names, or an array of names and 1-based indices. |
| `label` | string | no | Name in the Essential Graphics panel. Default: the property name. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-essential-property",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "path": "/absolute/path/file"
  }
}
```

### create-component

Turns layers into a reusable component: precomposes them into a new composition, leaves an instance in place with the same position and timing, and exposes the listed properties as Essential Properties that each instance can override. Use when: a button, card, badge or lower third repeats with different text, colours or sizes. Do not use for: a plain precomp without controls (precompose) or one more control on an existing component (add-essential-property). Inputs: layers (default the selected layers); name; properties [{layer, path, label}], layer being a name or 1-based index inside the new comp; startAtZero (default true) starts the content at 0 s; cropToContent (default true) fits the comp to the visible layers plus padding px (default 0); duration in seconds; templateName. Returns: the component comp, the instance layer, the time shift, the crop region, and the Essential Properties added or failed (with reasons). Notes: controls can be checkboxes, colours, Source Text, numbers, points and Dropdown Menu Controls; drive a width through a Slider Control expression. Content outside a cropped comp is clipped, so pad for controls that grow it. Undoable in one step. Example: layers [{name: "BG"}, {name: "Label"}], name "Button", properties [{layer: "Label", path: "Text/Source Text", label: "Label"}].

Pack: components. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createComponent`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected: true} | no | Layers to turn into the component. Default {selected: true}. |
| `name` | string | yes | Name of the new component composition. Must not already be used by a composition. |
| `properties` | {layer: string \| integer \| object, path: string \| string \| integer[], label?: string}[] | no | Properties to expose as Essential Properties, in panel order. |
| `startAtZero` | boolean | no | Shift the content so the component starts at 0 s; the instance is offset so nothing moves in time. Default true. |
| `cropToContent` | boolean | no | Size the component to the bounds of its visible layers so new instances are positioned by their centre. Default true. |
| `padding` | number | no | Pixels added around the content when cropping. Leave room for controls that make the content bigger (a Width slider), or the extra is clipped. Default 0. |
| `duration` | number | no | Component length in seconds. Default: the parent comp length minus the time shift. |
| `templateName` | string | no | Name in the Essential Graphics panel, used as the .mogrt file name on export. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-component",
  "arguments": {
    "name": "My Layer"
  }
}
```

### export-component-template

Exports a component composition as a Motion Graphics template (.mogrt) with its Essential Properties, for reuse in other projects or in Premiere Pro. Use when: a finished component should be shared or kept as a template file. Do not use for: saving the component inside a library folder as an .aep (save-project-as) or rendering video (render). Inputs: comp, the component; path, an absolute output folder or a full path ending in .mogrt (its file name becomes the template name); templateName (default the comp's Essential Graphics name); overwrite (default false); saveProject (default true). Returns: the template name, the .mogrt path, whether it exists and its size in bytes, and whether the project was saved first. Notes: After Effects asks to save a changed project before exporting, which would block the bridge, so the project is saved first; a project that was never saved fails with a hint to use save-project-as. Setting templateName changes the comp's Essential Graphics name. Can take several seconds. Example: comp {name: "Button"}, path "/tmp/components/Button.mogrt", overwrite true.

Pack: components. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `exportComponentTemplate`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | The component composition. |
| `path` | string | yes | Absolute output folder, or a full file path ending in .mogrt. "~/" is expanded. |
| `templateName` | string | no | Template name and file name. Default: the comp's Essential Graphics name. |
| `overwrite` | boolean | no | Replace an existing .mogrt with the same name. Default false. |
| `saveProject` | boolean | no | Save the project first when it has unsaved changes. Default true; false fails instead. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "export-component-template",
  "arguments": {
    "path": "/absolute/path/file"
  }
}
```

### insert-library-component

Inserts a component from a library folder into a composition: imports its .aep once per project (later calls reuse the imported folder), finds its main comp, and adds an instance with optional position, start time, duration and Essential Property values. Use when: building a scene from saved components, such as a button or lower third kept in the library. Do not use for: components already in the project (add-component-instances) or footage files (import-file). Inputs: component, the library name or folder name; folder (default AE_MCP_LIBRARY_DIR); aep and compName to bypass the library; comp, the target; name; position; startTime or startFrame; duration or durationFrames; values {label: value}; keyframes; reuseImported (default true). Returns: the instance layer, the component comp, whether the .aep was imported now or reused, the project folder that holds it, the values written, the instance's Essential Properties and the library entry. Notes: the main comp is the library entry's "comp" or the comp named like the component. A part kept online-only by OneDrive, iCloud or Dropbox (with its footage folder) is downloaded on the server before After Effects opens it; the result says what was downloaded. An unknown label removes the new instance and fails; the import stays for reuse. Missing fonts or footage inside the .aep can show warnings. Undoable in one step. Example: component "Primary Button", position [960, 800], values {"Label": "Start free trial"}.

Pack: components. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `insertLibraryComponent`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Target composition. Default: the active composition. |
| `component` | string | yes | Component name (component.json name or folder name). With aep, the comp name to look for. |
| `folder` | string | no | Library folder. Default: AE_MCP_LIBRARY_DIR. |
| `aep` | string | no | Path to an .aep to use directly instead of a library entry. |
| `compName` | string | no | Main comp inside the .aep. Default: component.json "comp", else the component name. |
| `name` | string | no | Layer name for the instance. |
| `position` | [number, number] \| [number, number, number] | no | Position in the target comp, pixels. Default: comp centre. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Layer start time in seconds. Default 0. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Layer start frame; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds from the in point to the out point. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Duration in frames; wins over duration. |
| `values` | object of string \| number \| boolean \| number[] \| object | no | Values by Essential Property label, for example {"Label": "Buy now", "Fill": "#2F6BFF", "Width": 320}. A label inside an Essential Graphics group can be written "Group/Label". |
| `keyframes` | {label: string, time?: number, frame?: integer, value: string \| number \| boolean \| number[] \| object, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object}[] | no |  |
| `reuseImported` | boolean | no | Reuse a copy of the same .aep already imported into this project. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "insert-library-component",
  "arguments": {
    "component": "value"
  }
}
```

### list-essential-properties

Lists a component's Essential Properties: the control names in panel order and, through an instance layer, each control's type, current value, keyframe count, expression state and the source property it comes from. Use when: before set-component-values or add-component-instances, to learn the exact labels and value types; or to check what an instance overrides. Do not use for: ordinary property values on a layer (get-property-value). Inputs: comp; layer (optional). Without layer, comp is the component itself and the result also lists where it is used. With layer, comp is the composition that holds the instance and the values are that instance's. Returns: component (id, name, size, duration, templateName), count, controllers [{index, name}], properties [{label, path, valueType, value, numKeys, hasExpression, source}], and instances or instance. Notes: read-only. Without an instance anywhere in the project only the names are known; place one with add-component-instances to see types. Sources need After Effects 2022 or later. Example: comp {name: "Main"}, layer {name: "Button 2"}.

Pack: components. Read-only. Bridge command: `listEssentialProperties`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | The component comp, or with layer the comp that holds the instance. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | An instance layer of the component. Optional. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-essential-properties",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### list-library-components

Lists the components in a component library folder without touching After Effects. A library is a folder with one subfolder per component holding <name>.aep and optionally component.json ({name, category, description, controls: [{label, type}], tags, comp}), preview.mp4 and poster.png; category subfolders one level deep and a top-level library.json index (written by save-to-library) are also read. Use when: before insert-library-component, to find a component and its control labels. Do not use for: compositions already in the open project (list-compositions). Inputs: folder (default the AE_MCP_LIBRARY_DIR environment variable); category to filter by; query, words that must all appear in the name, category, description, tags or control labels; limit (default 100). Returns: the library folder, the index file if any, total and matched counts, categories with counts, components [{name, category, description, tags, controls, comp, folder, aep, preview, poster, local}] and skipped folders with the reason. Notes: read-only and runs on the server. A library.json entry is used as written, and component.json is read only for folders library.json does not list, so a library kept online-only in OneDrive or iCloud is listed without downloading its parts. local false means the part is online-only; insert-library-component downloads it first. Example: category "Buttons", query "primary".

Pack: components. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `folder` | string | no | Library folder. Default: AE_MCP_LIBRARY_DIR. |
| `category` | string | no | Only this category (case-insensitive). |
| `query` | string | no | Search words; all must match. |
| `limit` | integer | no | Most components to return. Default 100. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-library-components",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### remove-from-library

Removes a part from the component library: deletes its folder (the .aep, poster and footage), drops its library.json entry and updates the README table. Use when: a part is wrong, replaced by a better one or no longer wanted. Do not use for: replacing a part with a new version (save-to-library with overwrite true keeps its added date). Inputs: component, the part name or its folder ("Buttons/Send Button"); folder (default AE_MCP_LIBRARY_DIR). Returns: the removed entry, the folder deleted and how many parts are left. Notes: does not touch After Effects or projects that already use the part. In a synced folder (OneDrive, iCloud, Dropbox) the files go to that service's recycle bin.

Pack: components. Changes the project (one undo step). Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `component` | string | yes | Part name or folder, for example "Send Button" or "Buttons/Send Button". |
| `folder` | string | no | Library folder. Default: AE_MCP_LIBRARY_DIR. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "remove-from-library",
  "arguments": {
    "component": "value"
  }
}
```

### save-to-library

Saves a composition into the component library as its own part: the composition and everything it uses (nested comps, footage, solids) become <Category>/<Name>/<Name>.aep, footage files are copied into the part's footage folder, a poster frame is saved, and library.json and the README table are updated. Use when: a component is finished and worth reusing in other films (a cursor, a button, a card, a rig). Do not use for: a .mogrt for Premiere Pro (export-component-template) or saving the whole project (save-project-as). Inputs: comp (default the active composition); name (default the comp name); category; description, one sentence on what it is and when to use it; tags; source, a short note on where it came from; folder (default AE_MCP_LIBRARY_DIR, created if its parent exists); posterTime or posterFrame (default the middle); overwrite (default false); saveFirst (default false) saves unsaved changes first; reopen (default true). Returns: the library entry (controls with their types, fonts, footage, size, length), the files written, item counts and whether the project was reopened. Notes: the open project must be saved. After Effects holds one project at a time, so the project is closed, imported into a new project, reduced to the composition and saved as the part, then reopened from disk; the original file is never written, but its undo history and open panels reset. Image sequences stay at their original paths. Text keeps its fonts by name, so the fonts must be installed where the part is used. Example: comp {name: "Send Button"}, category "Buttons", description "Round send button with a press state", tags ["chat", "cta"].

Pack: components. Changes the project (one undo step). Runs on the server; not available inside batch. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `name` | string | no | Part name. Default: the composition name. |
| `category` | string | yes | Category folder, for example "Buttons", "Cursors", "Cards", "Rigs". |
| `description` | string | yes | One sentence: what the part is and when to use it. |
| `tags` | string[] | no | Search words. |
| `source` | string | no | Where the part came from, for example the film it was built for. |
| `folder` | string | no | Library folder. Default: AE_MCP_LIBRARY_DIR. |
| `posterTime` | number | no | Poster frame time in seconds. Default: the middle of the composition. |
| `posterFrame` | integer | no | Poster frame number; wins over posterTime. |
| `overwrite` | boolean | no | Replace a part with the same category and name. Default false. |
| `saveFirst` | boolean | no | Save unsaved changes to the open project first. Default false (unsaved changes stop the call). |
| `reopen` | boolean | no | Reopen the original project afterwards. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "save-to-library",
  "arguments": {
    "category": "value",
    "description": "wiggle(2, 10)"
  }
}
```

### set-component-values

Sets the Essential Property values of one component instance by label: text, numbers, colours, checkboxes, points and menu items. Optional keyframes animate an override over time on that instance only. Use when: giving each copy of a button its own label and colour, or making one instance change state at a time. Do not use for: the component's default values (change the property inside the component) or new instances (add-component-instances). Inputs: layer, the instance; comp that holds it; values {label: value}; keyframes [{label, time or frame, value, easing}] in the holding comp's time; replaceKeys (default false) clears a label's keys before writing. Returns: count, failed, results per label with the value read back, the keyframes written, and the instance's Essential Properties. Notes: unknown labels fail before anything changes and the error lists the labels. A label that already has keys must be changed through keyframes. Numeric keys land softly (speed 0, influence 100 arriving, 25 leaving) unless easing is given; text, true or false and menu items hold. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Button 2"}, values {"Label": "Sign up", "Fill": "#1E1E1E", "Width": 280}.

Pack: components. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setComponentValues`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The component instance (a layer whose source is the component comp). |
| `values` | object of string \| number \| boolean \| number[] \| object | no | Values by Essential Property label, for example {"Label": "Buy now", "Fill": "#2F6BFF", "Width": 320}. A label inside an Essential Graphics group can be written "Group/Label". |
| `keyframes` | {label: string, time?: number, frame?: integer, value: string \| number \| boolean \| number[] \| object, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object}[] | no |  |
| `replaceKeys` | boolean | no | Remove the existing keys of each keyed label first. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-component-values",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-responsive-time

Sets Responsive Design - Time on a component composition: protected regions for the intro and outro as composition markers with Protected Region on, so an instance stretched to a longer or shorter duration keeps its in and out animations at their original speed and only the middle stretches. Use when: a lower third, button or title component must hold for different lengths in different places, or before exporting a .mogrt for editors. Do not use for: ordinary comp markers (add-marker) or slowing a whole layer (set-speed). Inputs: comp, the component; intro and outro in seconds, or introFrames and outroFrames; regions [{start or startFrame, duration or durationFrames, comment}] for more protected spans; replace (default true) removes existing protected-region markers first; introComment and outroComment. Returns: the intro, outro and stretchable span in seconds and frames, the composition markers, and the instances that already use the component. Notes: needs After Effects 2019 or later. A non-protected marker at the same time fails rather than being replaced. Existing instances need Layer > Markers > Update Markers from Source. Undoable in one step. Example: comp {name: "Lower Third"}, introFrames 18, outroFrames 12.

Pack: components. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setResponsiveTime`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | The component composition. |
| `intro` | number | no | Protected intro length in seconds from the comp start. |
| `introFrames` | integer | no | Intro length in frames; wins over intro. |
| `outro` | number | no | Protected outro length in seconds before the comp end. |
| `outroFrames` | integer | no | Outro length in frames; wins over outro. |
| `regions` | {start?: number, startFrame?: integer, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, comment?: string}[] | no | Extra protected spans. |
| `replace` | boolean | no | Remove existing protected-region markers first. Default true. |
| `introComment` | string | no | Comment on the intro marker. Default "Intro". |
| `outroComment` | string | no | Comment on the outro marker. Default "Outro". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-responsive-time",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

## Animation shortcuts

### add-camera-push

Adds a 2D null at a pivot, parents layers to it without moving them, and keys its Scale from 100 to 105 % (or any values) in two keys: the slow push that keeps a held frame alive, scaled about the point the shot centres on, with an optional drift of a few pixels. Use when: a scene or end card should breathe instead of sitting still. Do not use for: 3D camera moves (animate-camera) or one element's own move (animate-property). Inputs: layers (default every layer without a parent; cameras and lights are skipped); pivot [x, y] in composition pixels (default the centre); from and to percent (default 100 and 105); startTime or startFrame (default 0); duration or durationFrames (default to the end of the composition); easing (default linear); drift [dx, dy] pixels; name (default Camera Push). Returns: the null layer, the layers parented and those skipped with the reason, and the scale and drift keys. Notes: the null's anchor and position both sit on the pivot, so parenting changes no layer's values. Layers that already have a parent ride with it. Undoable in one step. Example: pivot [960, 540], to 104, duration 6.

Pack: animate. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addCameraPush`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | no | Layers to animate: an array of layer refs, {selected: true}, {all: true}, {indices: [...]} or {names: [...]}. |
| `pivot` | [number, number] | no | Point the push scales about, composition pixels. Default the composition centre. |
| `from` | number | no | Scale at the start, percent. Default 100. |
| `to` | number | no | Scale at the end, percent. Default 105. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds from the composition start. Default: 0. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default to the end of the composition. Key times snap to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Default linear, an even push. |
| `drift` | [number, number] | no | Pixels the null moves over the same span, for example [-12, 0]. |
| `name` | string | no | Null name. Default "Camera Push". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-camera-push",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### add-entrance

Brings one or many layers in from offset values to the values they already have, so the layout stays as built: fade, rise, slide, scale, blur, pop, or several at once, two keys per property, each layer a few frames after the last. Use when: a card, title, icon or list enters. Do not use for: words or characters inside one text layer (word-by-word-reveal, add-text-animator) or leaving (add-exit). Inputs: layers; kind as a name or an array; direction of travel for slide (default left) and distance in px (rise 40, slide 200); scale % (default 90) or popScale % (default 0) at the start; blur px (default 24, on a Gaussian Blur named Transition Blur); startTime or startFrame (default the earliest in point); duration (default 0.33 s); staggerFrames; order top-down, bottom-up, random (seed) or as-given; easing (default fast attack, long landing); overshoot for pop and scale. Returns: count, failed and per layer the status, time range and keys per property. Notes: rise with slide, or scale with pop, is refused. Offsets are in each layer's parent space. Undoable in one step. Example: layers {names: ["Card 1", "Card 2", "Card 3"]}, kind ["fade", "rise", "blur"], startFrame 12, staggerFrames 4.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addEntrance`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | yes | Layers to animate: an array of layer refs, {selected: true}, {all: true}, {indices: [...]} or {names: [...]}. |
| `kind` | "fade" \| "rise" \| "slide" \| "scale" \| "blur" \| "pop" \| "fade" \| "rise" \| "slide" \| "scale" \| "blur" \| "pop"[] | yes | fade (Opacity), rise (a short move up), slide (a move in a direction), scale, blur (Gaussian Blur) or pop (Scale from a small value), or an array of them to combine. |
| `direction` | "up" \| "down" \| "left" \| "right" | no | Direction of travel for slide. Default left. rise always travels up. |
| `distance` | number | no | Pixels travelled by rise or slide. Default 40 for rise, 200 for slide. |
| `scale` | number | no | For kind scale: the offset scale in percent of the layer's own scale. Default 90. |
| `popScale` | number | no | For kind pop: the offset scale in percent. Default 0. |
| `blur` | number | no | For kind blur: the offset blur in pixels. Default 24. |
| `staggerFrames` | integer | no | Frames between one layer and the next. Default about 0.07 s (4 frames at 60 fps, 2 at 30). |
| `order` | "top-down" \| "bottom-up" \| "random" \| "as-given" | no | Which layer goes first: top-down (timeline order, default), bottom-up, random (seeded) or as-given (the order of the layers array). |
| `seed` | integer | no | Seed for the random order. Default 1. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |
| `overshoot` | boolean | no | Adds one small overshoot key (an anticipation key on exits) to pop and scale. Default false. |
| `overshootAmount` | number | no | Fraction of the move for the overshoot. Default 0.08. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds from the composition start. Default: the earliest in point of the layers. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default 0.33. Key times snap to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-entrance",
  "arguments": {
    "layers": [
      {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    ],
    "kind": "fade"
  }
}
```

### add-exit

Takes one or many layers out from the values they have to offset values with an accelerating ease: the mirror of add-entrance, with the same kinds (fade, rise, slide, scale, blur, pop or an array), two keys per property, staggered. Use when: elements leave before a cut or make room for the next beat. Do not use for: entrances (add-entrance) or trimming layers (set-layer-timing). Inputs: layers; kind; direction of travel for slide (default left; rise travels up) and distance px; scale % or popScale % reached at the end; blur px; endTime or endFrame, when the last layer finishes (default the earliest out point), or startTime or startFrame instead, not both; duration (default 0.33 s); staggerFrames; order; easing (default speed 0, influence 85 leaving and 15 arriving); overshoot adds a small anticipation to pop and scale. Returns: count, failed and per layer the status, time range and keys per property. Notes: values are read where each exit starts, so an exit can follow an entrance on the same layer. A cut on the end key leaves at speed. Undoable in one step. Example: layers {all: true}, kind ["fade", "scale"], scale 95, endFrame 240, staggerFrames 2, order "bottom-up".

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addExit`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | yes | Layers to animate: an array of layer refs, {selected: true}, {all: true}, {indices: [...]} or {names: [...]}. |
| `kind` | "fade" \| "rise" \| "slide" \| "scale" \| "blur" \| "pop" \| "fade" \| "rise" \| "slide" \| "scale" \| "blur" \| "pop"[] | yes | fade (Opacity), rise (a short move up), slide (a move in a direction), scale, blur (Gaussian Blur) or pop (Scale from a small value), or an array of them to combine. |
| `direction` | "up" \| "down" \| "left" \| "right" | no | Direction of travel for slide. Default left. rise always travels up. |
| `distance` | number | no | Pixels travelled by rise or slide. Default 40 for rise, 200 for slide. |
| `scale` | number | no | For kind scale: the offset scale in percent of the layer's own scale. Default 90. |
| `popScale` | number | no | For kind pop: the offset scale in percent. Default 0. |
| `blur` | number | no | For kind blur: the offset blur in pixels. Default 24. |
| `staggerFrames` | integer | no | Frames between one layer and the next. Default about 0.07 s (4 frames at 60 fps, 2 at 30). |
| `order` | "top-down" \| "bottom-up" \| "random" \| "as-given" | no | Which layer goes first: top-down (timeline order, default), bottom-up, random (seeded) or as-given (the order of the layers array). |
| `seed` | integer | no | Seed for the random order. Default 1. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |
| `overshoot` | boolean | no | Adds one small overshoot key (an anticipation key on exits) to pop and scale. Default false. |
| `overshootAmount` | number | no | Fraction of the move for the overshoot. Default 0.08. |
| `endTime` | number | no | Seconds when the last layer's exit ends. Default the earliest out point of the layers. |
| `endFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over endTime. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds from the composition start. Default: derived from the end time. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default 0.33. Key times snap to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-exit",
  "arguments": {
    "layers": [
      {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    ],
    "kind": "fade"
  }
}
```

### animate-property

Animates one or more properties of a layer from a start value to an end value with two eased keys per property on the same frames. Use when: one directed move, such as a card sliding 200 px right, a scale-up, a colour change or an effect sweep. Do not use for: entrances and exits from offsets of the current layout (add-entrance, add-exit), more than two keys (set-keyframes-bulk) or many layers (set-keyframes-multi). Inputs: layer; properties[] of {property path, to or by (a change added to from), from (default the keyed value at the start)}; startTime or startFrame (default the layer in point); duration in seconds (default 0.5) or durationFrames; easing for the arrival (default a landing: speed 0, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving, no overshoot); spatial; overshootAmount for easing "overshoot". Returns: the time range in seconds and frames and, per property, its path, from, to and the keys written with their ease. Notes: key times snap to whole frames. Source Text is refused (use set-text with a time). Entries are checked before anything is written. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Card"}, properties [{property: "Transform/Position", by: [200, 0]}], startFrame 30, durationFrames 20.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `animateProperty`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `properties` | {property: string \| string \| integer[], to?: any, by?: any, from?: any}[] | yes |  |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds from the composition start. Default: the layer in point. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default 0.5. Key times snap to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |
| `spatial` | "linear" \| "auto" | no | For Position and Anchor Point: "linear" (default) gives straight motion paths, "auto" lets After Effects auto-bezier the path. |
| `overshootAmount` | number | no | Fraction of the move for easing "overshoot". Default 0.08. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "animate-property",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "properties": [
      {
        "property": "Transform/Opacity"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### move-along-path

Makes a layer travel along a path drawn on a shape layer or a mask: adds a Progress slider (0 to 100) keyed in two eased keys and a Position expression that reads pointOnPath at that progress through the path's group and layer transforms, with an optional Rotation expression that turns the layer along the path. Use when: a dot, icon, plane or cursor follows a curve. Do not use for: straight moves (animate-property) or changing the path shape (set-shape-path). Inputs: layer (the one that moves); path {layer, group (default the first) or mask}; startTime or startFrame (default the in point); duration (default 1 s) or durationFrames; from and to progress in percent of the path length (default 0 to 100; past 100 loops a closed path); easing (default landing); orient; sliderName (default Progress). Returns: the path reference, whether it is closed, the slider keys, and each expression with any After Effects error. Notes: the path must be a Bezier path, not a parametric rectangle or ellipse, and the path layer needs a unique name. Existing Position keys are overridden. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Dot"}, path {layer: {name: "Route"}}, durationFrames 45, orient true.

Pack: animate. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `moveAlongPath`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `path` | {layer: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, group?: string \| integer, mask?: string \| integer} | yes | Where the path lives. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds from the composition start. Default: the layer in point. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default 1. Key times snap to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `from` | number | no | Progress at the start, percent of the path length. Default 0. |
| `to` | number | no | Progress at the end. Default 100. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |
| `orient` | boolean | no | Turn the layer along the path direction. Default false. |
| `sliderName` | string | no | Name of the Slider Control. Default "Progress". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "move-along-path",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "path": {
      "layer": {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### play-at-markers

Replays a property's existing keyframed animation at every marker: one expression maps the time since the latest marker into the keyed range, so one curve plays as often as there are markers, with no copied keys. Can add the markers in the same call. Use when: a pulse, press, pop or nudge repeats on beats, clicks or cues. Do not use for: playing once (the keys already do) or endless loops (apply-expression-preset with a loop preset). Inputs: layer; properties[] (each with at least two keys); markerSource layer (default, the layer's own markers) or comp; markerLayer to read another layer's markers; comment to react only to markers with that exact comment; addMarkers[] of {time or frame, comment}. Returns: the triggering markers, the markers added, and per property its key span and expression state. Notes: before the first marker the property holds its first key value and after each play it holds the last; a new marker restarts the play. The keys can sit anywhere in the timeline; retime the animation by moving them. Replaces any existing expression on those properties. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Badge"}, properties ["Transform/Scale"], addMarkers [{frame: 30}, {frame: 75}, {frame: 120}].

Pack: animate. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `playAtMarkers`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `properties` | string \| string \| integer[][] | yes |  |
| `markerSource` | "layer" \| "comp" | no | Whose markers trigger the play. Default layer. |
| `markerLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Read the markers of this layer instead (for example a control null). Implies markerSource layer. |
| `comment` | string | no | Only markers whose comment equals this trigger. |
| `addMarkers` | {time?: number, frame?: integer, comment?: string}[] | no | Markers to add to the marker source first. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "play-at-markers",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "properties": [
      "value"
    ]
  }
}
```

### press

Presses a layer like a button: Scale dips to 96 % and returns in three eased keys, or with variant pop jumps to 108 % in one frame and settles. Optionally a cursor dips with it, properties flip state on the click frame with hold keys, and a ring ripples out. Use when: a cursor clicks a button, a chip is selected or a card is tapped. Do not use for: entrances (add-entrance) or the same click repeated (key it once, then play-at-markers). Inputs: layer; time or frame where the press starts (required); variant dip or pop; scale %; downDuration or downFrames (dip 0.1 s, pop one frame); upDuration or upFrames (dip 0.15 s, pop 0.25 s); easing; cursor {layer, scale %}; flip [{property, to}]; ripple true or {position, radius, style, strokeWidth, color, opacity, duration, name}. Returns: start, click and end times and frames, the Scale keys, cursor keys, flips and the ripple layer with its keys. Notes: layers scale about their anchor points. A cursor dips twice as deep by default. The ripple is trimmed to its animation. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Send"}, frame 90, cursor {layer: {name: "Cursor"}}, ripple true.

Pack: animate. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `press`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `variant` | "dip" \| "pop" | no | dip (default): Scale down and back. pop: a one-frame jump up, then a settle. |
| `scale` | number | no | Scale at the bottom (dip, default 96) or peak (pop, default 108), percent of the layer's own scale. |
| `downDuration` | number | no | Seconds to the bottom or peak. Default 0.1 for dip, one frame for pop. |
| `downFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over downDuration. |
| `upDuration` | number | no | Seconds back to rest. Default 0.15 for dip, 0.25 for pop. |
| `upFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over upDuration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Ease of each segment. Default speed 0 with influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving. |
| `cursor` | {layer: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, scale?: number} | no |  |
| `flip` | {property: string \| string \| integer[], to: any}[] | no | State changes on the click frame: hold keys from the old value to the new one, such as a fill colour. |
| `ripple` | boolean \| {position?: [number, number], radius?: number, style?: "ring" \| "fill", strokeWidth?: number, color?: string \| number[] \| object, opacity?: number, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, name?: string} | no | true for a default ring, or its settings. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "press",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### typewriter-text

Types a text layer on one character at a time with the mechanism of After Effects' own Typewriter preset: a text animator with Opacity 0 and a Range Selector by characters (Shape Square, Smoothness 0) whose Start is keyed 0 to 100 in two keys, plus an optional typing cursor that follows the last typed character. Use when: a prompt, field, console line or caption types itself. Do not use for: blurred word or line reveals (word-by-word-reveal) or changing the words (set-text). Inputs: layer; startTime or startFrame (default the in point); charactersPerSecond (default 20), or duration or durationFrames (these win); easing (default linear; an ease makes typing slow into the end); name; cursor true or {char, blinkRate, color, keepAfterTyping, leadIn}. Returns: characters, rate, time range, first and last character times, the keys, and the cursor layer with its expression states. Notes: the cursor is a copy of the text layer parented to it; expression selectors show only the slot after the last typed character and a Character Value animator draws the glyph there; its Opacity blinks while idle. Exact on left-aligned text. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Prompt"}, startFrame 20, charactersPerSecond 24, cursor {char: "|", leadIn: 0.5}.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `typewriterText`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds from the composition start. Default: the layer in point. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `charactersPerSecond` | number | no | Typing rate. Default 20. Ignored when duration or durationFrames is given. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default characters / charactersPerSecond. Key times snap to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing on the Start keys. Default linear (an even rhythm). |
| `name` | string | no | Animator name. Default "Typewriter". |
| `cursor` | boolean \| {char?: string, blinkRate?: number, color?: string \| number[] \| object, keepAfterTyping?: boolean, leadIn?: number} | no | true for a default "\|" cursor, or its settings. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "typewriter-text",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### word-by-word-reveal

Reveals a text layer word by word, or line by line, with one text animator: each word starts transparent, blurred and offset and lands sharp in place as a Range Selector based on words sweeps across, keyed in two eased keys. Use when: a headline or paragraph should read on calmly. Do not use for: typing (typewriter-text) or the other preset looks (add-text-animator). Inputs: layer; startTime or startFrame (default the in point); duration or durationFrames, or secondsPerWord (default 0.12 s per word, kept within 0.7 to 3 s); blur px (default 25); offset y px or [x, y] (default 20); opacity at the start (default 0); scale % at the start (optional); basedOn words or lines; drive start (Square shape, Start 0 to 100, default) or offset (Ramp Up, Offset -100 to 100, words overlap); easeHigh, easeLow; randomize with seed; easing (default influence 33.3 leaving, 80 arriving); name. Returns: the animator and selector paths, the keyed property, units counted, the start state, time range and keys. Notes: animator values are the start state, so the layout stays as built. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Paragraph"}, startFrame 30, secondsPerWord 0.1, blur 20, offset 24.

Pack: animate. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `wordByWordReveal`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds from the composition start. Default: the layer in point. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default from secondsPerWord. Key times snap to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `secondsPerWord` | number | no | Seconds per word (or line). Ignored when duration is given. |
| `blur` | number | no | Blur in pixels at the start. Default 25. 0 adds no Blur property. |
| `offset` | number \| [number, number] | no | Position offset at the start: a number is y pixels (positive starts lower), or [x, y]. Default 20. |
| `opacity` | number | no | Opacity at the start, percent. Default 0. |
| `scale` | number | no | Scale at the start, percent. Default none (no Scale property). |
| `basedOn` | "words" \| "lines" | no | Unit the selector counts. Default words. |
| `drive` | "start" \| "offset" | no | start: Square shape, Start 0 to 100 (one word after another, default). offset: Ramp Up, Offset -100 to 100 (overlapping words). |
| `easeHigh` | number | no | Range Selector Ease High, percent. |
| `easeLow` | number | no | Range Selector Ease Low, percent. |
| `randomize` | boolean | no | Reveal the words in a random order. Default false. |
| `seed` | integer | no | Random Seed for randomize. Default 1. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing into this key: a preset (ease = Easy Ease, ease-out for entrances, snappy 75/25, overshoot adds a key, smooth continues through), {type:"custom", inSpeed, inInfluence, outSpeed, outInfluence} (influence 0.1-100, default 33.33, speeds 0), {type:"bezier", x1, y1, x2, y2} as CSS, or "@style.<name>". |
| `name` | string | no | Animator name. Default "Word Reveal". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "word-by-word-reveal",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

## Layer styles

### add-layer-style

Turns on a layer style (drop-shadow, inner-shadow, outer-glow, inner-glow, bevel-emboss, satin, color-overlay, gradient-overlay or stroke) on one or many layers and sets it. Use when: a card, button or title needs a shadow, glow, bevel, overlay or outline. Do not use for: the Drop Shadow or Glow effects (apply-effect-template) or changing a style that is on (set-layer-style). Inputs: layer or layers; kind; settings (color, opacity, blendMode, angle, distance, spread, size, noise and kind-specific fields); fillOpacity; useDefaults; allowMenuCommand (default true). Returns: count, failed, and per layer the settings read back, whether it was added now, and the method. Notes: idempotent; a style already on only gets the given settings. New styles start from designed defaults: drop-shadow black 12 %, angle 90, distance 6, size 24; inner-shadow 10 %, distance 2, size 6; outer-glow white 35 %, size 30; stroke 1 px black 10 % inside. A style new to a layer may need the Layer Styles menu command, which opens the comp in the viewer and selects the layer briefly; both are restored. Gradient colours are drawn by a Gradient Ramp effect. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Card"}, kind "drop-shadow", settings {opacity: 14, size: 30}.

Pack: styles. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addLayerStyle`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected: true} \| {all: true} | no | Several layers: an array of layer references, {selected: true} or {all: true}. |
| `kind` | "drop-shadow" \| "inner-shadow" \| "outer-glow" \| "inner-glow" \| "bevel-emboss" \| "satin" \| "color-overlay" \| "gradient-overlay" \| "stroke" | yes |  |
| `settings` | {color?: string \| number[] \| object, opacity?: number, blendMode?: "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "linear-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity", angle?: number, useGlobalLight?: boolean, distance?: number, spread?: number, size?: number, noise?: number, layerKnocksOut?: boolean, technique?: "softer" \| "precise" \| "smooth" \| "chisel-hard" \| "chisel-soft", range?: number, jitter?: number, source?: "center" \| "edge", bevelStyle?: "outer-bevel" \| "inner-bevel" \| "emboss" \| "pillow-emboss" \| "stroke-emboss", depth?: number, direction?: "up" \| "down", soften?: number, altitude?: number, highlightMode?: "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "linear-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity", highlightColor?: string \| number[] \| object, highlightOpacity?: number, shadowMode?: "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "linear-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity", shadowColor?: string \| number[] \| object, shadowOpacity?: number, invert?: boolean, colors?: string \| number[] \| object \| object[], gradientStyle?: "linear" \| "radial" \| "angle" \| "reflected" \| "diamond", reverse?: boolean, alignWithLayer?: boolean, scale?: number, offset?: [number, number], smoothness?: number, position?: "outside" \| "inside" \| "center"} | no | Style settings. Only the fields that belong to the kind are accepted. |
| `fillOpacity` | number | no | Blending Options > Fill Opacity in percent. 0 hides the layer's own pixels and keeps its styles (outline-only text with a Stroke). |
| `useDefaults` | boolean | no | Apply the designed defaults under the given settings. Default true when the style is added now, false when it was already on. |
| `allowMenuCommand` | boolean | no | Allow the Layer Styles menu command (viewer and selection) when the style cannot be turned on through its property. Default true; false fails instead. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-layer-style",
  "arguments": {
    "kind": "drop-shadow",
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### list-layer-styles

Lists the layer styles on one or many layers: which of the nine kinds are on, off or never added, every setting read back by its friendly name (colours as hex, menus by name), keyframe counts, and the Blending Options values (global light angle and altitude, fill opacity). Use when: before set-layer-style, to see the current values; or to check what add-layer-style produced. Do not use for: effects (list-layer-effects) or text styling (get-text). Inputs: layer, or layers (an array, {selected: true} or {all: true}); includeOff (default true) also lists styles that are switched off. Returns: per layer the styles [{kind, label, on, path, settings, keyed}], blendingOptions, and colorsEffect when a Gradient Ramp draws the gradient colours. Notes: read-only. Layer-style gradient colours cannot be read by script and show as null. Cameras and lights have no layer styles and report an error entry. Example: layer {name: "Card"}.

Pack: styles. Read-only. Bridge command: `listLayerStyles`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected: true} \| {all: true} | no | Several layers: an array of layer references, {selected: true} or {all: true}. |
| `includeOff` | boolean | no | List styles that are added but switched off. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-layer-styles",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### remove-layer-style

Turns a layer style off, or every layer style on the layer off, on one or many layers. The style stays in the Layer Styles group with its settings, switched off, so add-layer-style can turn it back on unchanged. Use when: a shadow or glow is no longer wanted, or to compare a layer with and without its styles. Do not use for: hiding the layer (set-layer-flags) or changing a style (set-layer-style). Inputs: layer or layers; kind, or all true for every style. Returns: count, failed, and per layer the styles turned off and the ones that were already off or never added. Notes: After Effects has no script call that deletes a layer style, so styles are switched off (the eye in the timeline). For gradient-overlay, the Gradient Ramp effect that add-layer-style made for the colours is switched off too. Idempotent. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Card"}, kind "drop-shadow".

Pack: styles. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `removeLayerStyle`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected: true} \| {all: true} | no | Several layers: an array of layer references, {selected: true} or {all: true}. |
| `kind` | "drop-shadow" \| "inner-shadow" \| "outer-glow" \| "inner-glow" \| "bevel-emboss" \| "satin" \| "color-overlay" \| "gradient-overlay" \| "stroke" | no |  |
| `all` | boolean | no | true turns every style on the layer off. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "remove-layer-style",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### set-layer-style
