Changes the settings of a layer style that is already on a layer, as static values or as keyframes with easing, for example a shadow that grows as a card lifts or a glow that pulses once. Use when: adjusting or animating an existing shadow, glow, bevel, overlay or stroke. Do not use for: turning a style on (add-layer-style) or off (remove-layer-style). Inputs: layer; kind; settings, the static values (same fields as add-layer-style); keyframes [{setting, time or frame, value, easing}]; replaceKeys (default false) clears the keyed settings first; fillOpacity. Returns: the settings read back, the fields changed, the keyframes written per setting with property paths, and notes. Notes: fails with not-found when the style was never added. A setting that already has keys gets a key at the current time instead of a static value. Number keys land softly (speed 0, influence 80 arriving, 30 leaving) unless easing is given; menus and checkboxes hold. Gradient colours go to the Gradient Ramp effect and cannot be keyed here. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Card"}, kind "drop-shadow", keyframes [{setting: "distance", frame: 0, value: 4}, {setting: "distance", frame: 12, value: 16}].

Pack: styles. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setLayerStyle`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | Layer: {index} (1 = top), {id} (stable) or {name} (must be unique). |
| `kind` | "drop-shadow" \| "inner-shadow" \| "outer-glow" \| "inner-glow" \| "bevel-emboss" \| "satin" \| "color-overlay" \| "gradient-overlay" \| "stroke" | yes |  |
| `settings` | {color?: string \| number[] \| object, opacity?: number, blendMode?: "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "linear-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity", angle?: number, useGlobalLight?: boolean, distance?: number, spread?: number, size?: number, noise?: number, layerKnocksOut?: boolean, technique?: "softer" \| "precise" \| "smooth" \| "chisel-hard" \| "chisel-soft", range?: number, jitter?: number, source?: "center" \| "edge", bevelStyle?: "outer-bevel" \| "inner-bevel" \| "emboss" \| "pillow-emboss" \| "stroke-emboss", depth?: number, direction?: "up" \| "down", soften?: number, altitude?: number, highlightMode?: "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "linear-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity", highlightColor?: string \| number[] \| object, highlightOpacity?: number, shadowMode?: "normal" \| "dissolve" \| "darken" \| "multiply" \| "color-burn" \| "linear-burn" \| "darker-color" \| "lighten" \| "screen" \| "color-dodge" \| "linear-dodge" \| "lighter-color" \| "overlay" \| "soft-light" \| "hard-light" \| "vivid-light" \| "linear-light" \| "pin-light" \| "hard-mix" \| "difference" \| "exclusion" \| "subtract" \| "divide" \| "hue" \| "saturation" \| "color" \| "luminosity", shadowColor?: string \| number[] \| object, shadowOpacity?: number, invert?: boolean, colors?: string \| number[] \| object \| object[], gradientStyle?: "linear" \| "radial" \| "angle" \| "reflected" \| "diamond", reverse?: boolean, alignWithLayer?: boolean, scale?: number, offset?: [number, number], smoothness?: number, position?: "outside" \| "inside" \| "center"} | no | Style settings. Only the fields that belong to the kind are accepted. |
| `keyframes` | {setting: "color" \| "opacity" \| "blendMode" \| "angle" \| "useGlobalLight" \| "distance" \| "spread" \| "size" \| "noise" \| "layerKnocksOut" \| "technique" \| "range" \| "jitter" \| "source" \| "bevelStyle" \| "depth" \| "direction" \| "soften" \| "altitude" \| "highlightMode" \| "highlightColor" \| "highlightOpacity" \| "shadowMode" \| "shadowColor" \| "shadowOpacity" \| "invert" \| "gradientStyle" \| "reverse" \| "alignWithLayer" \| "scale" \| "offset" \| "smoothness" \| "position", time?: number, frame?: integer, value: any, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object}[] | no |  |
| `replaceKeys` | boolean | no | Remove the existing keys of each keyed setting first. Default false. |
| `fillOpacity` | number | no | Blending Options > Fill Opacity in percent. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-layer-style",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "kind": "drop-shadow"
  }
}
```

## Project check

### audit-composition

Checks how a composition is built and lists problems, each with severity (error, warning, info), comp, layer, property path, time and frame, and a one-line fix. Errors: frame-by-frame keys (3 or more on consecutive frames), expression errors, missing footage, fonts that are not installed. Warnings: hold-key ladders, Source Text with many keys, layers of 3 frames or less, many layers sharing one name stem (not copies an expression rig places), effects switched off or missing, precomps shorter than their use. Info: layers outside the comp, the properties with the most keys. Use when: before showing work to a person or after a large automated build. Do not use for: expression errors alone (get-expression-errors) or how a frame looks (see-frame). Inputs: comp; recurse into nested comps; checks to run (default all); limits; minSeverity. Returns: summary counts by severity, total, truncated, the comps checked, findings sorted errors first, and key statistics. Notes: read-only. Works from key times, never frame values, so it stays fast on large comps. Gradient colour properties are skipped. Example: comp {name: "Main"}, recurse true.

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Bridge command: `auditComposition`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `recurse` | boolean | no | Also audit every composition used as a layer, each once. Default false. |
| `checks` | "frame-by-frame" \| "hold-ladders" \| "source-text-keys" \| "short-layers" \| "name-stems" \| "expression-errors" \| "missing-footage" \| "missing-fonts" \| "effects" \| "precomp-duration" \| "outside-comp" \| "key-counts"[] | no | Checks to run. Default all. |
| `limits` | {consecutiveKeys?: integer, holdKeys?: integer, sourceTextKeys?: integer, shortLayerFrames?: integer, nameStemCount?: integer, topKeyCounts?: integer, maxFindings?: integer, maxDepth?: integer, maxComps?: integer} | no | Thresholds. Every field is optional. |
| `minSeverity` | "info" \| "warning" \| "error" | no | Lowest severity returned. Default info. The summary always counts everything. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "audit-composition",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### organize-project

Sorts Project panel items into folders by type: Comps (compositions no other comp uses), Precomps (compositions used inside other comps), Footage, Images, Audio, Solids and Vectors (.ai, .eps, .pdf, .svg). Existing folders with those names are reused. Items already inside other folders stay where the user put them unless asked. Use when: before handing a project over or when the Project panel is a long flat list. Do not use for: deleting unused items (remove-unused-footage, reduce-project) or moving one item (move-item-to-folder). Inputs: dryRun (default false) returns the plan only; includeFoldered (default false) also moves items that sit in user folders; folderNames to rename the target folders, for example {"precomps": "02 Precomps"}; removeEmptyFolders (default false) deletes user folders left empty by the move. Returns: dryRun, the moves [{item, from, to}], folders created, items kept in user folders, counts per folder, and empty folders removed. Notes: never renames or deletes items; nothing in a render changes. Undoable in one step. Example: dryRun true, then the same call without dryRun.

Pack: check. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `organizeProject`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dryRun` | boolean | no | Only return the plan. Default false. |
| `includeFoldered` | boolean | no | Also move items that are inside user folders. Default false. |
| `folderNames` | {comps?: string, precomps?: string, footage?: string, images?: string, audio?: string, solids?: string, vectors?: string} | no | Folder names to use instead of Comps, Precomps, Footage, Images, Audio, Solids and Vectors. |
| `removeEmptyFolders` | boolean | no | Delete user folders that end up empty. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "organize-project",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### purge-caches

Frees memory by purging After Effects caches, the same as Edit > Purge: all caches (RAM and, from 24.3, the disk cache), memory only, undo history, image caches or snapshots. Reports the memory in use before and after. Use when: after a long build or render, before rendering a time that was already cached (a stale frame can come back), or when After Effects slows down. Do not use for: removing unused project items (remove-unused-footage, reduce-project). Inputs: target all (default), memory (RAM only, After Effects 24.3 or later), undo, image or snapshot. Returns: target, the PurgeTarget used, memoryInUse before and after in bytes, the bytes freed, and notes. Notes: purging undo cannot be undone: every earlier step leaves the undo history. Before 24.3, all clears RAM only. Purging is not an undoable step itself. Example: target "all" after a batch of renders.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `purgeCaches`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `target` | "all" \| "memory" \| "undo" \| "image" \| "snapshot" | no | What to purge. Default all. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "purge-caches",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### save-version

Saves the project as the next version next to its current file and keeps working in the new file: name_v01.aep becomes name_v02.aep (padding kept), and a name without a version number gets _v02. An optional note is added to the file name. Use when: before a risky change, at a milestone, or before handing work over, so earlier versions stay on disk. Do not use for: saving in place (save-project) or choosing any path (save-project-as). Inputs: note, a few words added as _note (letters, digits, spaces, dots, dashes and underscores; spaces become dashes). Returns: previousPath, path, fileName, version, the version names skipped because they already existed, and saved. Notes: refuses a project that was never saved (use save-project-as first). A version that already exists is never overwritten; the next free number is used. Saving is not undoable. Example: note "before color pass" turns promo_v03.aep into promo_v04_before-color-pass.aep.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `saveVersion`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `note` | string | no | Short note added to the file name. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "save-version",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

## Scene planning

### add-reference-overlay

Puts a reference video or still over a composition as a guide layer that never renders: imported once into a References folder (or an existing item), fitted to the comp, starting at a time offset, at an opacity, in Normal or Difference mode for frame matching, labelled and locked. With sideBySide it instead builds a review comp twice as wide showing the reference and the comp next to each other at the same size, with rendered labels. Use when: building against a reference film, a storyboard frame or a client cut. Do not use for: footage that belongs in the film (add-footage-to-composition). Inputs: comp; path to import or item; startTime or startFrame of the reference's first frame (default 0); opacity (default 50, 100 in difference); blendMode normal or difference; fit contain (default), cover, stretch or none; name; label (default red); locked (default true); audio (default true); sideBySide; referenceSide left (default) or right; labels; gap. Returns: the item (imported or reused), the layer, the fit scale and, with sideBySide, the review comp and its layers. Notes: the same file is imported once per project. Undoable in one step. Example: comp {name: "02 Feature"}, path "/refs/feature.mp4", startFrame 0, blendMode "difference".

Pack: scenes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addReferenceOverlay`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `path` | string | no | Absolute path of the video or image to import. Reused when the project already has it. |
| `item` | {id?: integer, name?: string, index?: integer} | no | An item already in the project instead of a path. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds in the comp where the reference's first frame sits. Default 0. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `opacity` | number | no | Percent. Default 50, or 100 for difference. |
| `blendMode` | "normal" \| "difference" | no | normal (default) to trace over it, difference to see where frames differ (black means a match). |
| `fit` | "contain" \| "cover" \| "stretch" \| "none" | no | contain (default) fits inside the comp, cover fills it, stretch distorts to fill, none keeps 100 %. |
| `name` | string | no | Layer name. Default "Reference: <item name>". |
| `label` | "none" \| "red" \| "yellow" \| "aqua" \| "pink" \| "lavender" \| "peach" \| "sea-foam" \| "blue" \| "green" \| "purple" \| "orange" \| "brown" \| "fuchsia" \| "cyan" \| "sandstone" \| "dark-green" | no | Layer label colour. Default red. |
| `locked` | boolean | no | Lock the layer. Default true. |
| `audio` | boolean | no | Keep the reference audio switched on. Default true. |
| `sideBySide` | boolean | no | Build a review comp with the reference next to the comp instead of an overlay. |
| `referenceSide` | "left" \| "right" | no | Side of the reference in the review comp. Default left. |
| `labels` | boolean | no | Rendered name labels in the review comp. Default true. |
| `gap` | integer | no | Pixels between the two halves of the review comp. Default 0. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-reference-overlay",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### create-scene-plan

Lays out a film scene by scene: a folder named after the film holding the master composition and a Scenes folder with one numbered comp per scene (01 Intro, 02 Feature) placed in order in the master, a comp marker at each scene start with its name and notes, and in each scene a guide text layer with the notes that never renders. Use when: starting a film from a scene list or storyboard, or updating that plan. Do not use for: adding one scene later (insert-scene) or retiming one (set-scene-duration). Inputs: filmName (the master comp name); scenes [{name, duration or durationFrames, notes, transition planned into the next scene, overlapFrames}]; fps (default 30); width, height (default 1920 x 1080); backgroundColor (default black); backgroundLayer (default true, an opaque solid per scene); overlapFrames (default 0); guides; folderName; ifExists update (default) or report. Returns: the folders, the master, and per scene its comp id, layer, in, out, duration, notes and planned transition, plus the changes. Notes: re-running with the same filmName updates notes and durations and adds missing scenes, never duplicating. Undoable in one step. Example: filmName "Launch Film", fps 60, scenes [{name: "Intro", duration: 3, notes: "Logo builds"}, {name: "Feature", duration: 4.5}].

Pack: scenes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createScenePlan`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `filmName` | string | yes | Name of the film: the master composition and its folder. |
| `scenes` | {name: string, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, notes?: string, transition?: "match-cut" \| "zoom-through" \| "push" \| "shape-reveal" \| "blur-swap" \| "whip", overlapFrames?: integer}[] | yes |  |
| `fps` | number | no | Frame rate of every comp. Default 30. |
| `width` | integer | no | Pixels. Default 1920. |
| `height` | integer | no | Pixels. Default 1080. |
| `pixelAspect` | number | no | Default 1. |
| `backgroundColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Background of every comp. Default black. |
| `backgroundLayer` | boolean | no | Add a locked solid named Background at the bottom of each scene so scenes stay opaque inside the master (push and reveals need that). Default true. |
| `overlapFrames` | integer | no | Frames each scene overlaps the next. Default 0. |
| `guides` | boolean | no | Add the "Scene Notes" guide text layer to each scene. Default true. |
| `folderName` | string | no | Name of the folder holding the scene comps. Default "Scenes". |
| `ifExists` | "update" \| "report" | no | When a plan with this filmName exists: update it (default) or only report it. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-scene-plan",
  "arguments": {
    "filmName": "My Layer",
    "scenes": [
      {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### insert-scene

Adds a new scene composition to a scene plan: creates it in the scenes folder with the master's size, frame rate and background (plus the Background solid and Scene Notes guide when the plan uses them), places it in the master after or before a scene or at a position, shifts the later scenes by its length, and renumbers the scene comps. Use when: the film needs another beat between or around existing scenes. Do not use for: starting a plan (create-scene-plan) or a different length (set-scene-duration). Inputs: comp (the master); name; duration or durationFrames; notes; transition planned into the next scene; after, before (a scene) or position (1-based; default the end); overlapFrames with the previous scene; guides, backgroundLayer, renumber (default true); rippleOtherLayers. Returns: the new scene (comp id, layer, in and out), the renames, the layers moved, transitions removed at the split cut (with their arguments to rebuild) and the new sequence. Notes: a transition across the cut where the scene goes in is removed, because its two scenes are no longer neighbours. Undoable in one step. Example: comp {name: "Launch Film"}, name "Pricing", duration 3, after "Feature".

Pack: scenes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `insertScene`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `name` | string | yes | Scene name without a number. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Snapped to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `notes` | string | no | Notes for the new scene: shown on its guide layer, its comp comment and its marker. |
| `transition` | "match-cut" \| "zoom-through" \| "push" \| "shape-reveal" \| "blur-swap" \| "whip" | no | Transition planned from this scene into the next one. |
| `after` | integer \| string \| {compId?: integer, layer?: object} | no | Insert after this scene. |
| `before` | integer \| string \| {compId?: integer, layer?: object} | no | Insert before this scene. |
| `position` | integer | no | 1-based position the new scene takes. Default after the last scene. |
| `overlapFrames` | integer | no | Frames the new scene overlaps the previous one (or the next when it goes first). Default 0. |
| `guides` | boolean | no | Add the Scene Notes guide layer. Default true. |
| `backgroundLayer` | boolean | no | Add a Background solid. Default: when the other scenes have one. |
| `renumber` | boolean | no | Renumber the scene comps in sequence order. Default true when they are numbered. |
| `rippleOtherLayers` | boolean | no | Also move non-scene layers that start at or after the insertion point. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "insert-scene",
  "arguments": {
    "name": "My Layer"
  }
}
```

### list-scenes

Reads a master composition's scene sequence in time order: each scene's comp name and id, its layer, start, in and out points, duration, the comp's own duration, notes (the comp comment) and planned transition, plus the gaps and overlaps between neighbours and problems such as trimmed or time-stretched scene layers. Use when: checking a plan before or after changes, finding a scene's position or comp id for set-scene-duration, insert-scene or add-transition. Do not use for: every layer of a comp (list-layers) or transitions (list-transitions). Inputs: comp, the master (default the active composition). Returns: the composition, the scenes folder, count, scenes [{position, name, compId, layer, inPoint, outPoint, duration, frames, gapBeforeFrames, notes, transition}], gaps, overlaps, issues, unplaced scene comps, transitions at the cuts and the total length. Notes: read-only. Scenes are the precomp layers whose comps sit in the plan's scenes folder; in a master built by hand, every full-frame precomp layer that is not a guide. Example: comp {name: "Launch Film"}.

Pack: scenes. Read-only. Bridge command: `listScenes`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-scenes",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### remove-scene

Takes a scene out of the master: removes the transitions built on its cuts, deletes its layer, shifts the later scenes back so the next scene starts where it started (overlaps kept), renumbers the scene comps, and can delete the scene composition when nothing else uses it. Use when: a beat is cut from the film. Do not use for: hiding a scene for a preview (set-layer-flags enabled false) or shortening it (set-scene-duration). Inputs: comp (the master); scene by position, name, {compId} or {layer}; deleteComp (default false: the comp stays in the scenes folder and list-scenes reports it as unplaced); renumber (default true when numbered); rippleOtherLayers (default true). Returns: the removed scene, whether its comp was deleted, the transitions removed with their arguments, layers that started inside the removed span (left in place), the layers moved and the new sequence. Notes: layers that started inside the removed scene are not moved or deleted; check them. Undoable in one step. Example: comp {name: "Launch Film"}, scene 3, deleteComp true.

Pack: scenes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `removeScene`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `scene` | integer \| string \| {compId?: integer, layer?: object} | yes | Which scene: its position, its name, {compId} or {layer}. |
| `deleteComp` | boolean | no | Also delete the scene composition when no other comp uses it. Default false. |
| `renumber` | boolean | no | Renumber the remaining scene comps. Default true when they are numbered. |
| `rippleOtherLayers` | boolean | no | Also move non-scene layers that start at or after the next scene. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "remove-scene",
  "arguments": {
    "scene": 1
  }
}
```

### set-scene-duration

Changes one scene's length: its composition's duration and its layer in the master, then shifts every later scene by the difference so the sequence stays gapless with its overlaps kept. Layers inside the scene that ran to its old end are extended, later layers and comp markers of the master move too, and transitions built by add-transition or carry-element on this scene's cuts are removed and built again at the new cuts. Use when: a scene needs more or less time, or the plan changed. Do not use for: speeding footage up (set-speed) or moving one layer (set-layer-timing). Inputs: comp (the master); scene by position, name, {compId} or {layer}; duration in seconds or durationFrames; extendLayers (default true); rippleOtherLayers (default true: non-scene layers that start at or after the old end move with the scenes). Returns: the scene before and after, the change in frames, the layers and markers moved, the transitions rebuilt and the new sequence. Notes: assumes scene layers play at 100 % speed. A hand-trimmed scene layer gets its full new length. Undoable in one step. Example: comp {name: "Launch Film"}, scene "Feature", duration 5.

Pack: scenes. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `setSceneDuration`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `scene` | integer \| string \| {compId?: integer, layer?: object} | yes | Which scene: its position, its name, {compId} or {layer}. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Snapped to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `extendLayers` | boolean | no | Extend layers inside the scene comp that reached its old end. Default true. |
| `rippleOtherLayers` | boolean | no | Also move non-scene layers that start at or after the old end of the scene. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "set-scene-duration",
  "arguments": {
    "scene": 1
  }
}
```

## Transitions

### add-transition

Joins an outgoing and an incoming layer at a cut with eased keys on their own transforms: match-cut (outgoing pushes in accelerating, incoming opens oversized and decelerates, cut on the fastest frame), zoom-through (fast scale-up with a blur peak), push (both slide edge to edge at matched speed), shape-reveal (a growing circle or rounded rectangle track matte), blur-swap or whip (fast pan, mirrored edges). Use when: going from one scene layer to the next in a master. Do not use for: element entrances (add-entrance) or keeping one element across a cut alone (carry-element). Inputs: outgoing, incoming; kind (default the planned kind, else match-cut); time or frame of the cut (default the incoming in point; push defaults to the middle of the overlap); framesOut, framesIn; direction; intensity (default 1); origin; move scale or position (match-cut); shape and roundness (shape-reveal); carry; replace. Returns: the transition record (id, kind, cut, window), keys per layer, effects, helper layers and notes. Notes: trims at the cut, turns motion blur on, keeps scenes covering the frame. Push and shape-reveal need an overlap. Undoable; remove-transition undoes it. Example: outgoing {name: "01 Intro"}, incoming {name: "02 Feature"}, kind "match-cut", carry {outgoing: {name: "Send"}, incoming: {name: "Chip"}}.

Pack: transitions. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addTransition`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `outgoing` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The layer that leaves (usually the earlier scene layer). |
| `incoming` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The layer that arrives (usually the next scene layer). |
| `kind` | "match-cut" \| "zoom-through" \| "push" \| "shape-reveal" \| "blur-swap" \| "whip" | no | Default: the kind planned for the outgoing scene in create-scene-plan, else match-cut. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `framesOut` | integer | no | Frames of the outgoing move before the cut. Default about 0.33 s (match-cut, zoom-through), 0.3 s (push), 0.25 s (blur-swap), 0.2 s (whip). Not used by shape-reveal. |
| `framesIn` | integer | no | Frames of the incoming move after the cut. Default about 0.33 s, 0.5 s for shape-reveal, 0.27 s for whip. |
| `direction` | "left" \| "right" \| "up" \| "down" | no | Direction the picture travels. Default left (the outgoing leaves to the left, the incoming comes in from the right). |
| `intensity` | number | no | Scales the amount: match-cut push-in to 250 % at 1, zoom-through 400 %, blur peaks 40 px, whip travel 0.75 of the frame. Default 1. Not used by push or shape-reveal. |
| `origin` | [number, number] \| {layer: object, from?: "comp" \| "outgoing" \| "incoming"} | no | Point the scale pivots on or the reveal grows from: [x, y], or {layer, from}. Default the composition centre. |
| `move` | "scale" \| "position" | no | match-cut only: push in with Scale (default) or slide with Position. |
| `shape` | "circle" \| "rounded-rectangle" | no | shape-reveal only. Default circle. |
| `roundness` | number | no | shape-reveal rounded rectangle corner radius in pixels. Default 32 at 1080 lines. |
| `carry` | {outgoing: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, incoming: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, layer?: boolean, source?: "outgoing" \| "incoming", name?: string} | no | An element kept continuous across the cut. For match-cut it also sets the pivots and the incoming scale so the two elements line up at the cut. |
| `replace` | boolean | no | Remove an existing transition between the same two layers first. Default false (an existing one is an error). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "add-transition",
  "arguments": {
    "outgoing": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "incoming": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### carry-element

Keeps one element continuous across a cut: copies the element (or uses a layer you name) into this composition above both scene layers, keys it from its pose in the outgoing scene to its counterpart's pose in the incoming scene (or a target pose), and trims the two originals in their scene comps so they hide during the handover. Use when: a button, logo or card must stay one object while the scenes around it cut. Do not use for: the scene layers' own moves (add-transition, whose carry option calls this). Inputs: outgoing {scene, layer}; incoming {scene, layer} or {pose: {position, scale, rotation}}; time or frame of the cut (default the incoming scene in point); framesOut, framesIn (default 0.33 s); source; layer (an existing carry layer); name; cutProgress. Returns: the record id, the carry layer, poses at start, cut and end, whether the scenes line up, keys and trims. Notes: three keys per property, accelerating into the cut and landing after it, the speed passing through the cut frame; poses are read with toComp through both comps. Undoable; remove-transition removes it. Example: outgoing {scene: {name: "01 Intro"}, layer: {name: "Logo"}}, incoming {scene: {name: "02 Feature"}, layer: {name: "Logo"}}.

Pack: transitions. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `carryElement`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `outgoing` | {scene?: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, layer?: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}} | yes |  |
| `incoming` | {scene?: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, layer?: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, pose?: {position: [number, number], scale?: number, rotation?: number}} | yes |  |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `framesOut` | integer | no | Frames before the cut. Default about 0.33 s. |
| `framesIn` | integer | no | Frames after the cut. Default about 0.33 s. |
| `source` | "outgoing" \| "incoming" | no | Which element the carry layer copies. Default outgoing. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Use this existing layer of the composition as the carry instead of a copy. |
| `name` | string | no | Carry layer name. Default "<element> Carry". |
| `cutProgress` | number | no | Share of the move done at the cut when the two scenes do not line up. Default framesOut / (framesOut + framesIn). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "carry-element",
  "arguments": {
    "outgoing": {},
    "incoming": {},
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### list-transitions

Lists the transitions and carried elements that add-transition and carry-element built in a composition, read from the records they store in layer markers at each cut, and checks that their layers still exist. Use when: before remove-transition (to get the id), after a scene sequence changed, or to see which cuts already have a transition. Do not use for: the scene sequence (list-scenes) or markers in general (list-markers). Inputs: comp (default the active composition). Returns: count and transitions [{id, kind, cutTime, cutFrame, startFrame, endFrame, framesOut, framesIn, outgoing, incoming (each with exists), helpers (matte and carry layers), effects, keyCount, anchor, args}], and orphanHelpers: layers tagged as transition helpers whose record is gone. Notes: read-only. Records move with their layers, so a rippled sequence still lists the right cuts. Transitions made by hand are not listed. Example: comp {name: "Launch Film"}.

Pack: transitions. Read-only. Bridge command: `listTransitions`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-transitions",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### remove-transition

Undoes one transition (or every one) built by add-transition or carry-element, using its record: removes exactly the keys it added (keys another transition shares stay), the effects it named, the matte and carry layers it created, restores the in and out points it trimmed (on the scene layers and on carried elements inside their scene comps), the static values and motion blur switches it changed, and deletes the record marker. Use when: trying another kind at a cut, or before re-timing by hand. Do not use for: deleting scene layers (remove-scene) or keys in general (delete-keyframes-in-range). Inputs: id from list-transitions, or all true. Returns: removed [{id, kind, keysRemoved, effectsRemoved, layersDeleted, trimsRestored}], the remaining count and notes about anything already missing. Notes: a layer the user deleted is skipped with a note. Composition motion blur is switched back off only when no transition remains and it was off before. Undoable in one step. Example: id "t4k2a1b".

Pack: transitions. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `removeTransition`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `id` | string | no | Transition id from list-transitions or add-transition. |
| `all` | boolean | no | Remove every transition and carry in the composition. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "remove-transition",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

## UI demo kit

### add-toast

Adds a notification toast: a rounded card with an accent dot, a title and an optional body, sized to its text, that slides in from the nearest frame edge with two eased keys, rests, and slides out with two accelerating keys. Use when: a demo shows a confirmation, an alert or a "saved" message. Do not use for: a label pointing at part of a screen (highlight-region) or a pill that wraps changing text (add-text-box). Inputs: title; body; position top-right (default), top, top-left, bottom-right, bottom or bottom-left; margin (default 32); width (default 420); padding; theme light or dark; accentColor; titleSize and bodySize; font and titleFont; cornerRadius; time or frame; duration (default 0.5 s) or durationFrames; hold (default 2.5 s); exit (default true); exitDuration (default 0.35 s); easing; shadow; name. Returns: the card and text layers, the card size, rest and start positions, the times and the keys. Notes: the texts are parented to the card, so only the card is keyed; the card is a screen overlay that a camera null leaves alone. Undoable in one step. Example: title "Export finished", body "Your video is ready to share.", frame 150, position "top-right".

Pack: ui-demo. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `addToast`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `title` | string | yes | Bold first line of the toast. |
| `body` | string | no | Optional second line under the title. |
| `position` | "top-right" \| "top" \| "top-left" \| "bottom-right" \| "bottom" \| "bottom-left" | no |  |
| `margin` | number | no | Pixels from the frame edges. Default 32. |
| `width` | number | no | Card width in pixels. Default 420. |
| `padding` | number | no | Inner padding in pixels. Default 20. |
| `theme` | "light" \| "dark" | no | Default light. |
| `accentColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour of the dot. Default #2F6BFF. |
| `titleSize` | number | no | Default 22. |
| `bodySize` | number | no | Default 18. |
| `font` | string | no | PostScript font for the body (and the title unless titleFont is given). |
| `titleFont` | string | no | PostScript name of the title font. Default: the body font in bold where available. |

| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |

| `exit` | boolean | no | Slide out after the hold. Default true. |
| `exitDuration` | number | no | Seconds of the slide out. Default 0.35. |

Default landing: speed 0, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving. |

### cursor-journey

Moves a cursor through waypoints with one eased Position key per stop and clicks with the press mechanism: the target dips, or pops to 108 % for a state change, and the cursor dips deeper, with an optional click ring. Builds a clean arrow cursor (white, dark outline, soft layer-style shadow) when none is given and keeps it on top. Use when: a product demo needs a pointer that travels to buttons, fields and rows and clicks them. Do not use for: a press without travel (press) or a hand-drawn route (move-along-path). Inputs: waypoints [{to [x, y] or {layer, anchor, offset}, at or frame, click, hold, pressTarget, variant, scale, flip, easing}]; cursor (its anchor is the tip) or cursorStyle; arc (0.1 reads natural); clickRing; clickDelay; cursorScale (default 92); easing (default landing). Returns: the cursor, its Position keys, the resolved waypoints and each click with times and keys. Notes: speed 0 at every stop; a click holds the cursor through the press; targets are measured at arrival; a new cursor rides an existing UI Camera null. Undoable in one step. Example: waypoints [{to: [240, 980], at: 0.5}, {to: {layer: {name: "Send"}}, at: 1.4, click: true}], arc 0.1, clickRing true.

Pack: ui-demo. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `cursorJourney`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `waypoints` | {to: [number, number] \| object, at?: number, frame?: integer, click?: boolean, hold?: number, pressTarget?: boolean, variant?: "dip" \| "pop", scale?: number, flip?: object[], easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object}[] | yes |  |
| `cursor` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | An existing cursor layer. Its anchor point is the tip. Default: build an arrow cursor. |
| `cursorStyle` | {name?: string, height?: number, fillColor?: string \| number[] \| object, outlineColor?: string \| number[] \| object, outlineWidth?: number, shadow?: boolean \| object} | no | Look of the cursor that is built when cursor is not given. |
| `arc` | number | no | Bow of each travel segment as a fraction of its length; positive bows up (left on vertical moves). Default 0, straight. |
| `clickRing` | boolean \| {radius?: number, strokeWidth?: number, color?: string \| number[] \| object, opacity?: number, style?: "ring" \| "fill", duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer} | no | true for a soft ring at each click point, or its settings. |
| `clickDelay` | number | no | Seconds between arriving and pressing. Default 0. |
| `cursorScale` | number | no | Cursor scale at the bottom of each click, percent. Default 92. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Arrival at every waypoint. Default landing: speed 0, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving. |
| `keepOnTop` | boolean | no | Move the cursor to the top of the stack. Default true. |
| `rideCamera` | boolean | no | Parent a newly built cursor to an existing UI Camera or Camera Push null. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "cursor-journey",
  "arguments": {
    "waypoints": [
      {
        "to": [
          0.5,
          0.5
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### device-frame

Wraps a screen layer in a clean device frame drawn with shape layers and sized to the screen: a browser window (chrome bar, three dots, address pill with optional URL text), a phone (rounded body, bezel, optional island), a tablet or a laptop (screen and base), light or dark, with a soft layer-style shadow. Use when: a screenshot or screen precomp should read as an app on a device. Do not use for: a plain rounded card (create-shape-layer) or a window that scrolls (scroll-in-window first, then frame its window layer). Inputs: layer (the screen); device browser, phone, tablet or laptop; theme light or dark; color (frame colour); cornerRadius; url and font (browser); dots mono or color; notch (phone); shadow; name; component true or {name, templateName}; time or frame for measuring. Returns: the frame layers, the sizes used, the mask that rounds the screen, colour expressions, the shadow result and the component when made. Notes: parts are parented to the screen; derived colours follow the frame colour by expression, so one Theme Colour Essential Property recolours everything. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "App Screen"}, device "browser", url "app.example.com", component true.

### focus-on-region

Frames a region of a layer so it fills a share of the composition, centred, by keying a 2D camera null: Scale and Position in two eased keys, with an optional hold and return. Reuses a UI Camera or Camera Push null when there is one; otherwise creates one and parents every unparented layer to it once. Use when: the camera should zoom into the part of a screen that matters, then hold or come back. Do not use for: a slow push over a whole scene (add-camera-push) or 3D camera moves (animate-camera). Inputs: layer; rect in its pixels (default its bounds); padding (default 24 px); fill (default 0.7 of width or height, whichever binds); time or frame; duration (default 0.8 s); easing; return true or {hold, duration, easing}; holdPush; camera; name; exclude; maxScale. Returns: the null (created or reused), layers parented and skipped, the scale, and the keys. Notes: the region is measured at the landing; screen overlays stay unparented; a camera push carries on from the new framing; overlapping moves are refused. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Dashboard"}, rect {x: 820, y: 140, width: 560, height: 320}, frame 90, durationFrames 48, return {hold: 1.5}.

Pack: ui-demo. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `focusOnRegion`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The layer holding the region, or the element to frame. |
| `rect` | {x: number, y: number, width: number, height: number} | no | A rectangle in the layer's own pixels, measured from the top-left of its content (for images and precomps, the source pixels). Default: the whole layer. |
| `padding` | number | no | Pixels of room around the region. Default 24. |
| `fill` | number | no | Share of the frame the padded region fills, on the axis that binds. Default 0.7. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds of the move in. Default 0.8. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Default camera landing: speed 0, influence 33.3 leaving and 100 arriving. |
| `return` | boolean \| {hold?: number, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object} | no | true (hold 1 s, return as long as the move in) or settings. Default: stay on the region. |
| `holdPush` | number | no | Percent the zoom keeps growing over the hold, so the held frame stays alive. Default 0. |
| `camera` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Use this null as the camera. |
| `name` | string | no | Name of the camera null to find or create. Default "UI Camera". |
| `exclude` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}[] | no | Layers that must not ride a newly created camera null (captions, a fixed logo). |
| `maxScale` | number | no | Highest scale in percent. Default 800. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "focus-on-region",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### highlight-region

Draws attention to a part of a screen: dims everything else with a solid whose inverted, feathered rounded-rectangle mask leaves the region clear, outlines the region with a rounded stroke that contracts onto it, and adds an optional label in a pill with a line to it; everything reveals with eased keys and can hide again. Use when: a feature, button or number on a screen needs pointing out. Do not use for: moving the camera there (focus-on-region) or a fixed rectangle (create-shape-layer). Inputs: layer; rect in its pixels (default its bounds); padding (default 8); cornerRadius (default 12); dim true, false or {color, opacity, feather}; outline true, false or {color, width, opacity, grow}; label text or {text, side, distance, fontSize, font, textColor, fillColor, line, shadow}; time or frame; duration (default 0.4 s); hideTime or hideFrame; follow; above; name. Returns: the layers, the region in composition pixels, the keys, and every expression with any error. Notes: overlays stay fixed to the frame; follow true tracks the region through scrolls, parents and camera moves by expression. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Dashboard"}, rect {x: 40, y: 220, width: 380, height: 180}, frame 120, label "Live revenue", hideFrame 300.

Pack: ui-demo. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `highlightRegion`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The layer holding the region. |
| `rect` | {x: number, y: number, width: number, height: number} | no | A rectangle in the layer's own pixels, measured from the top-left of its content (for images and precomps, the source pixels). Default: the whole layer. |
| `padding` | number | no | Pixels added around the region. Default 8. |
| `cornerRadius` | number | no | Corner radius of the region, pixels; the outline adds the padding to stay concentric. Default 12. |
| `dim` | boolean \| {color?: string \| number[] \| object, opacity?: number, feather?: number} | no | Darken the rest of the frame. Default true. |
| `outline` | boolean \| {color?: string \| number[] \| object, width?: number, opacity?: number, grow?: number} | no | Rounded outline around the region. Default true. |
| `label` | string \| {text: string, side?: "auto" \| "top" \| "bottom" \| "left" \| "right", distance?: number, fontSize?: number, font?: string, textColor?: string \| number[] \| object, fillColor?: string \| number[] \| object, line?: boolean, lineColor?: string \| number[] \| object, lineWidth?: number, shadow?: boolean \| object} | no | A callout in a pill, with a line to the region. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds of each reveal. Default 0.4; parts follow each other by 3 frames at 60 fps. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Default landing: speed 0, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving. |
| `hideTime` | number | no | Seconds when the hide starts. Default: no hide. |
| `hideFrame` | integer | no | Frame; wins over hideTime. |
| `hideDuration` | number | no | Seconds of the hide. Default 0.3. |
| `hideDurationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over hideDuration. |
| `follow` | boolean | no | Track the region every frame (scroll, parents, camera). Default true; false pins the overlays where the region is at the reveal. |
| `above` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Stack the overlays directly above this layer. Default: the top, under a cursor made by cursor-journey. |
| `name` | string | no | Base name of the overlay layers. Default "Highlight". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "highlight-region",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### scroll-in-window

Scrolls a tall screen (image, footage or precomp) inside a window: the screen moves into a precomposition the size of the window, fitted to its width, and a "Scroll" slider in page pixels drives its Position by expression; each stop is one eased key on the slider. Use when: a web page or app screen scrolls inside a browser, phone or panel. Do not use for: moving the whole screen (animate-property) or framing a part of it (focus-on-region). Inputs: layer; window {x, y, width, height} in composition pixels, or windowLayer with inset; cornerRadius; fit width or keep; name; stops [{to pixels, "top" or "bottom", or fraction 0 to 1, at or frame, hold, easing}]; momentum (flick and glide); sliderName. Returns: the window composition and layer, the screen inside, the scroll range, the slider path and keys. Notes: a precomp clips by its bounds, so later moves, parents and camera pushes need no matte kept in step. Calling again on the screen inside adds stops. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Pricing Page"}, window {x: 360, y: 140, width: 1200, height: 800}, stops [{to: "top", at: 0.5}, {to: 1400, at: 2, hold: 0.8}, {to: "bottom", at: 4}].

Pack: ui-demo. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `scrollInWindow`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The tall screen layer, or the screen inside a window made earlier. |
| `window` | {x: number, y: number, width: number, height: number} | no | The visible region in composition pixels: top-left corner and size. |
| `windowLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Use this layer's content bounds as the window (a window body or a device screen); the window layer is parented to it and stacked above it. |
| `inset` | number | no | Pixels taken off each side of windowLayer's bounds. Default 0. |
| `cornerRadius` | number | no | Rounded corners on the window, pixels (a mask on the window layer). Default 0. |
| `fit` | "width" \| "keep" | no | width (default) scales the screen to the window width; keep keeps its current scale, centred. |
| `name` | string | no | Name of the window composition. Default "<layer> Window". |
| `stops` | {to?: number \| "top" \| "bottom", fraction?: number, at?: number, frame?: integer, hold?: number, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object}[] | yes |  |
| `momentum` | boolean | no | Each move launches fast and glides into its stop (cubic-bezier 0.16, 1, 0.3, 1). Default false: landing ease. |
| `sliderName` | string | no | Name of the Slider Control. Default "Scroll". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "scroll-in-window",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "stops": [
      {}
    ]
  }
}
```

## Motion rigs

### clone-array

Duplicates a layer N times (or takes given copies) and offsets each copy by its number from controls on a null: Step X, Step Y (Step Z), Rotation Step, Scale Step, Opacity Step and Time Offset in frames, or in ring mode Center, Radius, Radius Step, Start Angle, Angle Step and Orient. Copies read the source live, so editing the source's keys updates every copy. Use when: a row, fan, spiral or ring of one element, or a staggered echo of one animated layer. Do not use for: shapes repeated inside one shape layer (add-repeater) or different layers in a grid (grid-layout). Inputs: layer (the source); count 1 to 200, or copies; positionStep [x, y] or [x, y, z] (default the source's width plus 24 in x); rotationStep degrees; scaleStep percent; opacityStep points; timeOffsetFrames; radial {radius, startAngle, angleStep, center, radiusStep, orient}; delayContent time-remaps precomp and footage copies; stack below or above; controlLayer, controlName. Returns: the control layer and controls, per copy its number and expression states, cleared keys and skipped layers. Notes: in ring mode the source sits on the ring too. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Dot"}, count 11, radial {radius: 240, orient: true}, timeOffsetFrames 2.

Pack: motion-rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `cloneArray`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The source layer. Its name must be unique. |
| `count` | integer | no | Copies to make with duplicate. |
| `copies` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | no | Use these layers as copies 1, 2, 3 instead of duplicating. |
| `order` | "top-down" \| "bottom-up" \| "random" \| "as-given" | no | Order of given copies: as-given (default), top-down, bottom-up or random. |
| `positionStep` | [number, number] \| [number, number, number] | no | Pixels added per copy, [x, y] or [x, y, z]. |
| `rotationStep` | number | no | Degrees added per copy. |
| `scaleStep` | number | no | Scale percent added per copy (toward 0 for mirrored layers). |
| `opacityStep` | number | no | Opacity points added per copy, for example -10. |
| `timeOffsetFrames` | number | no | Frames each copy runs behind the one before. Default 0. |
| `radial` | {radius?: number, startAngle?: number, angleStep?: number, center?: [number, number], radiusStep?: number, orient?: boolean} | no | Place the source and copies on a ring. |
| `delayContent` | boolean | no | Also delay what plays inside precomp and footage copies (Time Remap by expression). Default false. |
| `stack` | "below" \| "above" | no | New copies go below the source in order (default) or above it. |
| `controlLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Put the controls on this existing layer (for example a null shared with another rig) instead of a new null. Controls with the same names are reused and keep their values unless given. |
| `controlName` | string | no | Name of the new control null. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "clone-array",
  "arguments": {
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### fit-to-box

Scales layers so their content fits a box, by expression: Scale comes from the layer's bounds (sourceRectAtTime) and the controls Box Width, Box Height, Upscale and Scale Offset, so text that changes keeps fitting and the box can be animated. Use when: a logo, label or icon must fit a button, a card slot or a frame whatever its size. Do not use for: font size (fit-text-to-box), grid cells (grid-layout with fit) or filling the composition (fit-layer-to-composition). Inputs: layers; size [width, height] px; mode contain (default, the smaller ratio), cover, width or height; upscale (default false, only shrink); offset in percent points added; controls on the layer (default for one layer) or a null (default for several), or controlLayer. Returns: the control layer and controls, per layer its bounds now, the scale it gets now and the expression state. Notes: the layer scales about its anchor point, so the anchor decides which edge stays put. Undoable in one step. Example: layers [{name: "Logo"}], size [320, 120].

Pack: motion-rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `fitToBox`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | yes | Layers: an array of layer refs, {selected: true}, {all: true}, {indices: [...]} or {names: [...]}. |
| `order` | "top-down" \| "bottom-up" \| "random" \| "as-given" | no |  |
| `size` | [number, number] | yes | [width, height] of the box in pixels. |
| `mode` | "contain" \| "cover" \| "width" \| "height" | no | contain (default): fit inside. cover: fill the box. width or height: match one side. |
| `upscale` | boolean | no | Also scale small content up. Default false. |
| `offset` | number | no | Percent points added to the fitted scale. Default 0. |
| `controls` | "layer" \| "null" | no | On the layer (default for one layer) or on a new null shared by all (default for several). |
| `controlLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Put the controls on this existing layer (for example a null shared with another rig) instead of a new null. Controls with the same names are reused and keep their values unless given. |
| `controlName` | string | no | Name of the new control null. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "fit-to-box",
  "arguments": {
    "layers": [
      {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    ],
    "size": [
      0.5,
      0.5
    ]
  }
}
```

### follow-chain

Makes a list of layers repeat a leader's motion a few frames later, each step later than the one before (valueAtTime(time - delay x step)), on position, rotation, scale and/or opacity, with an optional decay that shrinks the motion per step. Delay (frames) and Decay (percent) are slider controls on the leader or a null. Use when: a trail of dots, cards or letters follows a lead element, or one keyed move should echo down a stack. Do not use for: copies made from one layer (clone-array) or separate staggered entrances (add-entrance). Inputs: leader; followers in order (as-given by default, or order); properties (default position); delayFrames (default 3); decay 0 to 100 (default 0); mode offset (each keeps its layout and adds the leader's motion, default) or absolute (each moves onto the leader's delayed value); controls leader or null, or controlLayer. Returns: the control layer and controls, per follower its step, space and expression states, and skipped layers. Notes: key the leader once; changing its keys updates the chain. A follower with another parent follows in composition or world space. Undoable in one step. Example: leader {name: "Dot"}, followers [{name: "Dot 2"}, {name: "Dot 3"}], delayFrames 4, decay 15.

Pack: motion-rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `followChain`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `leader` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | yes | The layer the others follow. Its name must be unique. |
| `followers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | yes | The followers, step 1 first. |
| `order` | "top-down" \| "bottom-up" \| "random" \| "as-given" | no | Step order: as-given (default), top-down, bottom-up or random. |
| `properties` | "position" \| "rotation" \| "scale" \| "opacity"[] | no | What follows. Default ["position"]. |
| `delayFrames` | number | no | Frames between one step and the next. Default 3. |
| `decay` | number | no | Percent of the motion lost per step. Default 0. |
| `mode` | "offset" \| "absolute" | no | offset (default): own value plus the leader's delayed change. absolute: the leader's delayed value itself. |
| `controls` | "leader" \| "null" | no | Where Delay and Decay go: on the leader (default) or a new null. |
| `controlLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Put the controls on this existing layer (for example a null shared with another rig) instead of a new null. Controls with the same names are reused and keep their values unless given. |
| `controlName` | string | no | Name of the new control null. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "follow-chain",
  "arguments": {
    "leader": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    },
    "followers": [
      {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### grid-layout

Arranges layers in a grid or bento layout driven by controls on a null (Columns, Cell Width, Cell Height, Gap X, Gap Y, Origin, Center On Origin, Column First, Reverse Order, Rows for bento). Each Position expression puts the centre of the layer's bounds on its cell, so changing a control reflows the grid; a fractional Columns glides cells between layouts. Use when: cards, tiles, icons or photos form a grid or bento board. Do not use for: copies of one layer in a row (clone-array) or spacing layers once (distribute-layers). Inputs: layers (top-down, or order); columns (default the square root); cellSize [w, h] (default the largest layer); gap number or [x, y] (default 24); origin [x, y] with align center or top-left; fill rows or columns; reverse; fit none, contain, cover or stretch; bento [{layer, col, row, colSpan, rowSpan}] 1-based; animate {control, to, start, duration, easing}; controlLayer, controlName. Returns: controls, layout, each layer's slot, column, row and fill, expression states and animation keys. Notes: a shape layer holding one Rectangle fills its cell through the rectangle's Size. One undo step. Example: layers {all: true}, columns 3, gap 24, animate {control: "columns", to: 2, startFrame: 60}.

Pack: motion-rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `gridLayout`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | no | The cells. Required unless every bento entry names its layer. |
| `order` | "top-down" \| "bottom-up" \| "random" \| "as-given" | no | Which layer takes slot 1: top-down (timeline order, default), bottom-up, random or as-given. |
| `columns` | integer | no | Number of columns. Default the square root of the count, rounded up; for bento the widest span. |
| `cellSize` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] of one cell in pixels. Default the largest layer (fit none) or a size that fills 80 % of the composition. |
| `gap` | number \| [number, number] | no | Space between cells in pixels: one number or [x, y]. Default 24. |
| `origin` | [number, number] | no | Composition pixels: the grid centre (align center) or top-left corner (align top-left). Default the composition centre, or a centred grid. |
| `align` | "center" \| "top-left" | no | What origin marks. Default center. |
| `fill` | "rows" \| "columns" | no | Fill left to right row by row (default) or top to bottom column by column. |
| `reverse` | boolean | no | Fill from the last slot. Default false. |
| `fit` | "none" \| "contain" \| "cover" \| "stretch" | no | Scale each layer into its cell: none (grid default), contain (bento default), cover or stretch. |
| `bento` | {layer?: object, col: integer, row: integer, colSpan?: integer, rowSpan?: integer}[] | no | Explicit cells per layer for a bento layout. |
| `animate` | {control: "columns" \| "rows" \| "cell-size" \| "gap" \| "origin", to: number \| [number, number], startTime?: number, startFrame?: integer, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object} | no | A reflow: two eased keys on one control. |
| `controlLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Put the controls on this existing layer (for example a null shared with another rig) instead of a new null. Controls with the same names are reused and keep their values unless given. |
| `controlName` | string | no | Name of the new control null. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "grid-layout",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### number-counter

Makes a text layer count from one number to another: a Value slider keyed with two eased keys drives Source Text by expression, formatted with decimals, a thousands separator, prefix, suffix, leading zeros and an optional plus sign. Use when: a statistic, price, percentage or score ticks up or down. Do not use for: swapping words (set-text) or typing text on (typewriter-text). Inputs: layer (an existing text layer, style kept) or font, fontSize, fillColor, justification, position and name for a new one; from (default 0) and to; startTime or startFrame (default the in point); duration (default 2 s) or durationFrames; easing (default a landing); decimals; thousandsSeparator ("" for none); decimalSeparator; prefix and suffix such as "$", "%" or "k"; minDigits (leading zeros); showPlus; tabular (figure-space padding holds the width); controls on the layer (default) or a null, or controlLayer. Returns: the text layer, the control layer, the controls (Value with its keys, Decimals, Min Digits, Separator), the expression state and the first and last strings. Notes: prefix, suffix and separators sit in the expression's first lines; past one million a multiplier is used. Undoable in one step. Example: layer {name: "Revenue"}, to 12500, prefix "$", durationFrames 90.

Pack: motion-rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `numberCounter`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Existing text layer to drive; its font and style are kept. Omit to create a text layer. |
| `from` | number | no | Start value. Default 0. |
| `to` | number | yes | End value. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds from the composition start. Default: the layer in point. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default 2. Key times snap to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Arrival of the Value keys. Default a landing (speed 0, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving, no overshoot). |
| `decimals` | integer | no | Digits after the decimal point. Default 0. |
| `thousandsSeparator` | string | no | Between groups of three digits. Default ","; "" for none. |
| `decimalSeparator` | string | no | Default ".". |
| `prefix` | string | no | Text before the number, for example "$". |
| `suffix` | string | no | Text after the number, for example "%", "k" or " users". |
| `minDigits` | integer | no | Pad the whole part with leading zeros to this many digits (3 gives 007). Default 0. |
| `showPlus` | boolean | no | Put + before positive values. Default false. |
| `tabular` | boolean | no | Pad the left with figure spaces to the widest end value so the width holds while counting. Best with a font whose figures are tabular. Default false. |
| `controls` | "layer" \| "null" | no | Where the controls go: on the text layer (default) or on a new null. |
| `controlLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Put the controls on this existing layer (for example a null shared with another rig) instead of a new null. Controls with the same names are reused and keep their values unless given. |
| `controlName` | string | no | Name of the new control null. |
| `font` | string | no | New layer only: PostScript font name. |
| `fontSize` | number | no | New layer only: pixels. Default 72. |
| `fillColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | New layer only. Default white. |
| `justification` | "left" \| "center" \| "right" | no | New layer only. Default center. |
| `position` | [number, number] | no | New layer only: composition pixels. Default the centre. |
| `name` | string | no | New layer only: layer name. Default "Counter". |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "number-counter",
  "arguments": {
    "to": 0.5,
    "layer": {
      "name": "My Layer"
    }
  }
}
```

### proximity-effector

Makes layers react to their distance from an effector layer: inside a Radius with a linear or smooth Falloff they scale, fade, blur, turn or move by amounts set on controls on the effector (Radius, Falloff, Smooth Falloff, Invert, Strength, one Amount per reaction). Animate the effector; the field responds. Use when: a cursor or orb passes over tiles, icons or letters. Do not use for: trailing a leader (follow-chain) or aiming at a target (look-at-layer). Inputs: layers; effector (an existing layer) or name and position for a new null (3D when a layer is 3D); radius px (default 300); falloff, percent of the radius that fades (default 100); smooth; invert; strength percent; scale percent added; opacity points added; blur px (Gaussian Blur "Effector Blur"); rotation degrees; offset [x, y] or [x, y, z] px; animate {from, to, start, duration, easing} keys the effector; radiusGuide draws the radius (guide layer). Returns: effector, controls, per-layer expression states, skipped layers, effector keys. Notes: stacks on grid-layout and other rig expressions; a re-run replaces only its own part. 3D layers measure in world space. Example: layers {all: true}, scale 40, blur 12, radius 250, animate {to: [1720, 540], duration: 3}.

Pack: motion-rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `proximityEffector`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | yes | Layers: an array of layer refs, {selected: true}, {all: true}, {indices: [...]} or {names: [...]}. |
| `effector` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Existing layer to use as the effector; its controls go on it. Default a new null. |
| `name` | string | no | Name of a new effector null. Default "Effector". |
| `position` | [number, number] \| [number, number, number] | no | Position of a new effector in composition pixels. Default the centre. |
| `threeD` | boolean | no | Make a new effector 3D even when every layer is 2D. Default false. |
| `radius` | number | no | Reach in pixels. Default 300. |
| `falloff` | number | no | Percent of the radius over which the reaction fades out: 100 fades from the centre, 0 is a hard edge. Default 100. |
| `smooth` | boolean | no | Smooth (S-shaped) falloff instead of linear. Default true. |
| `invert` | boolean | no | React outside the radius instead of inside. Default false. |
| `strength` | number | no | Percent applied to every reaction. Default 100; key it to fade the field in. |
| `scale` | number | no | Percent added to the layer's scale at full weight, for example 40 (140 %) or -50. |
| `opacity` | number | no | Opacity points added at full weight, for example -100 to vanish or 100 to appear. |
| `blur` | number | no | Blur in pixels at full weight. |
| `rotation` | number | no | Degrees added to Rotation at full weight. |
| `offset` | [number, number] \| [number, number, number] | no | Position offset in pixels at full weight, [x, y] or [x, y, z]. |
| `animate` | {from?: [number, number] \| [number, number, number], to: [number, number] \| [number, number, number], startTime?: number, startFrame?: integer, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object} | no | Two eased keys on the effector's Position. |
| `radiusGuide` | boolean | no | Add a guide shape layer (does not render) that draws the radius. Default false. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "proximity-effector",
  "arguments": {
    "layers": [
      {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### shape-morph

Morphs one shape layer path into another between two times: reads both paths, gives the one with fewer vertices extra vertices on its longest segments (shape and corners stay exact), turns the other so each vertex travels the least, and writes two eased Path keys on the from path. Resampling and matching run inside After Effects in the same command (one round trip, one undo step); the result is two ordinary path keys to edit. Use when: an icon becomes another, a card outline becomes a pill, a blob reshapes. Do not use for: masks (set-mask-path-keyframe) or moving along a path (move-along-path). Inputs: from {layer, path}, the path that gets the keys; to {layer, path}, the target (rectangles, ellipses, stars and polygons are converted); path, like "Path 1" or "Group 1/Path 1", is needed when a layer has several paths; startTime or startFrame (default the in point); duration (default 0.5 s) or durationFrames; easing; space comp (default) or local; matchStart auto or keep; hideTo (default true); replaceKeys. Returns: both paths, vertex counts, what was subdivided, the match, the keys. Notes: both paths must be closed or both open. Example: from {layer: {name: "Play"}}, to {layer: {name: "Pause"}}, durationFrames 18.

Pack: motion-rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `shapeMorph`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `from` | {layer: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, path?: string} | yes | The path that animates and gets the two keys. It must be a Bezier path. |
| `to` | {layer: {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer}, path?: string} | yes | The shape to morph into. The same layer with another path is allowed. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds from the composition start. Default: the from layer in point. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default 0.5. Key times snap to whole frames. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Default a landing (speed 0, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving, no overshoot). overshoot is refused for paths. |
| `space` | "comp" \| "local" | no | comp (default): the to shape lands where it appears on screen, through both layers' and groups' transforms. local: its raw path coordinates. |
| `matchStart` | "auto" \| "keep" | no | auto (default): pick the start vertex and direction that move the least. keep: use the paths as drawn. |
| `hideTo` | boolean | no | Hide the to layer (or switch off the to path on the same layer) after keying. Default true. |
