| `replaceKeys` | boolean | no | Replace keys already on the from path. Default false (refused when it has keys). |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "shape-morph",
  "arguments": {
    "from": {
      "layer": {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    },
    "to": {
      "layer": {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### sway-loop

Makes layers float in a seamless loop: Position moves on a sine (Sway X on the cosine, Sway Y on the sine, so both together trace an ellipse), with optional rotation and scale sway, repeating exactly every Period seconds, each layer shifted by a Phase Step so they never move in unison. Controls on a null: Period, Phase Step, Sway X, Sway Y, Sway Rotation, Sway Scale and Strength. Use when: hero cards, badges or icons hover gently, or a looping background needs calm motion. Do not use for: random motion (wiggle-rig) or a slow push on a whole scene (add-camera-push). Inputs: layers; period seconds (default 4); sway [x, y] px (default [0, 12]); rotation degrees; scale percent; phaseStep degrees per layer (default 60); strength percent; order; controlLayer, controlName. Returns: the control layer and controls, per layer its phase and expression states, and a note when the composition is not a whole number of periods. Notes: adds to the layers' keys and stacks on another rig's expression, such as a grid. Undoable in one step. Example: layers {names: ["Card A", "Card B", "Card C"]}, sway [0, 10], rotation 1.5, period 5, phaseStep 90.

Pack: motion-rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `swayLoop`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | yes | Layers: an array of layer refs, {selected: true}, {all: true}, {indices: [...]} or {names: [...]}. |
| `order` | "top-down" \| "bottom-up" \| "random" \| "as-given" | no | Which layer gets phase 0: top-down (default), bottom-up, random or as-given. |
| `period` | number | no | Seconds per cycle. Default 4. |
| `sway` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] amplitude in pixels. Default [0, 12]; [0, 0] leaves Position alone. |
| `rotation` | number | no | Rotation amplitude in degrees. Default none. |
| `scale` | number | no | Scale amplitude in percent points. Default none. |
| `phaseStep` | number | no | Degrees of phase between one layer and the next. Default 60. |
| `strength` | number | no | Percent of the amplitudes applied. Default 100; key it to fade the sway. |
| `controlLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Put the controls on this existing layer (for example a null shared with another rig) instead of a new null. Controls with the same names are reused and keep their values unless given. |
| `controlName` | string | no | Name of the new control null. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "sway-loop",
  "arguments": {
    "layers": [
      {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### wiggle-rig

Adds wiggle to the position, rotation, scale and/or opacity of one or many layers with every setting on controls: Frequency, an Amount per property, Seed, Loop Seconds and Strength. Each layer gets its own seed so no two move in step; Loop Seconds above 0 makes the wiggle repeat seamlessly; Strength keyed 0 to 100 ramps it in. Use when: floating UI, drifting particles, handheld life on a camera null, restless icons. Do not use for: a smooth periodic float (sway-loop) or a single fixed wiggle (apply-expression-preset). Inputs: layers; properties (default position); frequency per second (default 0.8); positionAmount px (default 10), rotationAmount degrees (2), scaleAmount percent (2), opacityAmount points (10); axes xy, x, y or xyz; loop seconds (default 0, off); seed; strength percent; rampIn {start, duration, easing} keys Strength up from 0; order; controlLayer, controlName. Returns: the control layer and controls, the ramp keys and per layer the expression states. Notes: adds to the layer's keys and stacks on another rig's expression, so grid cells wiggle around their slots. Undoable in one step. Example: layers {names: ["Card 1", "Card 2"]}, properties ["position", "rotation"], frequency 0.5, positionAmount 8, loop 4.

Pack: motion-rigs. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `wiggleRig`. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `layers` | object[] \| {selected?: boolean, all?: boolean, indices?: integer[], names?: string[]} | yes | Layers: an array of layer refs, {selected: true}, {all: true}, {indices: [...]} or {names: [...]}. |
| `order` | "top-down" \| "bottom-up" \| "random" \| "as-given" | no | Which layer gets seed offset 0: top-down (default), bottom-up, random or as-given. |
| `properties` | "position" \| "rotation" \| "scale" \| "opacity"[] | no | What wiggles. Default ["position"]. |
| `frequency` | number | no | Wiggles per second. Default 0.8, a calm drift. |
| `positionAmount` | number | no | Pixels. Default 10. |
| `rotationAmount` | number | no | Degrees. Default 2. |
| `scaleAmount` | number | no | Percent, the same on every axis. Default 2. |
| `opacityAmount` | number | no | Opacity points. Default 10. |
| `axes` | "xy" \| "x" \| "y" \| "xyz" | no | Position axes that wiggle. Default xy; xyz only moves Z on 3D layers. |
| `loop` | number | no | Seconds after which the wiggle repeats exactly. Default 0 (no loop). |
| `seed` | integer | no | Seed; change it for another pattern. Default 1. |
| `strength` | number | no | Percent of the amounts applied. Default 100. |
| `rampIn` | {startTime?: number, startFrame?: integer, duration?: number, durationFrames?: integer, easing?: "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| object \| object} | no | Key Strength from 0 to strength in two eased keys. |
| `controlLayer` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | Put the controls on this existing layer (for example a null shared with another rig) instead of a new null. Controls with the same names are reused and keep their values unless given. |
| `controlName` | string | no | Name of the new control null. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "wiggle-rig",
  "arguments": {
    "layers": [
      {
        "name": "My Layer"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## Charts and data

### create-bar-chart

Builds a bar chart as editable layers: one shape layer per bar (rounded data end, square base), value labels that count with the bars, category labels, soft gridlines on round steps with axis labels, and an axis line. Each value is a Slider Control named by its label on the null "<name> / Controls"; bars, labels and counters follow the sliders by expression. Use when: a film shows amounts by category, such as revenue per quarter. Do not use for: change over time (create-line-chart) or parts of a whole (create-pie-chart). Inputs: data [{label, value, color}] or dataFile (CSV or JSON); orientation; position (plot centre) and size (plot area); barWidth, gap, cornerRadius; colorMode, highlight; valueLabels end, inside or none; format; max, steps; gridlines, axis, axisLabels; startTime, duration per bar (default 0.8 s), staggerFrames, easing; animate; component. Returns: layers by role, controls with slider paths and expressions, the axis, layout, timing and expression errors. Notes: each bar grows on its own Grow slider (two keys). Values must be 0 or more. Undoable in one step. Example: data [{label: "Q1", value: 42}, {label: "Q2", value: 58}], format {prefix: "$", suffix: "K"}, highlight ["Q2"].

Pack: charts. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createBarChart`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `data` | {label: string, value: number, color?: string \| number[] \| object}[] | no | Items in order: [{label, value, color?}]. |
| `dataFile` | string | no | Absolute path (or ~/) to a .csv, .tsv or .json file, read by the server. CSV: a header row, then one row per item. JSON: [{label, value}], [[label, value]], {label: value} or {data: [...]}. Numbers like "1,234", "$12K" and "45%" are read. |
| `dataColumns` | {label?: string, value?: string, color?: string} | no | Which columns of dataFile to read. Default: a column named label, name, category, date or month (else the first text column), one named value, amount, count or total (else the first number column), and color. |
| `name` | string | no | Chart name, the start of every layer name ("<name> / Controls"). Default by type, numbered when taken. |
| `position` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] |
| `orientation` | "vertical" \| "horizontal" | no | vertical (default): columns grow up. horizontal: bars grow right, labels on the left. |
| `size` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] of the plot area (bars and gridlines) in pixels; labels sit outside it. Default half the comp width and 42 % of its height. |
| `barWidth` | number | no | Bar thickness in pixels. Default about 64 % of each category's space, capped so few bars stay slim. |
| `gap` | number | no | Pixels between bars. With barWidth too, the bars are packed and centred. |
| `cornerRadius` | number | no | Radius of the data end in pixels. Default 16 % of the bar width, at most 16 at 1080. |
| `roundBase` | boolean | no | Round the baseline end too. Default false (square on the axis). |
| `max` | number | no | Fixed top of the axis. Default: a round number above the largest value. |
| `steps` | integer | no | Gridline steps. Default 4 to 6, whichever fits the data tightest. |
| `gridlines` | boolean | no | Default true. |
| `axis` | boolean | no | Baseline axis line. Default true. |
| `axisLabels` | boolean | no | Numbers on the gridlines. Default: on with gridlines. |
| `categoryLabels` | boolean | no | Default true. |
| `valueLabels` | "end" \| "inside" \| "none" | no | end (default) past the bar end, inside the bar (shown once it fits), or none. |
| `format` | {decimals?: integer, prefix?: string, suffix?: string, thousands?: boolean \| string, decimalMark?: string, compact?: boolean} | no | How numbers are written. Counters use the same format while they count. |
| `colorMode` | "accent" \| "palette" \| "shades" | no | accent: every item in the accent colour. palette: a colour-blind-safe palette in fixed order. shades: the accent stepped toward the background. |
| `palette` | string \| number[] \| object[] | no | Your own palette for colorMode palette, in order. |
| `highlight` | string[] | no | Labels to show in the accent colour; every other item becomes a neutral grey (mutedColor). |
| `mutedColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | The neutral for items not highlighted. |
| `theme` | "auto" \| "light" \| "dark" | no | auto (default) reads a full-frame solid at the bottom of the comp, else the comp background colour, and uses dark text on light backgrounds and light text on dark ones. |
| `accent` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | The data colour. Default a calm blue (#2A78D6 on light, #3987E5 on dark). |
| `textColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Numbers. Default near-black or near-white from the theme. |
| `labelColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Category and axis labels, legends, captions. Default a grey from the theme. |
| `gridColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Gridlines. Default a soft grey from the theme. |
| `axisColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Axis line. Default a slightly stronger grey. |
| `font` | string | no | PostScript font name for labels. Default: the After Effects default font. |
| `numberFont` | string | no | PostScript font for numbers and counters. Default: font. |
| `fontSize` | number | no | Value labels and counters in pixels. Default scales with the comp height (28 at 1080). |
| `labelFontSize` | number | no | Category labels and legends in pixels. Default scales with the comp height (22 to 24 at 1080). |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds when the intro starts. Default 0. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds each bar takes to grow. Default 0.8. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing of the intro keys. Default: speed 0 at both keys, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving (a fast start and a long landing, no overshoot). |
| `animate` | boolean | no | false builds the finished chart with no keys. Default true. |
| `staggerFrames` | integer | no | Frames between one bar and the next. Default about 0.07 s. |
| `component` | boolean \| {name?: string, templateName?: string} | no | Also make the chart a component (create-component): its layers move into a comp of that name, cropped to the chart, and each data slider becomes an Essential Property labelled with the slider name, so every instance can show its own numbers. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-bar-chart",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### create-line-chart

Builds a line chart as editable layers: a smooth or straight line that draws on with Trim Paths (two eased keys), dots that pop on as the line reaches them, an optional area under the line (a soft gradient or a solid wash) that follows the line as it draws, value labels on the last or every point, category labels, and gridlines on round steps. The path is built by expression (createPath) from one Slider Control per point. Use when: a metric changes over time. Do not use for: comparing categories (create-bar-chart). Inputs: data or dataFile (at least 2 points); position, size; min (number or "auto"), max, steps; smooth; lineWidth; dots, dotSize; area gradient, solid or none; areaOpacity; pointLabels last, all or none; labelEvery; format; startTime, duration of the draw (default 1.2 s), easing; animate; component. Returns: layers by role, controls, the axis, when each point is reached, and expression errors. Notes: the smooth line never swings past its data (monotone curve). The gradient area uses a luma track matte (After Effects 23 or later). Undoable in one step. Example: dataFile "~/data/mrr.csv", format {prefix: "$", compact: true}, area "gradient".

Pack: charts. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createLineChart`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `data` | {label: string, value: number, color?: string \| number[] \| object}[] | no | Items in order: [{label, value, color?}]. |
| `dataFile` | string | no | Absolute path (or ~/) to a .csv, .tsv or .json file, read by the server. CSV: a header row, then one row per item. JSON: [{label, value}], [[label, value]], {label: value} or {data: [...]}. Numbers like "1,234", "$12K" and "45%" are read. |
| `dataColumns` | {label?: string, value?: string, color?: string} | no | Which columns of dataFile to read. Default: a column named label, name, category, date or month (else the first text column), one named value, amount, count or total (else the first number column), and color. |
| `name` | string | no | Chart name, the start of every layer name ("<name> / Controls"). Default by type, numbered when taken. |
| `position` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] |
| `size` | [number, number] | no | [width, height] of the plot area in pixels; the first and last points sit on its edges. Default 60 % of the comp width and 42 % of its height. |
| `min` | number \| "auto" | no | Bottom of the axis: a number, or "auto" to fit the data. Default 0 (lower when data goes below 0). |
| `max` | number | no | Top of the axis. Default: a round number above the largest value. |
| `steps` | integer | no | Gridline steps. Default 4 to 6. |
| `smooth` | boolean | no | Curved through the points (default) or straight segments. |
| `lineWidth` | number | no | Pixels. Default 4 at 1080. |
| `dots` | boolean | no | A dot on every point, popping on as the line passes. Default true up to 24 points, false for denser lines. |
| `dotSize` | number | no | Dot diameter in pixels. Default 3 times the line width. |
| `area` | "gradient" \| "solid" \| "none" | no | Fill under the line. Default gradient (fading toward the bottom). |
| `areaOpacity` | number | no | Area opacity in percent at its strongest. Default 32 for gradient, 14 for solid. |
| `pointLabels` | "last" \| "all" \| "none" | no | Value labels: on the last point (default, to its right), above every point, or none. |
| `labelEvery` | integer | no | Show every Nth category label. Default: enough for about 12 labels. |
| `gridlines` | boolean | no | Default true. |
| `axis` | boolean | no | Axis line at 0 (or the bottom). Default true. |
| `axisLabels` | boolean | no | Default: on with gridlines. |
| `categoryLabels` | boolean | no | Default true. |
| `format` | {decimals?: integer, prefix?: string, suffix?: string, thousands?: boolean \| string, decimalMark?: string, compact?: boolean} | no | How numbers are written. Counters use the same format while they count. |
| `theme` | "auto" \| "light" \| "dark" | no | auto (default) reads a full-frame solid at the bottom of the comp, else the comp background colour, and uses dark text on light backgrounds and light text on dark ones. |
| `accent` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | The data colour. Default a calm blue (#2A78D6 on light, #3987E5 on dark). |
| `textColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Numbers. Default near-black or near-white from the theme. |
| `labelColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Category and axis labels, legends, captions. Default a grey from the theme. |
| `gridColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Gridlines. Default a soft grey from the theme. |
| `axisColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Axis line. Default a slightly stronger grey. |
| `font` | string | no | PostScript font name for labels. Default: the After Effects default font. |
| `numberFont` | string | no | PostScript font for numbers and counters. Default: font. |
| `fontSize` | number | no | Value labels and counters in pixels. Default scales with the comp height (28 at 1080). |
| `labelFontSize` | number | no | Category labels and legends in pixels. Default scales with the comp height (22 to 24 at 1080). |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds when the intro starts. Default 0. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds the line takes to draw. Default 1.2. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing of the intro keys. Default: speed 0 at both keys, influence 40 leaving and 75 arriving, an even draw that settles. |
| `animate` | boolean | no | false builds the finished chart with no keys. Default true. |
| `staggerFrames` | integer | no | Frames between category labels fading in. Default about 0.07 s. |
| `component` | boolean \| {name?: string, templateName?: string} | no | Also make the chart a component (create-component): its layers move into a comp of that name, cropped to the chart, and each data slider becomes an Essential Property labelled with the slider name, so every instance can show its own numbers. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-line-chart",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### create-pie-chart

Builds a donut or pie chart as editable layers: one stroked circle per slice trimmed to its share (Trim Paths Start and End by expression), a gap between slices, percentage or value labels at the slice centres that count as the slices fill, a legend, and an optional total in the donut hole. Slices fill clockwise one after another from the start angle. Use when: parts of a whole, such as plan mix or traffic sources. Do not use for: more than about 6 parts or comparing sizes closely (create-bar-chart). Inputs: data or dataFile; position (centre); radius; innerRadius (default 62 % of radius, 0 for a pie); startAngle (degrees clockwise from 12 o'clock); gap in pixels; colorMode (default shades), palette, highlight; labels percent, value, label, label-percent or none; labelPosition auto, inside or outside; minLabelShare; legend; center {total, label}; format; startTime, duration per slice (default 0.8 s), staggerFrames; animate; component. Returns: layers by role, controls, each share in percent with its colour, layout, timing and expression errors. Notes: labels on slices under minLabelShare (default 4 %) are hidden. Undoable in one step. Example: data [{label: "Pro", value: 62}, {label: "Team", value: 28}, {label: "Free", value: 10}], center {label: "Customers"}.

Pack: charts. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createPieChart`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `data` | {label: string, value: number, color?: string \| number[] \| object}[] | no | Items in order: [{label, value, color?}]. |
| `dataFile` | string | no | Absolute path (or ~/) to a .csv, .tsv or .json file, read by the server. CSV: a header row, then one row per item. JSON: [{label, value}], [[label, value]], {label: value} or {data: [...]}. Numbers like "1,234", "$12K" and "45%" are read. |
| `dataColumns` | {label?: string, value?: string, color?: string} | no | Which columns of dataFile to read. Default: a column named label, name, category, date or month (else the first text column), one named value, amount, count or total (else the first number column), and color. |
| `name` | string | no | Chart name, the start of every layer name ("<name> / Controls"). Default by type, numbered when taken. |
| `position` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] |
| `radius` | number | no | Outer radius in pixels. Default 30 % of the smaller comp side. |
| `innerRadius` | number | no | Hole radius in pixels. Default 62 % of radius; 0 draws a pie. |
| `startAngle` | number | no | Where the first slice starts, degrees clockwise from 12 o'clock. Default 0. |
| `gap` | number | no | Space between slices in pixels. Default 4 for a donut, 3 for a pie at 1080. |
| `labels` | "percent" \| "value" \| "label" \| "label-percent" \| "none" | no | What each slice shows. Default percent. |
| `labelPosition` | "auto" \| "inside" \| "outside" | no | auto (default): inside when the ring is thick enough, else outside. |
| `minLabelShare` | number | no | Slices smaller than this percent get no label. Default 4. |
| `legend` | boolean | no | Swatch and label rows to the right. Default true. |
| `center` | boolean \| {total?: boolean, label?: string, format?: object} | no | Donut only: the total (and a caption) in the hole. |
| `format` | {decimals?: integer, prefix?: string, suffix?: string, thousands?: boolean \| string, decimalMark?: string, compact?: boolean} | no | Number format. Percent labels default to whole numbers with "%". |
| `colorMode` | "accent" \| "palette" \| "shades" | no | accent: every item in the accent colour. palette: a colour-blind-safe palette in fixed order. shades: the accent stepped toward the background. |
| `palette` | string \| number[] \| object[] | no | Your own palette for colorMode palette, in order. |
| `highlight` | string[] | no | Labels to show in the accent colour; every other item becomes a neutral grey (mutedColor). |
| `mutedColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | The neutral for items not highlighted. |
| `theme` | "auto" \| "light" \| "dark" | no | auto (default) reads a full-frame solid at the bottom of the comp, else the comp background colour, and uses dark text on light backgrounds and light text on dark ones. |
| `accent` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | The data colour. Default a calm blue (#2A78D6 on light, #3987E5 on dark). |
| `textColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Numbers. Default near-black or near-white from the theme. |
| `labelColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Category and axis labels, legends, captions. Default a grey from the theme. |
| `gridColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Gridlines. Default a soft grey from the theme. |
| `axisColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Axis line. Default a slightly stronger grey. |
| `font` | string | no | PostScript font name for labels. Default: the After Effects default font. |
| `numberFont` | string | no | PostScript font for numbers and counters. Default: font. |
| `fontSize` | number | no | Value labels and counters in pixels. Default scales with the comp height (28 at 1080). |
| `labelFontSize` | number | no | Category labels and legends in pixels. Default scales with the comp height (22 to 24 at 1080). |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds when the intro starts. Default 0. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds each slice takes to fill. Default 0.8. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing of the intro keys. Default: speed 0 at both keys, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving (a fast start and a long landing, no overshoot). |
| `animate` | boolean | no | false builds the finished chart with no keys. Default true. |
| `staggerFrames` | integer | no | Frames between one slice and the next. Default about 0.12 s. |
| `component` | boolean \| {name?: string, templateName?: string} | no | Also make the chart a component (create-component): its layers move into a comp of that name, cropped to the chart, and each data slider becomes an Essential Property labelled with the slider name, so every instance can show its own numbers. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-pie-chart",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### create-progress

Builds a progress bar or ring that fills to a value: a pill-shaped track with a fill that grows from the left, or a ring whose fill draws clockwise from 12 o'clock with round caps, plus a percentage (or value) that counts with the fill and an optional caption. Value and Max are Slider Controls on the control null and a Grow slider (two eased keys) drives the fill. Use when: completion, usage against a limit, a goal or a score, such as "Onboarding 72 %". Do not use for: several categories (create-bar-chart) or a headline number with a change (create-stat). Inputs: value; max (default 100); style bar or ring; position (centre); size [width, height] for a bar; radius and thickness for a ring; roundCaps; display percent, value or none; format; caption; accent, trackColor (default a light step of the accent); startTime, duration (default 1 s), easing; animate; component. Returns: the layers by role, the controls, the percent shown, layout and timing. Notes: the fill stops at 0 and 100 %. Undoable in one step. Example: value 72, style "ring", caption "Profile complete".

Pack: charts. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createProgress`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `value` | number | yes | How far along, in the units of max. |
| `max` | number | no | The full amount. Default 100. |
| `style` | "bar" \| "ring" | no | Default bar. |
| `name` | string | no | Chart name, the start of every layer name ("<name> / Controls"). Default by type, numbered when taken. |
| `position` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] |
| `size` | [number, number] | no | Bar only: [width, height] in pixels. Default 40 % of the comp width by 16. |
| `radius` | number | no | Ring only: radius to the middle of the stroke in pixels. Default 16 % of the smaller comp side. |
| `thickness` | number | no | Ring only: stroke width in pixels. Default 14 % of the radius. |
| `roundCaps` | boolean | no | Ring only: round ends on the fill. Default true. |
| `display` | "percent" \| "value" \| "none" | no | The counter: a percentage (default), the value itself, or nothing. |
| `format` | {decimals?: integer, prefix?: string, suffix?: string, thousands?: boolean \| string, decimalMark?: string, compact?: boolean} | no | How numbers are written. Counters use the same format while they count. |
| `caption` | string | no | Text beside the bar (left above it) or under the ring's number. |
| `trackColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | The unfilled track. Default a light step of the accent. |
| `fontSize` | number | no | The counter in pixels. |
| `labelFontSize` | number | no | The caption in pixels. |
| `theme` | "auto" \| "light" \| "dark" | no | auto (default) reads a full-frame solid at the bottom of the comp, else the comp background colour, and uses dark text on light backgrounds and light text on dark ones. |
| `accent` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | The data colour. Default a calm blue (#2A78D6 on light, #3987E5 on dark). |
| `textColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Numbers. Default near-black or near-white from the theme. |
| `labelColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Category and axis labels, legends, captions. Default a grey from the theme. |
| `gridColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Gridlines. Default a soft grey from the theme. |
| `axisColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Axis line. Default a slightly stronger grey. |
| `font` | string | no | PostScript font name for labels. Default: the After Effects default font. |
| `numberFont` | string | no | PostScript font for numbers and counters. Default: font. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds when the intro starts. Default 0. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds the fill takes. Default 1. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing of the intro keys. Default: speed 0 at both keys, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving (a fast start and a long landing, no overshoot). |
| `animate` | boolean | no | false builds the finished chart with no keys. Default true. |
| `component` | boolean \| {name?: string, templateName?: string} | no | Also make the chart a component (create-component): its layers move into a comp of that name, cropped to the chart, and each data slider becomes an Essential Property labelled with the slider name, so every instance can show its own numbers. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-progress",
  "arguments": {
    "value": 0.5
  }
}
```

### create-stat

Builds a KPI block: a big number counting up to a value in a number format, a label above it, an optional change row (an up or down arrow and the change, green when good and red when bad, with a caption such as "vs last month") and an optional sparkline that draws while the number counts. Value, From, Delta and the sparkline points are Slider Controls on the control null; a Count slider (two eased keys) drives the count. Use when: a film calls out one metric, such as revenue. Do not use for: several values (create-bar-chart) or a percentage bar or ring (create-progress). Inputs: value; label; from (default 0); format; align left or center; position (top-left, or top-centre); fontSize, labelFontSize; delta {value, format (default "%"), goodWhen up or down, caption}; sparkline {data, width, height, color, dot, smooth}; upColor, downColor; startTime, duration (default 1.2 s), easing; animate; component. Returns: the layers by role, the controls, the final text, layout and timing. Notes: arrow, colour and text follow the Delta slider, so a later change can flip them. Undoable in one step. Example: value 48250, label "Monthly revenue", format {prefix: "$"}, delta {value: 12.4, caption: "vs last month"}.

Pack: charts. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `createStat`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | Composition: {id}, {name} (exact, then any case) or {active: true}. Default: the active composition. |
| `value` | number | yes | The number the counter lands on. |
| `label` | string | no | Caption above the number. |
| `from` | number | no | Where the count starts. Default 0. |
| `format` | {decimals?: integer, prefix?: string, suffix?: string, thousands?: boolean \| string, decimalMark?: string, compact?: boolean} | no | How numbers are written. Counters use the same format while they count. |
| `align` | "left" \| "center" | no | Default left. |
| `name` | string | no | Chart name, the start of every layer name ("<name> / Controls"). Default by type, numbered when taken. |
| `position` | [number, number] | no | [x, y] |
| `fontSize` | number | no | The number in pixels. Default 112 at 1080. |
| `labelFontSize` | number | no | Label, change and caption in pixels. Default 28 at 1080. |
| `delta` | {value: number, format?: {decimals?: integer, prefix?: string, suffix?: string, thousands?: boolean \| string, decimalMark?: string, compact?: boolean}, goodWhen?: "up" \| "down", caption?: string} | no |  |
| `sparkline` | {data: number[], width?: number, height?: number, color?: string \| number[] \| object, dot?: boolean, smooth?: boolean} | no |  |
| `upColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour of a good change. Default green. |
| `downColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Colour of a bad change. Default red. |
| `theme` | "auto" \| "light" \| "dark" | no | auto (default) reads a full-frame solid at the bottom of the comp, else the comp background colour, and uses dark text on light backgrounds and light text on dark ones. |
| `accent` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | The data colour. Default a calm blue (#2A78D6 on light, #3987E5 on dark). |
| `textColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Numbers. Default near-black or near-white from the theme. |
| `labelColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Category and axis labels, legends, captions. Default a grey from the theme. |
| `gridColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Gridlines. Default a soft grey from the theme. |
| `axisColor` | string \| number[] \| {r: number, g: number, b: number, a?: number} | no | Axis line. Default a slightly stronger grey. |
| `font` | string | no | PostScript font name for labels. Default: the After Effects default font. |
| `numberFont` | string | no | PostScript font for numbers and counters. Default: font. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds when the intro starts. Default 0. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds the number counts. Default 1.2. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Easing of the intro keys. Default: speed 0 at both keys, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving (a fast start and a long landing, no overshoot). |
| `animate` | boolean | no | false builds the finished chart with no keys. Default true. |
| `component` | boolean \| {name?: string, templateName?: string} | no | Also make the chart a component (create-component): its layers move into a comp of that name, cropped to the chart, and each data slider becomes an Essential Property labelled with the slider name, so every instance can show its own numbers. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "create-stat",
  "arguments": {
    "value": 0.5
  }
}
```

### get-chart-data

Reads the charts made by the chart tools in a composition: for each, its name and type, the control null, every value by label as it stands at a time (with the number of keys on its slider), the axis range and steps, the number format, and the chart's layers. With instance it reads a component instance's own values. Use when: before update-chart-data, to see the labels and current numbers; or to check what a chart shows at a moment. Do not use for: reading any other layer (get-layer-details). Inputs: comp; chart (name), controls or instance to read one chart (default every chart in the comp); time or frame (default the current time). Returns: composition, time, count and charts[] with values [{label, effect, value, keys, source}], axis {min, max, steps, auto} and layers. Notes: read-only. Values are the sliders' own (keyed) values times the chart's scale; a slider that was deleted reads null. Example: chart "Revenue", time 3.

Pack: charts. Read-only. Bridge command: `getChartData`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | The composition holding the chart (or the component instance). Default: the active composition. |
| `chart` | string | no | Chart name (the part before " / Controls"). Optional when the comp holds one chart. |
| `controls` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | The chart's control null, instead of chart. |
| `instance` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | A component instance of a chart made with component: its Essential Properties are changed instead of the chart inside. |
| `time` | number | no | Time in seconds from the composition start. |
| `frame` | integer | no | Frame number from the composition start. Wins over time when both are given. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "get-chart-data",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### update-chart-data

Changes a chart's values by label, made by the chart tools, and can animate the change: each changed slider gets two eased keys from the value it shows at the start to the new one, so bars, lines, slices, labels and counters move together. An automatic axis is rescaled to round numbers for the new data in the same move. Use when: a scene compares two periods or a number updates on screen. Do not use for: adding or removing categories (build the chart again) or styling. Inputs: chart (name), controls (the control null) or instance (a component instance, whose Essential Properties change); values {label: number}, data or dataFile; startTime or startFrame (default the current time), duration (default 0.8 s), easing; animate (default true when a time or duration is given); rescale (default true). Stats take Value, From, Delta and Spark N; progress takes Value and Max. Returns: each change with from, to and keys, the axis before and after, and notes. Notes: unknown labels fail before anything changes. Without animation a keyed slider gets the value on its last key. Undoable in one step. Example: chart "Revenue", values {"Q3": 71, "Q4": 88}, startTime 4, duration 0.8.

Pack: charts. Changes the project (one undo step). Bridge command: `updateChartData`.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp` | {id?: integer, name?: string, active?: boolean} | no | The composition holding the chart (or the component instance). Default: the active composition. |
| `chart` | string | no | Chart name (the part before " / Controls"). Optional when the comp holds one chart. |
| `controls` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | The chart's control null, instead of chart. |
| `instance` | {index?: integer, name?: string, id?: integer} | no | A component instance of a chart made with component: its Essential Properties are changed instead of the chart inside. |
| `values` | object of number | no | New values by label, for example {"Q3": 71}. |
| `data` | {label: string, value: number}[] | no | New values as [{label, value}]. |
| `dataFile` | string | no | Absolute path (or ~/) to a .csv, .tsv or .json file, read by the server. CSV: a header row, then one row per item. JSON: [{label, value}], [[label, value]], {label: value} or {data: [...]}. Numbers like "1,234", "$12K" and "45%" are read. |
| `dataColumns` | {label?: string, value?: string, color?: string} | no | Which columns of dataFile to read. Default: a column named label, name, category, date or month (else the first text column), one named value, amount, count or total (else the first number column), and color. |
| `animate` | boolean | no | Animate the change. Default true when startTime, startFrame, duration, durationFrames or easing is given, else false. |
| `startTime` | number | no | Seconds when the change starts. Default the current time. |
| `startFrame` | integer | no | Frames; wins over startTime. |
| `duration` | number | no | Seconds. Default 0.8. |
| `durationFrames` | integer | no | Frames; wins over duration. |
| `easing` | "linear" \| "ease" \| "ease-in" \| "ease-out" \| "ease-in-out" \| "smooth" \| "snappy" \| "overshoot" \| "hold" \| string \| {type: "custom", inSpeed?: number, inInfluence?: number, outSpeed?: number, outInfluence?: number} \| {type: "bezier", x1: number, y1: number, x2: number, y2: number} | no | Default: speed 0, influence 25 leaving and 100 arriving. |
| `rescale` | boolean | no | Move an automatic axis to fit the new data. Default true. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "update-chart-data",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

## Help

### call-tool

Runs any tool of this server by name with its arguments, whether its pack is on or off, and returns exactly what that tool returns. Use when: a tool from a pack is not in your tool list (the client did not reload after tool-packs). Do not use for: tools you can call directly. Returns: that tool's own result. Notes: list-tools with schema true shows a tool's inputs. Example: tool "create-bar-chart", args {data: [{label: "Q1", value: 42}]}.

Core tool, always on. Changes the project (one undo step). Runs on the server; not available inside batch. Uses the render timeout.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tool` | string | yes | Tool name, for example "create-bar-chart". |
| `args` | object of any | no | Arguments for that tool. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "call-tool",
  "arguments": {
    "tool": "value"
  }
}
```

### get-help

Returns the agent guide for this server: how the bridge works, how addressing works, the standard workflow, units, animation craft rules, text and effect advice, recovery steps and what not to do. The text is read from docs/AGENT-GUIDE.md on disk each time, so it is always current. Use when: starting a session, before your first build, or when something behaves unexpectedly. Do not use for: listing tools (use list-tools) or looking up one tool's arguments (the schema has them). Inputs: topic (optional), a section name or slug such as "addressing", "workflow", "animation-craft", "recovery"; omit for the whole guide; "toc" for the list of sections. Returns: markdown text. With an unknown topic, the list of available topics. Notes: if a style file is configured (AE_MCP_STYLE_FILE), its summary is appended to the "reference-and-style" section. Example: get-help with topic "animation-craft" before animating a lower third.

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `topic` | string | no | Section name or slug. Omit for everything, or "toc" for the table of contents. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "get-help",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### list-tools

Lists every tool this server offers, on or off, grouped by pack, with a one-line summary of each. Use when: you are unsure which tool does something, before writing a batch, or to read the inputs of a tool from a pack that is off. Returns: count and a list of {name, group, active, mutating, summary}, plus inputSchema per tool with schema true. Notes: read-only. tool-packs switches packs on; call-tool runs a tool that is off. Example: search "keyframe"; or names ["create-bar-chart"], schema true.

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `group` | string | no | Pack (group) name such as "layers" or "charts". |
| `search` | string | no | Case-insensitive text to match in the name or description. |
| `names` | string[] | no | Exact tool names. |
| `schema` | boolean | no | Add each tool's input schema. Default false; at most 20 tools. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "list-tools",
  "arguments": {}
}
```

### tool-packs

Lists the tool packs and switches them on or off. The core tools are always on; the rest come in packs (charts, ui-demo, scenes, transitions, motion-rigs, components, camera-3d, masks, styles and more). Use when: a job needs tools outside the core, for example charts or a cursor journey. Call it with no arguments to see the packs. Returns: the packs with their tools and whether each is on, and how many tools are active. Notes: switching sends a tool-list change to the client. If the new tools do not show up, run them with call-tool (list-tools with schema true shows their inputs).

Core tool, always on. Read-only. Runs on the server; not available inside batch.

| Input | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `enable` | string[] | no | Packs to switch on, or ["all"]. |
| `disable` | string[] | no | Packs to switch off, or ["all"]. Core tools stay on. |

Example call:

```json
{
  "tool": "tool-packs",
  "arguments": {}
}
```
